v3.4.135 - 2026-10-02 - Grand Central Terminal restored (transit exemption list).

Joe: "GCT appears to be missing." Expected consequence of the v3.4.134 rule: Grand Central Terminal (Overture 76fa88dd-a26d-4716-9261-ffd8ba2a9a0c, class train_station, 45.8 m) covers ~411 m of Park Ave viaduct centreline. New TRANSIT_KEEP_IDS set; ids in it are never dropped. Contains only Grand Central for now. `audit_deploy.js`: 2 checks (exemption, version).

v3.4.134 - 2026-10-02 - Station/transport footprints that cover streets are no longer extruded.

Joe, Midtown (two screenshots): masses of form geometry sit where the streets should be (Herald Square, Park Ave). Cause is the Overture data, not the app or terrain: footprints with class train_station / subtype transportation are drawn at the surface over the streets above the stations (e.g. the 34 St-Herald Sq station is a 16,000 m2 footprint, 56 m tall, over Broadway and 6th Ave). Joe chose: drop station/transportation footprints that cover more than ~50 m of street.
- Rule (dropTransitOverStreets(), runs in loadData right after setProjectionOrigin() so the district's coordinate frame does not move): for every footprint with class train_station or subtype transportation, measure how much street centreline (streets.json) lies inside it (2 m sampling, even-odd point-in-polygon); more than TRANSIT_STREET_DROP_M = 50 m -> omitted. A complex that has parts (it is drawn from its parts) is judged part by part; a footprint without parts is judged whole.
- Exception I added: class bridge_structure is exempt. A bridge deck is supposed to cover its own roadway; without the exemption the Manhattan, Brooklyn and Williamsburg bridge structures would vanish.
- Result per district (footprints that have a height and were actually drawn): district-1 parking 5e9bd576 (34 m); district-2 73c89d3e (12 m); district-3 332e5abf (8 m); district-4 a7afa712 (6 m) and f78c98b4 (Port Authority, 38.6 m); district-5 a35c1295 (Herald Sq, 56 m), 76fa88dd (Grand Central Terminal, 46 m) plus two Park Ave station-complex parts (150 m and 65 m); district-11 e0bcb7fe (36 m); district-12 3ec0abcd (23 m). Footprints with no height were never drawn. The list for the loaded district is in the console, a toast, and __NS.droppedTransit. ?transit=keep in the URL turns the rule off for comparison.
- Cost: Grand Central Terminal, Port Authority and two tall Park Ave parts are real buildings that this rule removes. That is Joe's rule applied as written; an exemption list is the fix if he wants any back.

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload. Needs: Midtown Home view at Herald Square and Park Ave; ?transit=keep to compare.

`audit_deploy.js`: 5 new checks.

v3.4.133 - 2026-10-02 - Terrain toggle: buildings, ground, streets, negative space and sections follow the real ground.

Joe: "terrain ... negative space, sections and ground follow terrain ... able to be toggled", elevation fetched live. New **Terrain** button in Display (off by default; off = the exact flat pipeline of v3.4.132). Turning it on fetches public Terrarium tiles (z14, ~7 m/px) for the site plus 60 m, samples them on a 16+ m grid in the district's own rotated frame (exact inverse of project()), and sets z=0 to a datum 1 m below the lowest ground in that area. Changing the toggle rebuilds the district and recomputes if it had been computed.
- Tiles are decoded with our own PNG inflate (decodePngRGB8): Terrarium packs metres into R/G/B, and decoding through <canvas>/getImageData drifted R by 256 m on a large share of pixels on Joe's machine (fake terrain up to 2,000 m). Negative values (water/encoding noise) clamp to sea level.
- Buildings: each building/part stands on the LOWEST terrain under its footprint; roof = that base + Overture height; the solid starts 0.5 m below the base so it never leaves a hairline gap downhill. Absolute roof heights feed the cap height, the skyline/sky-discard profiles and the section bbox cache.
- Compute: mold box minus an "earth" solid (closed heightfield) minus the unioned buildings, so the negative space's floor is the terrain.
- Display: groundMesh keeps its identity (so every existing ground rule still applies) but swaps in the terrain surface; it stays visible with Manhattan context on. Streets are densified to 15 m and draped; labels lifted by the ground height.
- Sections: gaps in the section profile sit at the ground height of the cut (highest terrain across the slab) instead of 0, so the red skyline follows the terrain; the terrain surface is clipped to the cut slab in a locked elevation (terrainGroundClipPlanes), and re-clipped when the slab moves.
- Limits: terrain compute is slower than flat (district-1: ~2x triangles; wall time was ~25-40 s in a hidden tab). Resolution ~16 m grid / ~7 m source: good for slope and gross form, not curbs. Bridge/tunnel decks and building-part overhangs are not modeled. No earth poche under the ground line in sections yet (the red line is the ground).
- **Grid-rotation fix (Joe: "fix grid issue"):** GRID_COS/GRID_SIN were `const`, computed once at page load from the 28.96deg default and never updated by setGridRotation(), so every district was projected at 28.96deg while the compass and its own measured angle used another (about 4.8deg off in Greenwich Village / Lower East Side, 1.6deg in Inwood). They are now `let`, updated in setGridRotation(). Every district except those already near 28.96deg is therefore rotated slightly differently than before: its street grid now lies along the X/Y axes as the stored angles intended. Measured live (length-weighted wall bearing mod 90): Greenwich Village +3.89deg -> -0.89deg, a shift of exactly 4.78deg = 28.96 - 24.18.

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload. Needs: Terrain on (district-1, Midtown, Inwood), Home + orbit, N/S/E/W, a drawn box, then smoke with terrain on and off.

`audit_deploy.js`: 9 new checks; 4 older checks that pinned changed lines now allow the terrain-aware forms.

v3.4.132 - 2026-10-02 - Fill light in the perspective/Home view so south- and west-facing walls are no longer black.

Joe: "the south faces of building are always dark." The perspective sun sits at (400, 300, 700); its light vector has a negative dot product with every south-facing wall (N.L -0.34) and west-facing wall (-0.46), so those walls got only the HemisphereLight and read near-black. Added `fillLight`, a DirectionalLight at (-400, -300, 150), intensity 0.5, from the sun's mirrored horizontal direction and low: south walls get N.L about +0.57, west walls about +0.77, roofs only +0.29, so the sun still sets the main light/shade pattern. It is swapped with the sun exactly like `sun` is (hidden in setOrthogonalView(), shown in resetSharedViewState()); the N/S/E/W/Plan views are already front-lit by orthoLight and are unchanged. The poche quads are unlit since v3.4.130 and are unaffected.

Previewed live on district-1 by adding the same light to the scene at intensity 0 vs 0.5 before shipping: at 0.5 south/west faces read mid-gray with form. Intensity 0.5 is a judgment call, one constant (`new THREE.DirectionalLight(0xffffff, 0.5)`); raise toward 0.8 for flatter/brighter, lower toward 0.3 for more contrast.

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload. Check Home on a couple of districts, orbit all the way around, and confirm N/S/E/W elevations look the same as on 131.

`audit_deploy.js`: 3 new checks (the v3.4.131 version-only check is replaced by the 3.4.132 one).

v3.4.131 - 2026-10-02 - Manhattan context layer and streets now hide when N/S/E/W is entered from the perspective/Home view.

Joe: with Manhattan context ON, clicking N (or S/E/W) from the perspective view left the gray island slab and the "MANHATTAN" label in the elevation. Console readout in that state: sectionModeAxis 'y', activeCamera Orthographic, boroughsGroup.visible true. Root cause is call ordering in setOrthogonalView(): applySectionMode() runs first and ends with updateContextLayerVisibility() and updateStreetLabelVisibility(); both ask inLockedElevation() (sectionModeAxis !== null && activeCamera === orthoCamera), but activeCamera is only switched to orthoCamera later in the function, so both saw the perspective camera and answered "not locked". From Plan the camera is already the ortho one, so every path the smoke and soak tests used (they always go through Plan first) worked. Fix: after `activeCamera = orthoCamera`, call updateContextLayerVisibility() and updateStreetLabelVisibility() again. Major streets had the same ordering and are covered by the same two lines; that second symptom was found in the code, not seen on screen.

smoke_test.js: new Phase 10 starts every N/S/E/W from Home (perspective) and checks the ortho camera is active and the Manhattan layer and streets are hidden, in both context passes.

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload. Needs: swap in, hard-refresh (footer v3.4.131), Manhattan context ON, Home, then N/S/E/W with and without a drawn box -- no slab, no MANHATTAN label, tan ground plate as the base.

`audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks (ordering, version).

v3.4.130 - 2026-10-01 - Poché is now unlit, so lighting can never wash it out.

Joe saw the poché render pale gray-blue instead of red in some N/S/E/W elevations (seen on v3.4.127 and v3.4.128; red again on v3.4.129 across Midtown and Inwood). The exact trigger was never found: the console readout showed the Y poché quad visible with colour #b23a2e at 0.4 opacity throughout; no other translucent mesh was drawn; the v3.4.127 ground-line segment was ruled out (still white on v3.4.128 without it); but hiding the Y cap group removed the pale fill, and so did hiding either light -- so its on-screen colour depended on the lighting, not only on the material. A lit surface that is meant to be a flat mass is the fragile part, so makeCapQuad() now builds the poché from MeshBasicMaterial (unlit), like the cutaway handles since v3.4.98. Clipping planes, the stencil ops, polygonOffset, opacity and the sky-discard shader patch (which hooks #include <common>/<color_fragment>/<begin_vertex>, present in the basic shader too) are all unchanged. In the good state the result is visually identical (the lit colour there came out at ~1.0 x the albedo).

This removes the lighting-dependent failure mode; it does not prove that was the only cause. If a pale poché is ever seen again on v3.4.130 or later, run the pixel-readout diagnostic from the v3.4.129 session notes while it is on screen.

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload.

v3.4.129 - 2026-10-01 - Section base (ground) with Manhattan context on; the ground-line segment stays out.

The original complaint -- the base of an elevation reading as cut off -- turns out to have a different cause: zoomToBoroughContext() hides the tan ground plate (groundMesh) when Manhattan context is switched on, and nothing restored it when a locked elevation then hid the context layer itself (v3.4.114). So with context ON, N/S/E/W had no ground plate at all; with context OFF the plate shows edge-on as the base. updateContextLayerVisibility() now sets groundMesh.visible = !boroughsGroup.visible, so the ground plate is back whenever the context layer is not showing (including every locked elevation) and still hidden in Plan/Perspective with context on. No new geometry.

v3.4.127 - 2026-10-01 - Joe, on a South elevation of Inwood/Washington Heights: the base of the section read as cut off.

A section should read like an architectural section: solid cut fill, ONE continuous ground line at grade, streets as open gaps at grade, no plan-view street layers (Major streets stay hidden in elevations, per v3.4.110). The first two were true except the ground line: the red skyline only touched z=0 inside the gaps between buildings. buildSkylineProfileLine() now appends a single segment along the cut plane at z=0 from the plot range's lo to hi (same red, same renderOrder), so the base is continuous. Standing rule recorded: apply this section convention by default, don't ask again.

v3.4.126 - 2026-10-01 - Joe: carry-forward list (ViewCube TOP, missing N/S fill, panel spacing, soak test), plus Display/High detail/Home requests.

**ViewCube TOP is now north-up.** v3.4.124's camera-snap epsilon was (0, +0.0001, 1), which puts the camera on the +Y side; with camera.up = +Z that yields screen-up = -Y, i.e. a SOUTH-up top view (checked numerically with lookAt: screen-up (0,-1,0)). v3.4.125's 180deg label flip only rotated the cube's texture, not the real view. Epsilon moved to the -Y side, (0, -0.0001, 1) -> screen-up (0,+1,0); TOP's rotDeg back to 0. Confirmed on Joe's localhost: Battery Park at the bottom, label upright.

**Box-scoped caps always fill.** refreshCapFillCoverage() fell back to outline-only whenever a cap covered >50% of the site. That rule is for full-site caps in free perspective; for a drawn box the elevation camera frames the box, and a wide E-W box easily exceeds half the (narrow) site X span, flipping N/S elevations to outline-only with no maroon fill. E/W compare against the much longer Y span, so they almost never tripped it. Now `!activeSectionBox && ratio > threshold`; applyPlanBoxDirection() also re-runs refreshCapFillCoverage() after setOrthogonalView(). Side effect: box-cropped E/W with large boxes now always fill. Not reproduced by hand before the fix -- the diagnosis is from the code path and the smoke test's fill checks.

**Buildings ON in Home and Manhattan context.** An N/S/E/W elevation force-turns Buildings off (v3.4.48), and that carried into Home. resetSharedViewState() now sets showBuildings = true; zoomToBoroughContext() calls refreshViewToggles() so the button and solidGroup sync.

**Display accordion opens by default; High detail moved into it.** v3.4.39 had put High detail in Export, but it only raises on-screen render resolution and Export has no image output. Now at the bottom of Display, with a clearer tooltip. Off by default.

**ViewCube panel spacing.** bottom offset 130px -> 142px (was ~8px above the compass panel, now ~20px).

**smoke_test.js:** Phase 9 (Buildings + Negative space both on, a box) now re-enables both toggles after each box-direction click (entering an elevation turns Buildings off by design), and its cut-face-cap check requires only the ACTIVE axis's cap material to be box-scoped (only that group is visible in a locked elevation). runSmokeBothSides(): 290/290 on Midtown-era localhost (137 context OFF, 153 context ON).

**Not done / still open:** soak_test.js has not been run successfully (needs Joe's own foreground tab); the original missing-fill report was not reproduced by hand; the intermittent N/S/E/W rendering bug remains open per the project notes.

`audit_deploy.js`: v3.4.124/125 checks updated for the epsilon and rotDeg change, High detail / Display / resetSharedViewState / refreshCapFillCoverage expectations updated, 6 new checks.

v3.4.125 - 2026-09-30 - Joe, immediately after v3.4.124's azimuth fix: "still wrong needs to be rotated 180 degrees."

v3.4.124 fixed a real, confirmed bug (a ~45deg azimuth baked into the TOP/BOTTOM camera-snap offset). That fix was correct and stays. This is a separate, second adjustment on top of it: TOP's label itself still needed a further, plain 180-degree turn.

Took the opportunity to also remove v3.4.122/123's live GRID_ROTATION_DEG correction (via Texture.rotation) entirely. That mechanism was wrong in 122 (backwards sign), "fixed" in 123, and still wrong per Joe's own report right after -- three attempts at a rotation direction is enough to stop trusting derivation and stop carrying the mechanism forward. Replaced with a plain rotDeg:180 on TOP, baked into the canvas the same proven way E/W/N/S/BOTTOM's own rotDeg already works -- no live per-frame texture update, no GRID_ROTATION_DEG dependency, and critically, no CW/CCW ambiguity: a full 180 is its own inverse regardless of which way you turn it, so this correction can't be backwards the way the last two were.

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload.

`audit_deploy.js`: v3.4.122/123's now-removed checks replaced with one check confirming the Texture.rotation mechanism is gone entirely; 1 new check for TOP's rotDeg:180; v3.4.120's own check (which still referenced TOP's old rotDeg:0) updated to match.

v3.4.124 - 2026-09-29 - Joe's side-by-side screenshots settled it: fresh page load (image 1), click TOP, expected a clean top-down (image 2) -- got one rotated by what turned out to be exactly 45 degrees (image 3). "Top should be north facing" was this bug the whole time, not the grid-rotation question v3.4.122/123 chased.

Root cause, confirmed with real measured numbers on Joe's own live page, not guessed: `snapPerspectiveToDirection('top')` used a camera offset of (0.0001, 0.0001, 1) -- a tiny nudge off the exact vertical pole, needed because OrbitControls' own polar-angle clamp and lookAt's degenerate-parallel-to-up case both misbehave exactly at the pole. But OrbitControls derives its on-screen rotation (azimuth) from that same offset vector via `spherical.setFromVector3()`, and (0.0001, 0.0001) -- EQUAL, both-positive X and Y -- has its own azimuth of atan2(0.0001, 0.0001) = 45 degrees. That 45 was landing directly in the rendered view. Measured directly: `screenUp` after a real TOP click was (-0.71, -0.71, 0), an exact 45-degree diagonal -- not an approximation, not damping, not anything upstream of this one number.

Fixed by moving the epsilon onto the Y axis only: (0, 0.0001, 1). Still dodges the pole by the identical tiny amount; the azimuth this implies is now exactly 0, so nothing diagonal leaks into the click result. Verified directly before shipping: replayed the same position-and-orient math with the corrected vector and got `screenUp` = (0, -1, 0) -- a clean cardinal axis, not a diagonal.

A real cost of chasing the wrong theory for two versions, stated plainly: v3.4.122 and v3.4.123 spent real effort getting Texture.rotation's sign right for a grid-rotation correction that was answering a question Joe never actually asked -- the cube's label texture was never the problem, a single miscalculated epsilon in the camera-snap math was. That code is left in place (it's independently correct, verified, and does what it claims -- nudges the TOP label a further ~29 degrees toward true north on top of this fix's clean local-north baseline) but it was not the fix Joe needed, and won't be re-litigated based on this round alone.

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload.

`audit_deploy.js`: 1 new check.

v3.4.123 - 2026-09-29 - Joe: "North is wrong" after v3.4.122 shipped a grid-rotation correction for the ViewCube's TOP face.

Root cause: THREE.Texture.rotation is documented counterclockwise-positive -- the opposite of the canvas/CSS clockwise-positive convention updateCompass()'s own -GRID_ROTATION_DEG was written in. v3.4.122 copied that sign onto Texture.rotation without accounting for the different property's different convention, flipping the correction's direction entirely (not just its size) -- the fix made it wrong in the other direction instead of right.

This is the fourth time this exact cube has needed a rotation-sign correction caught only by rendering and looking (the E/W/N label mapping, twice, during v3.4.119/120). Treated it the same way this time: built an isolated test (a plane textured with an arrow pointing up plus an asymmetric marker, using ONLY texture.rotation, nothing else) and rendered it at rotation=0 and rotation=+90deg side by side. The arrow moved from pointing up to pointing left -- a counterclockwise rotation for a positive value, confirming Texture.rotation's convention directly rather than trusting documentation recall a second time. Flipped the correction from -GRID_ROTATION_RAD to +GRID_ROTATION_RAD to match.

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload. The isolated test confirms the PROPERTY's rotation direction with high confidence; it does not by itself confirm that TOP now visually points true north on Joe's actual screen, since (as noted in v3.4.122) there is still no live reference on the running page to check that against -- only Joe's own look at it settles that.

`audit_deploy.js`: 1 new check; v3.4.122's now-wrong check corrected in place.

v3.4.122 - 2026-09-29 - Joe: "top should be north facing," then a screenshot of TriBeCa near-top-down showing the cube's TOP label reading diagonally, not matching what the compass indicates.

The TOP face's texture was baked with no rotation, so its "up" reading direction is local +Y with no correction. That's not the same as true north in this app: updateCompass() has applied a GRID_ROTATION_DEG correction since v3.0.27, because Manhattan's real street grid doesn't align to the raw world axes -- true north sits GRID_ROTATION_DEG to the west of local +Y (documented, already-verified in that function's own comment: "facing local +Y ... should show true north slightly to the LEFT of straight up"). TOP's static bake never accounted for this, so it was off by the district's grid-tilt angle (~29 degrees for TriBeCa), not a clean 90.

Fixed by applying updateCompass()'s own exact correction (-GRID_ROTATION_DEG) to the TOP face specifically, via live Texture.rotation rather than baking it into the canvas: GRID_ROTATION_DEG is set per-district, after initViewCube() has already run once at startup, so a static bake would go stale the moment you switch districts. viewCubeTopTexture.rotation is refreshed to -GRID_ROTATION_RAD every frame in updateViewCube(), reading whatever the CURRENT district's real value is.

Deliberately scoped to TOP's visual orientation only. N/S/E/W's rotDeg values and dirKey click-mapping are untouched -- those already match the rest of the app's own local-axis convention (the N/S/E/W nav buttons use the same un-corrected local +Y as "north," not true compass north), and changing that would have created a NEW inconsistency between the cube and the nav buttons it's meant to mirror, not fixed one.

Honest limitation: I could not get a full live visual A/B confirmation before shipping. I tried to read updateCompass()'s own live needle rotation as ground truth to compare against, and found updateCompass() is only called once at page load, not continuously -- so there was no live reference to check the fix against on Joe's actual running page. The correction itself is not a guess: it's the exact, already-shipped, already-verified formula this codebase uses for the identical problem elsewhere, applied through a mechanism (live texture rotation) chosen specifically to survive a district switch. But the visual result on Joe's own screen, matched against what he's actually looking at, is still unconfirmed.

`audit_deploy.js`: 3 new checks; one v3.4.121 check repaired in place after its declaration line changed length.

v3.4.121 - 2026-09-29 - Joe: "why is it so dark?"

The mini-scene's DirectionalLight sat at a single fixed world position (2,3,4), set once in initViewCube() and never touched again -- but the mini-camera orbits every frame to match wherever the user is currently looking (updateViewCube()). Whichever face the camera actually faced was frequently the one facing AWAY from a light that never moved, rendering it dark or near-black regardless of which label the user was trying to read.

Exact same bug class, exact same fix already established in this file for the identical reason: orthoLight (v3.4.24) exists because the main scene's own fixed sun light left whichever facade a locked N/S/E/W elevation was showing dark half the time -- fixed there by repositioning a light to track the camera every frame instead of leaving it fixed. Applied the same pattern here: the directional light is now repositioned to the mini-camera's own position on every updateViewCube() call, so whatever face is actually being looked at is always front-lit.

Verified live before shipping: built the old (fixed-position) and new (camera-tracking) lighting side by side against the same face, viewed from an angle chosen specifically because it's NOT near the old light's fixed position -- the old cube's face rendered essentially unreadable (near-black), the new one clearly lit. Not yet confirmed on Joe's own v3.4.121 reload.

`audit_deploy.js`: 3 new checks.

v3.4.120 - 2026-09-29 - Joe's screenshot: "the view cube does is not functioning as a view cube would... like any other 3d software solution" -- a flat square showing one letter, no visible depth even from a clear 3/4 camera angle.

Root cause: v3.4.119's technique itself, not a tuning problem. Six separate flat PlaneGeometry quads positioned at cube-face offsets never reads as a real 3D object from most angles -- no connecting volume, so it looks like whatever single face happens to be closest to the camera. Rebuilt as one real THREE.BoxGeometry with a 6-material array (E/W/N/S/TOP/BOTTOM matching BoxGeometry's own fixed +X,-X,+Y,-Y,+Z,-Z group order) -- a genuine box volume with real corners and adjacent faces, the standard technique every 3D tool actually uses. Confirmed live: a corner view now shows two labeled faces simultaneously with visible depth between them, not a flat square.

Verifying this surfaced a second, real bug: BoxGeometry's own UV layout is NOT uniform across its six faces (confirmed by dumping geometry.attributes.uv directly -- e.g. the +X face maps its U axis to world Z inverted, while +Z maps U to X direct). The same canvas texture, applied without correction, read upright on some faces and rotated/mirrored on others.

Determined each face's correction empirically, not by formula: render the labeled face alone (every other face plain black) straight-on from its own real-world direction, read whether the text comes out upright. Final values: E=270°, W=90°, N/S/TOP/BOTTOM=0°.

This took two passes to get right, and the failure mode of the first pass is worth recording. Reusing the same canvas/renderer/scene across successive tests produced corrupted, misleading results -- N appeared to need a 90° correction and at one point rendered as "Z", neither of which was real; both were artifacts of stale state bleeding between tests, not the actual UV behavior. Redone with a completely fresh canvas, renderer, and scene for every single face check, N (and every other face) confirmed correct with no rotation at all. The lesson, stated for next time: a verification technique that silently corrupts its own results is worse than not verifying, since it produces false confidence -- rebuilding the test harness cleanly once the anomaly was noticed, rather than trusting the first pass, is what caught it.

Also fixed along the way: an unrelated earlier finding that the ViewCube panel sits close to the compass panel below it and near the viewport edge -- not changed this round, flagged but not acted on, since the depth and label bugs were the confirmed, reported problems.

`audit_deploy.js`: v3.4.119's two now-obsolete checks (asserting the six-plane technique) corrected in place to assert its ABSENCE rather than deleted, consistent with how superseded sections are handled elsewhere in this file; 3 new checks for the actual v3.4.120 implementation.

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload. Everything above was verified via direct injection against his live v3.4.119 session and isolated synthetic tests, not the final packaged file's real click-through-the-page behavior.

v3.4.119 - 2026-09-29 - Joe: "is there we can get view controls in a 3d view top, left, right etc? ... view cube."

Distinct from N/S/E/W/Plan: those lock the scene into the orthographic section system. A ViewCube is a different idea entirely -- snap the FREE perspective camera to a standard angle and leave it exactly as orbit-able as it always was. New small widget, stacked above the existing compass panel (bottom-right): a real 3D cube, rendered in its own small WebGLRenderer/canvas (a second tiny GL context, not scissor math against the main canvas), that rotates to match the current camera orientation every frame and can be clicked on any face to jump the view there.

Two real bugs found and fixed via live testing before shipping, not caught by static reasoning alone -- both traced with the same technique: pick an unambiguous camera angle (straight down from above, due east, due north) and check which label actually renders.

1. **Label mapping was inverted.** First attempt used "the face you'd see while standing IN that view carries that label" (N's face normal = world -Y). Looked right on paper. Live: pointing the camera straight down from above showed BOTTOM, not TOP. The actual, simpler rule: N's face carries world +Y directly -- approach the cube from the north and you see the face labeled N, like a real physical die.

2. **Even with the correct mapping, the face geometry pointed inward.** `lookAt(0,0,0)` alone left the textured front of each plane facing the cube's own center; from outside, nothing was visible until `material.side = DoubleSide` was added purely as a diagnostic, and even then the label read mirrored (wrong side of the plane facing out). Fixed by flipping each face 180 degrees after `lookAt()`, back to single-sided FrontSide.

Along the way, a third issue surfaced and was fixed before it could bite: `lookAt(0,0,0)`'s default up vector (0,1,0) is exactly parallel to the N/S faces' own target direction (both lie along Y) -- a genuinely undefined case for the basis Three.js builds internally. A single fixed replacement up vector would only move the same problem onto TOP/BOTTOM (both along Z). Each face now gets whichever of (0,0,1)/(0,1,0) is non-degenerate for its own direction.

Confirmed against three independent, unambiguous camera angles before shipping: straight down from above -> TOP (correct, non-mirrored), due east -> E, due north -> N. Also confirmed the click/raycast path separately: identifying which face was hit under the corrected geometry still resolves to the correct dirKey.

`snapPerspectiveToDirection()` reuses the exact direction vectors and "camera stands opposite the label, facing toward it" convention `setOrthogonalView()` already established (v3.0.18) for N/S/E/W -- clicking the cube's N face puts the camera in the identical position the N nav button would, the only difference being which camera (perspective, never `orthoCamera`) and that no section mode ever engages. The ViewCube only shows in the free perspective view (hidden during any locked N/S/E/W/Plan elevation, which already has the compass for that).

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload -- everything above was verified via direct injection into his live v3.4.118 session, not the final packaged file's actual UI (positioning, click-through-the-real-DOM-canvas, and the stacked-panel layout still need his own look). `audit_deploy.js`: 5 new checks.

v3.4.118 - 2026-09-29 - Joe: "keyboard arrow nudging seems to freeze the app can you check."

Measured live on his own Upper West Side session (4979 buildings) before touching anything: a single setYCutaway() call -- one keystroke's worth of work -- took 2642ms. Almost all of it is rebuildBuildingXYCap()/rebuildBuildingZCap() (v3.4.91/93) re-slicing EVERY building footprint in the district and merging the result, synchronously on the main thread, on every single call -- with no debounce, since v3.4.91/93 shipped.

That's fine for a mouse click. It's not fine for a held arrow key: the keyboard nudge handler (v3.2.28) calls setXCutaway()/setYCutaway()/setHeightCut() directly on every keydown, and OS key-repeat fires those every ~30-50ms once a key is held. JS is single-threaded, so each repeat queues another 2.6s of work behind whatever's still running -- holding a key for two seconds backs up over a minute of unresponsive freeze, growing without bound the longer the key stays down. Same mechanism, smaller scale, on a fast slider drag (the slider's own 'input' event calls these same setters).

Fixed by debouncing just the expensive part: a new scheduleBuildingCapRebuild(axis) coalesces to at most one requestAnimationFrame callback per axis, regardless of how many events fire before it runs -- reading xThreshold/yThreshold/sectionPlane.constant fresh when it does, so a burst of keypresses collapses to the LATEST value, not a queue of stale ones. The cheap parts of these setters (plane constants, slider/number display, handle positions, the skyline profile) are completely untouched and stay exactly as synchronous/responsive as before. buildBuildingCapFillGroups()'s own initial build (once per compute) still calls rebuildBuildingZCap()/rebuildBuildingXYCap() directly, on purpose -- nothing to debounce for a one-time call.

Known limitation, stated plainly: this fixes the FREEZE (unbounded backlog), not the underlying slowness -- each individual commit in a large district still takes real time (measured 2.6s here), so nudging will still feel a beat behind in a big district, just no longer compound into a multi-minute lockup. Speeding up the rebuild itself (e.g. spatially culling to buildings actually near the current threshold instead of slicing the whole district every time) is a real, separate, larger change -- not attempted this round; flagged as a follow-on if the residual lag itself becomes the complaint.

`audit_deploy.js`: 3 new checks. Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload -- the 2642ms measurement above is real, live, on his own data; the debounce itself needs an actual held-key test on his machine to confirm the freeze is gone in practice, not just in the math.

v3.4.117 - 2026-09-29 - Joe's screenshots: Buildings AND Negative space toggled on at once, a box active, N view in Upper West Side -- an isolated dark rectangle floating far from the box.

Root-caused live using the project's own hide-one-thing-at-a-time technique: solidGroup, all three capFillGroups, and streetsGroup were each hidden in turn on his real session with the artifact still present; hiding one specific mesh removed it. That mesh turned out to be buildingXCapMaterial/buildingYCapMaterial -- the solid cut-face shown where a locked axis slices through a building's interior, Buildings mode only (v3.4.82/93). Two compounding causes: buildBuildingXYCapGeometry() slices EVERY building footprint in the district at the current threshold, not just ones in a drawn box; and the two materials' clippingPlanes were fixed to the whole site (...SITE_BOUND_PLANES) at construction time and never revisited -- unlike syncBuildingClipping()/the poche/ground/streets, which all correctly box-scope themselves already. With a box active but no box-aware crop on these two materials specifically, the cap kept showing real cut faces for buildings anywhere in the district that happened to cross that one line.

This combination -- Buildings and Negative space BOTH on -- had never been tested. Every existing smoke-test phase deliberately keeps exactly one of the two toggles on at a time (setMode()), which is exactly why this survived every prior fix.

Fixed with a new syncBuildingCapClipping(axis), called from applySectionMode() right after the existing syncBuildingClipping(), adding the identical box-plotted-range crop (same getSectionRanges(axis).plotLo/plotHi, same plane normals) that the real solid already gets -- on top of each material's existing site-wide base, not replacing it, so the no-box case is untouched. Verified twice on Joe's live session before shipping: (1) applied the fix's exact new plane values to the actual broken mesh and re-rendered -- the stray geometry disappeared, screenshot-confirmed; (2) later, on the still-unfixed live build, confirmed both cap materials read exactly 8 clip planes with no box-boundary match -- the precise broken state the fix targets.

smoke_test.js: added Phase 9, the first test to turn Buildings and Negative space on simultaneously with a box active, checking both cap materials are box-scoped in all four directions. Run as a negative control against the live, unfixed build: both xCapScoped and yCapScoped came back false (8 clip planes, no box match) -- exactly the reported bug, confirming the test catches it.

`audit_deploy.js`: 5 new checks.

v3.4.116 - 2026-09-28 - Joe, on the "density of the floor" follow-up: "there are a lot more building/forms in plan view from what i see in elevation. so either the plan geometry is wrong or the elevation is." Then, after I laid out the mechanism and asked how to proceed: "well it needs to read correctly."

Checked both. Plan geometry is correct -- it shows every building whose footprint overlaps the drawn box. The real building SOLID has also always clipped to the box's FULL depth on the cut axis (`getCutAxisClipPlanes()`, using `activeSectionBox.xMin/xMax` or `.yMin/.yMax` directly, unchanged since v3.4.13). The actual bug: `getSectionRanges()` fed `buildSectionProfile()` (the poche's own hole-punch profile, what Negative-space mode actually shows) a thin +-2m slab at ONE line (`xThreshold`/`yThreshold`), regardless of the box's real depth -- so Buildings mode showed everything the box's real depth contains, but Negative-space mode only punched holes for buildings crossing that one specific line. Two display modes of the same box disagreeing was the tell.

Verified with real numbers before changing anything: drew a box in Harlem/Polo Grounds, found 3 buildings whose footprint overlaps it, only 2 crossed the old 2m slab. This is exactly the earlier "single-line section vs. full box" tension from the tabled Option A/B design fork -- Option B (shipped, v3.4.53) widened the CUTOUT to match the profile at one line, but never widened the line itself. Joe's "it needs to read correctly" is the answer to that fork, finally given: when a box is active, use it fully.

Fixed: `getSectionRanges()`'s box-active branches now use the box's own `xMin/xMax` (axis x) or `yMin/yMax` (axis y) as `slabLo/slabHi` -- identical to what `getCutAxisClipPlanes()` already uses for the real solid, so Buildings and Negative-space modes now agree, and Negative-space shows everything Plan's box outline actually contains. The free full-site case (no box) is untouched -- a true single-line section is still exactly right there, since there's no box expressing how deep the user wants to look.

Live-verified the fix's actual output against the same box and real data before shipping: recomputed the crossing count with the new slab -- 3 of 3, matching the box footprint exactly (previously 2 of 3).

`smoke_test.js`: added a check that every building in a drawn box's footprint also crosses the section slab (Negative-space mode) -- this is what would have caught the bug directly, rather than just checking "something shows." `audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks; the v3.4.50 (Option A) section corrected in place rather than deleted -- its no-box checks are still accurate, its box-active checks now assert the behavior they used to forbid, with the history kept in the comment rather than erased. 345/346 (the one failure is this entry's own version bump, expected until this ships).

Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload.

v3.4.115 - 2026-09-28 - Joe: "we need to examine both sides Manhattan and Non Manhattan -- this was a rule we made a long time ago."

I could not find that rule written anywhere in the project notes (searched the whole Negative Space file), so I am recording my reading of it here so it stops living only in memory: EVERY view/fix is examined with the Manhattan context layer both OFF and ON. It is a real axis: the layer changes framing (Plan frames the whole ~20km island when it is on), adds a large backdrop, and its toggle routes through the perspective camera without clearing the elevation state. v3.4.114's fix was found and first verified on the ON side only; the OFF side was never re-run.

`smoke_test.js` now takes a context side (`runSmokeTest(label, { context: 'off' | 'on' })`) and has `runSmokeBothSides(label)`, which runs the full matrix twice: once with context OFF, once ON (the second pass reuses the computed district). ON-side additions: context layer shown in Plan / hidden in every locked elevation (full-site and box-scoped), and the elevation must frame the site (ortho frustum < 8000m), plus everything the OFF pass already checks. With context ON, Plan frames the whole island, so a 180m box would be ~7px wide; the ON pass zooms Plan to the site first, as a real user does, via a `goPlan()` helper used before every box draw. Phase 8 (toggle the layer through every view) now restores whichever side the pass is testing.

Negative control on Joe's live, unfixed v3.4.112 (Inwood): context OFF 112/118, context ON 99/129. OFF fails only on the two bugs already fixed since (box N/S edge handles, v3.4.113; context layer in elevations, v3.4.114). ON adds the context-specific failures (layer visible in 8 full-site and 8 box-scoped elevations, framing 8, plus Phase 8's 4) and nothing else -- no unexplained failures on either side.

Not yet done: the same both-sides run on v3.4.114+ (needs Joe's reload), and the other districts. Joe: if I have misread which two "sides" you meant, tell me and I will change the test.

`audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks, one older check loosened for the new signature.

v3.4.114 - 2026-09-28 - Joe: "also North South East West elevations are not correct" (screenshot: Inwood, Negative space, South elevation, Manhattan context ON -- a huge gray island slab filling the view behind and around the section).

Same class as v3.4.110's streets, different layer. The Manhattan context layer is an extruded, ~0.92-opacity plate of the whole island built in `loadData()` and shown whenever its toggle is on; nothing ever gated it by view mode. In Plan/Perspective that's the point (orientation). In a locked N/S/E/W elevation it renders behind the section, so the building holes show gray slab instead of open background and the elevation reads wrong. Second contributor: with the layer on, `currentTargetAndSpan()` framed the elevation on the whole ~20km island rather than the site.

Reproduced live on his district (Inwood, Manhattan context on, box, S): the section floated in front of a gray backdrop. Forcing the layer invisible on the same live view gave the correct elevation -- warm poche, red skyline, open background through the building holes -- so the mechanism was confirmed before the code change.

Fix: `updateContextLayerVisibility()` hides the layer whenever `inLockedElevation()` and restores it to exactly `showBoroughs` otherwise; called from the borough toggle, `applySectionMode()` and `refreshViewToggles()` (the last covers `zoomToBoroughContext()`/`exitBoroughContext()`, which run after the camera changes). `currentTargetAndSpan(forElevation)` only uses the island span when NOT framing an elevation, and `setOrthogonalView()` passes the flag.

A trap found on the way: `inLockedElevation()` is `sectionModeAxis !== null && activeCamera === orthoCamera`, not just the axis. Turning Manhattan context ON from inside an elevation goes through `resetSharedViewState()`, which swaps back to the perspective camera but does NOT clear `sectionModeAxis`. A bare axis check would have hidden the very layer the user just switched on, in the borough view. The v3.4.110 street rule had the same latent quirk and now uses the same helper.

Test: `smoke_test.js` Phase 8 -- context visible in Plan when on, hidden in each locked elevation with the ortho frustum framing the site (< 8000m), back in Plan, and off when toggled off. Run against the live, unfixed v3.4.112 as a negative control: the four elevation-hidden checks and the four framing checks all fail (layer stays visible in every elevation; ortho frustum ~23,000m, i.e. the whole island), while the Plan-visible, back-in-Plan and toggled-off checks pass -- exactly the reported fault and nothing else.

Not verified: the smoke test checks state, not pixels; a real look at the fixed elevation in Joe's browser is the confirmation. `audit_deploy.js`: 4 new checks plus 3 older checks repaired to match changed code (none deleted).

v3.4.113 - 2026-09-28 - Joe: "cannot drag shape handle North & South edges" (screenshot: a tall narrow box in Inwood in the awaiting-direction state).

Reproduced live before touching code. Drew a 100m x 300m box, grabbed the middle of its NORTH edge and dragged it 40m north: the WEST edge moved 50m and the north edge did not move. Comparing the old hit test against the same box: it returned the west edge for every probe point -- north-mid, south-mid, and even the dead center of the box.

Two compounding causes in `hitTestBoxEdge()`: (1) the grab tolerance is 1.5% of the district span (~60m in Inwood), wider than half of a narrow box, so every point on the N/S edges also sat within tolerance of the W or E edge; (2) it returned the FIRST edge within tolerance and tested W/E before N/S, so W/E always won those ties -- N/S were unreachable on any box narrower than twice the tolerance. Tall narrow boxes (a couple of lots wide, several blocks long) hit this every time; wide boxes mostly didn't, which is why it wasn't noticed earlier.

Fix: each axis's tolerance is capped at 30% of the box's own size on that axis (a thin box keeps a real interior), the NEAREST edge within tolerance wins (corners and near-ties resolve to what you pointed at), and `boxEdgeGrabTolerance()` is additionally capped at ~12 screen pixels on the orthographic Plan camera, since 60m is enormous once you zoom in to draw a box. Checked the new logic against the same box with the tolerance the live page computes: north-mid -> yMax, south-mid -> yMin, west/east unchanged, interior -> nothing (no longer steals the box body).

Test: `smoke_test.js` Phase 7 drags each of the four edges of a tall-narrow and a wide-short box by 30m and asserts only that edge moved. Run against the live, unfixed v3.4.112 as a negative control: exactly the two tall-narrow N/S cases fail (each moved the west edge instead), and the other six pass -- the same failure Joe reported, nothing else.

Not fixed / not verified: only pointer events dispatched from script were used; a real mouse drag on Joe's machine is the actual confirmation. `audit_deploy.js`: 3 new checks.

v3.4.112 - 2026-09-28 - Joe's UES screenshots ("what a mess"): drew a box, picked W (fine), then S -- one flat, holeless gray block, with the Y slider disabled so there was no way out of it.

Reproduced live on his district before touching code. Sequence: a box whose Y midpoint sat in a street gap, W via the box picker, then S via the compass. Result: yThreshold parked at 96 (inside a gap), skyline profile `maxZ` 0 with zero non-zero points -- an empty section -- and the Y slider disabled.

Root cause is the v3.4.109 bug reached through a SECOND entry path. v3.4.109 taught only `applyPlanBoxDirection()` (the box picker) to snap the cut to a real building crossing. The compass buttons go through `setOrthogonalView()`, whose v3.4.51 recenter still used the raw box midpoint, and only fired when the threshold was OUTSIDE the box -- a threshold inside the box but stranded in a gap was left alone. A third copy of the same raw-midpoint logic lived in `resetCutaway()` (the Recenter button).

Fix: one shared `snapBoxCutToBuildings(axis)` (with `boxCutHasBuildings()`, the same slab + plotted-range test `buildSectionProfile()` applies). It leaves a threshold alone only when it is inside the box AND has buildings; otherwise it moves to the real crossing nearest the box midpoint. `setOrthogonalView()` (compass) uses it, and `resetCutaway()` now uses `findNearestBuildingCrossing()` for both axes. Verified the mechanism on his live page: forcing the slider to the value the snap chooses (93, 3m from the midpoint) turned the empty profile (`maxZ` 0) into a real one (`maxZ` 56.9).

Second finding, same screenshot: the active-axis slider was disabled. v3.4.60 dimmed X/Y sliders in a locked elevation on the premise that Option B made them inert. That premise is false in the current code: the section profile is rebuilt from a +-2m slab at the threshold (v3.4.50), so moving the slider changes which buildings the section contains -- measured live: five slider positions in a locked N view gave five different skyline profiles (2058-2082 segments, peak 22-40m). Dimming it stranded the user in an empty section. The ACTIVE axis's slider is now live in a locked elevation; Height and the other axis stay inert as before. The stale Option B tradeoff comment is superseded here.

The test gap, stated plainly: `smoke_test.js` (v3.4.111) never took this path -- it only used the box picker, and it checked that a fill was *visible*, which is true even for an empty section. Its first compass sweep (typical box) passed on the broken build, so that version of the sweep would not have caught this either -- and so did a second draft that used a gap-midpoint box but left thresholds where earlier phases put them (a leftover threshold inside the box hides the bug). The final Phase 6 forces each threshold outside the box before every compass step, and was confirmed as a real negative control: against the live, unfixed v3.4.111 it fails every step (section empty, `maxZ` 0; active slider disabled). Rewritten: (1) a real emptiness check -- the skyline profile's tallest point must be > 0 in every Negative-space elevation, box or not; (2) Phase 6 builds an ADVERSARIAL box whose midpoint on both axes sits in a street gap, picks W, then sweeps the compass N/S/E/W, checking non-empty + active slider enabled; (3) the wait no longer uses setTimeout, which Chrome throttles in a hidden tab (a run that took ~20 min now takes ~3); (4) box placement is data-driven so it lands on real buildings in any district. Also added `soak_test.js`, a randomized burst-transition test with invariant checks for the long-standing intermittent N/S/E/W bug; it has NOT been run yet and needs a foreground tab.

Multi-district results on v3.4.111 (before this fix): Midtown, Upper East Side, East Harlem, Inwood all 55/55 on the earlier test; TriBeCa 64/64. Those runs used typical boxes and did not cover the gap-midpoint case.

`audit_deploy.js`: 3 new checks; the stale v3.4.51 block was rewritten and three older checks (v3.4.60 inert-row, resetCutaway, smoke_test signature) were repaired to match the changed code, none deleted. Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload.

v3.4.111 - 2026-09-27 - Joe: "we need to catch these issues upfront before deployment." Added `smoke_test.js`, a live-browser test that drives the compute-state x display-mode x view x box x Major-Streets matrix -- every one of v3.4.105-110's bugs was an unvisited cell in that grid.

`audit_deploy.js` runs under Node against static source and can't see a THREE.js scene, a compute, or a click, so it can't catch this class of bug. `smoke_test.js` runs inside a live page (paste into the console, or run via Claude-in-Chrome): pre-compute Buildings nav, compute, full-site nav in both display modes, right-click-during-box-draw must not draw a box (v3.4.108), then a drawn box in all four directions in both modes, checking that the right quad/outline is showing, that some real building overlaps the plotted range, and that streets are hidden in every locked elevation (v3.4.110). It checks STATE, not pixels -- a screenshot pass is still required before shipping.

Two bugs in the test itself, caught by its own first real run rather than assumed: (1) it treated the app's deliberate post-compute behavior -- entering N/S/E/W auto-switches Buildings off and Negative space on (v3.4.31/48) -- as a failure; it now reads the mode actually active after the click. (2) Its "both display modes" loops only labeled the two passes and never actually switched modes; fixed with a `setMode()` helper that puts the toggles in a known state first.

`window.__NS` now also exposes `streetsGroup` and `showStreets` (purely additive) so street visibility can be checked directly instead of by color-matching the scene graph, which is how v3.4.110 was diagnosed. `audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks. Confirmed against live v3.4.111: 64/64 passing, and both street predicates and the empty-fill predicate were shown to flip to failing when the corresponding bug is deliberately re-injected (a test that has never failed on a real fault isn't trustworthy). Added two street checks after the first packaging (streets shown in Plan, and the Streets toggle clicked inside a locked elevation must not bring them back).

Known limits, stated plainly: the "real building crosses" check is looser than the app's own 2m slab (thresholds aren't exposed on `__NS`), so it catches "nothing here at all," not every thin miss. It runs against one district per invocation and takes minutes in a background tab because Chrome throttles it; run it in a foreground tab if possible.

v3.4.110 - 2026-09-27 - Joe: "layers of i don't know what in elevation. this project is about negative space between building. not layers of road, sidewalk etc. this need to be reconciled" -- screenshots showed a cool gray-blue translucent rectangle sitting behind/around the correctly-shaped buildings in a locked N and E elevation.

First theory (negativeMesh's own ghost-shell material, a similar cool gray-blue, stuck visible) -- asked Joe to check `window.__NS.negativeMesh.visible` in his own console. Came back `false`. Ruled out cleanly, no code touched on that guess.

Asked for a full scene-graph dump instead of guessing again -- Joe pasted his own console output. It showed dozens of `Mesh` entries, color `9fb8cc`, opacity 0.7, scattered at real-world positions up to ~1900m+ away from his actual drawn box (e.g. y:1922-2251 while his box sat far outside that range). That color and pattern matches the MAJOR STREETS ribbon geometry exactly -- confirmed against the code: `updateStreetLabelVisibility()` (v3.4.76) has only ever box-scoped the street NAME LABEL sprites; the ribbon Mesh geometry itself was explicitly "always visible" by original design, never gated by view mode at all. That's correct in Plan/Perspective (streets everywhere is the point) but was never reconsidered for locked N/S/E/W elevations -- where it means the ENTIRE district's street network renders edge-on, stacking hundreds of small translucent ribbon segments into exactly the flat gray-blue mess in the screenshots, regardless of whether a box scopes the view.

Live-confirmed the mechanism directly (not just the theory): found the same streetsGroup on a live session, forced it invisible mid-session, and re-rendered -- small artifact ticks bleeding into the elevation's base line disappeared. Combined with Joe's own scene dump, treated this as confirmed.

Fixed in `updateStreetLabelVisibility()`: the whole `streetsGroup` (ribbons AND labels) now hides entirely whenever `sectionModeAxis !== null` (any locked elevation), regardless of `showStreets` or box scoping -- matching Joe's own stated principle that elevations here are about the void between real buildings, not ground infrastructure. Plan/Perspective's own street display (box-scoped or full-district) is completely untouched. The Streets toggle button (`refreshStreetsToggle()`) now routes through this same function instead of setting `streetsGroup.visible` directly, so clicking it while already locked into an elevation can't fight the new rule -- `showStreets` still applies exactly as before the moment you're back in Plan/Perspective.

`audit_deploy.js` updated (3 new checks), 329/329 passing. Syntax-checked clean (`node --check`). Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload -- the live checks above verified the mechanism (both via his own scene dump and a direct live toggle test), but only an actual reload + the same box + the same locked view confirms the shipped code itself.

v3.4.109 - 2026-09-27 - Joe: "local live. see images no north or south views for section box" -- two screenshots, a tall box drawn in Chelsea/Clinton/Hudson Yards, E/W looked fine, N showed one flat, textureless gray block with no red outline at all.

Live-reproduced the exact symptom first (Claude-in-Chrome, real building data, not a guess): drew a similarly-shaped box, picked N, got the identical flat block. Root cause: `applyPlanBoxDirection()` has always centered the cut threshold on the box's literal geometric MIDPOINT (`setYCutaway((box.yMin + box.yMax) / 2)`), but the real cut this app draws is still just a thin `SECTION_SLAB_HALF_WIDTH` (2m) slab at that exact position, unchanged since v3.4.50 -- deliberately left untouched this round (Joe tabled the broader section-cutout design question separately; this fix doesn't touch that constant or reopen it). If nothing real crosses that specific 2m line -- a street, a courtyard, a gap between buildings -- the whole discard profile comes back empty, and the result is one flat, holeless block.

Verified directly against the real per-building geometry (extracted actual bounding boxes from `solidGroup`'s own meshes in a live session, not a reimplementation of the projection math, to rule out any transcription mismatch): for Joe's exact box, the X-midpoint (E/W's own cut variable) genuinely crosses a real building within the box's own footprint -- that's why E/W worked. The Y-midpoint (N/S's own cut variable), same box, crosses nothing within the box's footprint at all -- confirmed with a direct query, not an assumption. Same box, same underlying mechanism, opposite result purely by chance depending on where the geometric midpoint happened to land.

Fixed with a new `findNearestBuildingCrossing(axis, box, target)`: instead of the raw midpoint, finds the real building crossing on the cut axis nearest to it, restricted to buildings whose extent on the OTHER (plotted/screen) axis actually falls inside the drawn box -- a building nowhere near the box's own footprint can't steal the cut line. Falls back to the plain midpoint (today's behavior) if truly nothing in the box qualifies, so this can never produce a worse result than before. Still the same "thin true 2m slice" semantics everywhere else in the app -- just picked somewhere that actually shows something. Live-verified the fix's own output against the same real building data before shipping: the new Y value it picks for Joe's exact box does cross a real building within the box's footprint, confirmed with the same direct query used to diagnose the bug.

`audit_deploy.js` updated (2 new checks, 2 stale v3.4.107 checks repaired to match the changed lines), 326/326 passing. Syntax-checked clean (`node --check`). Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload -- the live checks above verified the underlying mechanism (old midpoint's real miss, new crossing's real hit) against his exact repro data, but only an actual reload + drawing that same box + picking N confirms the shipped code itself.

v3.4.108 - 2026-09-27 - Joe tabled the section-cutout design fork discussion (Option B has been the settled, shipped behavior since v3.4.53 -- an earlier reply this session had wrongly represented it as still an open decision; corrected, nothing touched, no code change from that). Separately: "when drawing section box i usually use right mouse button to pan and then zoom in. current behavior right mouse button draws a section box."

Live-confirmed the bug first, not just from the report: dispatched a real right-button (button: 2) pointerdown/move/up sequence at the canvas during box-draw mode on the live v3.4.107 session -- came back with a genuine `pendingPlanBox`, exactly as described.

Root cause: the box-draw `pointerdown` handler (`attachPlanBoxListeners()`) never checked which mouse button was actually pressed -- it ran identically for left, right, or middle click. The box-draw banner has said "right-drag to pan" since v3.4.0 (see its own markup), and `setBoxDrawMode()`'s own comment explicitly reasons that pan/zoom "never conflicted with box-drawing in the first place" -- both were describing the INTENDED behavior, but nothing had ever actually enforced it. `controls.enabled` is deliberately left true during box-draw specifically so pan/zoom keep working (a past live-verify fix, still correct) -- but with no button check, this handler's own `e.preventDefault()` and pointer capture intercepted a right-drag before OrbitControls' own default RIGHT = PAN could take it.

Fixed with a single early return -- `if (e.button !== 0) return;` -- right at the top of the pointerdown handler, before any raycasting or edge-hit-testing happens. `e.button === 0` is the primary button (left click, and a touch contact's equivalent), so this leaves right and middle clicks completely unhandled by this listener, falling through untouched to OrbitControls' own listener on the same element. Left-click behavior (drawing a new box, grabbing an existing box's edge) is unchanged.

`audit_deploy.js` updated (1 new check), 324/324 passing. Syntax-checked clean (`node --check`). Not yet confirmed on Joe's own reload -- the live check above verified the bug's exact mechanism on the OLD code; the fix itself needs an actual reload + a real right-drag to confirm.

v3.4.107 - 2026-09-27 - Joe asked to start on the open "intermittent N/S/E/W rendering bug" item -- instead of guessing at a repro, he sent 3 screenshots of Chelsea/Clinton/Hudson Yards: Buildings-only, a box drawn in Plan, then E and W both showing almost nothing -- one giant thin red rectangle spanning nearly the whole screen, with a tiny sliver of real content in one corner.

Live-reproduced the exact sequence against a fresh Claude-in-Chrome session (Chelsea/Clinton/Hudson Yards, Compute, Plan, Buildings-only, drew a box, picked E) after the extension dropped once mid-session and had to be reconnected. Confirmed via `window.__NS`: `capFillGroups.x.userData.quad.visible` was `false` and the outline showing instead had real world-space points spanning the site's FULL Y range (0 to 1966m) at a fixed X -- while `activeSectionBox`'s own real Y range was only ~445m. The camera, correctly framed tight to the small box, was looking at a rectangle roughly 4x too large on the plotted axis -- explaining exactly what the screenshots showed: mostly empty, with only a stray edge or corner of the oversized outline crossing the visible frustum.

Root cause: `capQuadBounds()` (added v3.4.99, decides whether a section shows a filled poché wash or a thin boundary outline based on how much of the site it would cover) was written entirely in terms of the classic single-threshold octant-cutaway model (`yThreshold`/`flipYCutaway`/`siteMinY`/`siteMaxY`) and never updated when box-scoped sections (v3.4.0, `getSectionRanges()`/`activeSectionBox`) were added -- `getSectionRanges()` itself already substitutes the box's own min/max on the plotted axis, `capQuadBounds()` never got the same treatment. With a box active, this badly overestimated the real coverage ratio (comparing the box's real, small footprint against the WHOLE site), tripping the >0.5 outline threshold, and then drew that outline using site-wide corners instead of the box's own.

A second, compounding bug: even with `capQuadBounds()` fixed, `applyPlanBoxDirection()` was setting `activeSectionBox = box` AFTER calling `setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()` -- which is what actually triggers the coverage recompute (via `syncCapFillPlanes()` -> `refreshCapFillCoverage()`). So the recompute always ran one step stale, seeing the PREVIOUS box (or null, on a first-ever draw) rather than the one just drawn. Fixed by reordering: `activeSectionBox` is now set first.

Live-verified the actual fix's effect directly against Joe's real repro data before writing it: recomputed the real ratio (0.23, using the box's real ~445m Y-span against the site's ~1967m) -- correctly under the 0.5 threshold, correctly picks the filled quad -- and rendered it, showing real buildings and a correct red skyline outline inside the drawn box, nothing like the broken screenshots.

Also fixed the same gap in reverse: `clearActiveSectionBox()` ("Full width") now re-runs `refreshCapFillCoverage()` after clearing the box, so the outline/fill decision reflects the real, now-full-site exposed area again rather than staying stale at whatever the box-scoped ratio last was.

**Open question, not yet answered:** whether this is the SAME bug as the long-standing "intermittent N/S/E/W rendering bug" (solid unclipped block / flat-empty skyline / stray band, last confirmed recurring v3.4.47) or a genuinely separate one that happened to look similar. This fix is scoped specifically to the box-drawn case -- `capQuadBounds()`'s non-box branches (`flipYCutaway`/`yThreshold`/site-wide) are untouched, so the classic no-box symptom, if it's still real, is not addressed by this and needs its own live repro. Worth testing after this ships: does the classic bug still occur WITHOUT ever drawing a box?

Traced live: `solidGroup.visible` read `false`. Root cause is in `setOrthogonalView()`, not `applySectionMode()` -- the v3.4.31/v3.4.48 auto-toggle pair ("entering N/S/E/W turns Negative space on, Buildings off") fires unconditionally on entering an elevation, which is correct post-compute (there's always a negativeMesh to switch TO) but pre-compute `negativeMesh` doesn't exist, so `showNegative = true` is a silent no-op while `showBuildings = false` still fires -- leaving nothing visible at all. Fixed by gating both halves of that transition on `negativeMesh` actually existing -- pre-compute, entering an elevation now leaves Buildings as the only (and correct) thing showing, untouched; post-compute behavior (the whole reason v3.4.31/v3.4.48 exist) is completely unchanged.

**This is exactly the class of bug the standing rule exists to catch** -- v3.4.105's own clipping-plane mechanism was independently live-verified against real data before shipping, but a downstream side effect of unblocking `applySectionMode()` (a second, unrelated function reading the same `negativeMesh` truthiness) only surfaced once Joe actually clicked the real button on the real page. Reasoning about every call downstream of a guard is necessary but not sufficient -- a real click is still the only thing that catches an interaction between two separately-correct pieces of code.

Live-verified the toggle-restore mechanism only (via the Buildings button, on the OLD v3.4.105 code, to get Joe's session back to a working state) -- the actual gating fix above has NOT yet been live-tested, since it requires the new file. v3.4.104 - 2026-09-24 - Joe's UI/UX audit, continued: "no way to share or return to a specific configuration" -- nothing about neighborhood, cutaway values, or which layer was showing lived in the URL at all.

Investigated the OTHER queued item first (full navigation gated behind Compute for a Buildings-only user) and found it's more separable than first estimated -- site bounds (siteMinX/MaxX/MinY/MaxY/siteCapHeight) are computed purely from buildingsGeo, the raw building data, completely independent of the actual Manifold CSG boolean. But `siteCapHeight === undefined` is used as a gate in a large number of places throughout the file, and de-risking a change to WHEN it becomes defined would mean auditing each one to make sure none silently assumes "negative space is also ready," not just the ones directly relevant to navigation. That's touching enough of the codebase to want explicit sign-off on the approach before starting, rather than re-architecting a load-bearing assumption on a brief "continue" -- flagged to Joe, not started this round.

Implemented URL state instead, since it's additive rather than something that reaches into existing gating logic. `syncUrlFromState()` encodes district (`d`), Height/X/Y cutaway (`h`/`x`/`y`, always real meters regardless of the current display unit -- unambiguous for whoever opens the link), flip states (`fx`/`fy`), and which layer(s) are showing (`l`: b/n/both) via `history.replaceState` (never `pushState` -- moving a slider shouldn't spam browser history). Hooked into `syncCapFillPlanes()` (covers every Height/X/Y/flip change, since all of them route through it directly or via `setHeightCut()`/`setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()`) and both layer-toggle click handlers -- 3 call sites total, not duplicated into each individual setter.

`applyUrlState()` is the inverse, replacing the old unconditional `loadData(NEIGHBORHOODS[0].id)` in `boot()`: falls back to that same default district when `d` is missing or invalid, and only runs `computeNegativeSpace()` + applies the rest when at least one of h/x/y/l is actually present -- a bare district link doesn't force a compute nobody asked for. The layer-restore logic accounts for the real post-compute defaults (`showBuildings=true`, `showNegative=false`) rather than clicking every toggle unconditionally -- caught and fixed a real bug in my own first draft here, where `l==='b'` (buildings only, already the default) would have gotten an extra click that toggled buildings OFF instead of leaving them on.

Added a **Copy link** button in Export, since the URL updating silently has no discoverability of its own -- writes `location.href` directly rather than reconstructing its own copy of the same params, so it can never drift out of sync with what `syncUrlFromState()` actually wrote.

**Deliberately out of scope for this version:** camera view/orientation (N/S/E/W/Plan/Home) and an active drawn box. Restoring either means also reconciling `pendingPlanBox`/box-drawn state and `setOrthogonalView()`'s target-span framing math -- real additional surface area, noted directly in the Copy link tooltip and the help modal rather than silently doing less than the button implies.

v3.4.99 - 2026-09-24 - Joe (with a screenshot): "ok thanks also another questions the red color as a cut plane what could we do with this ? red outline boundary? Seem very strong against other colors" -- the big flat red plane is the Section fill (poché), a deliberate feature (v3.0.29 -- Joe asked for a solid-reading fill instead of a hollow outline). It reads fine at typical size. The problem: Z's own cap (Height Cut) is UNCONDITIONALLY the full site rectangle whenever it's shown at all (its restrictPlanes dropped the other two axes back in v3.4.88), so at any Height Cut value, in free Perspective, the fill covers the entire visible site -- that's what read as overwhelming, not the color itself. X/Y's caps genuinely vary in size (bounded by the OTHER axis's threshold and how much height range is still exposed above the cut), so the same principle applies conditionally there.

Offered three options: lower the fill opacity everywhere, always use an outline instead of a fill (Joe's own suggestion), or fill only when the exposed area is small and switch to outline once it's large. Joe picked the third -- doesn't trade away the "solid material" feature he originally asked for, just stops applying it past the point where it becomes visual noise.

Implemented via `capQuadBounds(axis)`: computes each cap's REAL rectangle directly from state already in memory (siteMinX/MaxX/MinY/MaxY, xThreshold/yThreshold, flipXCutaway/flipYCutaway, sectionPlane.constant, siteCapHeight) -- no polygon-clipping needed, since every one of a cap's restrictPlanes is a single axis-aligned half-plane, so the clipped shape is always a plain rectangle. Z is unconditionally ratio=1 (matching its real always-full-site behavior); X/Y multiply the OTHER axis's coverage fraction by the height range still exposed above the cut. `makeCapOutline()` builds a thin LineLoop per cap (same accent-red, theme-synced through the existing `pocheMaterials` array); `refreshCapFillCoverage()` sets each cap's exact outline geometry and toggles quad.visible/outline.visible directly against a 0.5 coverage threshold (`POCHE_OUTLINE_COVERAGE_THRESHOLD`) -- independent of each group's own on/off `.visible` (set elsewhere by `refreshCapFillVisibility()`), so the two compose correctly rather than fighting. Runs at every compute (`buildCapFillGroups()`) and every threshold change (`syncCapFillPlanes()`), so the fill/outline split stays live as the sliders move, not just at load.

Live-verified on Joe's own running session, the same Height Cut=5 near-ground case his screenshot showed: swapped the real fill quad's visibility off and drew the exact outline rectangle the new code computes -- buildings clearly visible through a thin boundary line instead of the overwhelming wash, confirming the fix before shipping.

Scoped to negative space's own poché (POCHE_COLOR) for now, matching what Joe actually screenshotted -- buildings' charcoal poché (BUILDING_POCHE_COLOR) uses a structurally different cap technique (real per-building geometry, not a clipped quad) and wasn't touched; the same idea could extend there if it turns out to have the same large-area problem.

v3.4.98 - 2026-09-24 - Joe (with a screenshot): "graphics can we get the shape handles to pop out more they appear a bit to transparent and are hard to see" -- they were never actually transparent (`makeHandle()` never set `transparent`/`opacity` on the cone material, both default to fully opaque). The real cause: the material was `MeshStandardMaterial`, a LIT material whose rendered brightness depends on the angle each face happens to catch the scene's hemisphere/directional lights at -- a handle facing away from the light reads dim and washed-out against a busy, already-translucent scene (the negative space shell, poché fills) even though its own opacity was always 1, which reads to the eye as "transparent" even though nothing about it actually is.

Switched to `MeshBasicMaterial` (unlit) -- renders each handle's pure, fully-saturated axis color every time, regardless of lighting angle or what's behind it, the same reasoning most 3D editors (Blender, Unity, etc.) use for gizmo handles that need to stay legible against anything in the scene. `depthTest: false` and the AXIS_COLORS themselves are unchanged -- this is a lighting-dependency fix, not a color-scheme change. `setHandleHighlight()` (the hover-color swap) needed no changes -- `MeshBasicMaterial` has the same `.color` property `MeshStandardMaterial` did.

Live-verified on Joe's own running session before shipping: swapped all 3 handle materials live, re-rendered, and confirmed visually -- flat, clearly saturated cones instead of the previous washed-out look, at the same camera angle the reported screenshot was taken from.
