v3.4.96 - 2026-09-24 - Joe: "local host live" -- prompted a real end-to-end verification of v3.4.95's fix, not just a re-read of the code. `camera.far` on the actual shipped compute pipeline came back as 6294.6 for Chelsea, matching the standalone formula almost exactly -- confirmed the fix was genuinely running, not just reasoned about. But re-running the same live diagnostic that found the original bug (dolly the camera out and measure real corner distances) against this now-live code turned up a real gap in the fix itself: v3.4.95's formula (`far = siteDiagonal * 1.5`, `maxDistance = far - 500`) only accounted for the one camera angle it happened to be tested from. Pushed the camera to a corner-opposite angle at roughly the real reachable zoom-out distance and found genuine clipping again -- real geometry sat 7640 units from the camera while `camera.far` was only 6294.6.

The actual worst case: `controls.maxDistance` bounds the camera's distance from its orbit target (near the site center), but a target-relative distance cap says nothing about distance to the FARTHEST point in the site, which can sit up to another full site diagonal beyond the target on the opposite side from wherever the camera happens to be orbiting. `far = diagonal * 1.5` had no principled relationship to that. Fixed: `siteAwareMaxDistance` now scales with the site (`Math.max(ORIGINAL_MAX_DISTANCE, siteDiagonal * 1.2)`, rather than v3.4.95's fixed multiple of `far`) and `siteAwareCameraFar` is built from `siteAwareMaxDistance`'s own real worst case -- `maxDistance + siteDiagonal + 500` (a full diagonal, not half, since the orbit target isn't guaranteed to sit exactly at the site's geometric center). Re-ran the exact camera position that clipped under v3.4.95 (7640-unit corner) against the new formula (far=9720.2 for Chelsea) -- confirmed clean, no clipping, with real margin (2080 units) to spare.

audit_deploy.js updated to match the new formula, 293/293 passing. Syntax-checked clean. This one WAS verified against the real shipped compute pipeline, live, including the specific worst-case angle that broke the previous version -- not just the formula in isolation.

v3.4.95 - 2026-09-24 - Joe (with two screenshots): "view frame: when zoomed out the model gets clipped but when zoomed it it will display but i cannot see the full context. is there anything we can do?" Traced to the site-scale camera's `far` clip plane (`ORIGINAL_CAMERA_FAR = 4000`) and its `controls.maxDistance` cap (`ORIGINAL_MAX_DISTANCE = 3500`) -- both fixed constants, applied identically regardless of which district is loaded. Chelsea/Clinton/Hudson Yards' real site diagonal (including its 395m height) is ~4191m by itself, and depending on where the camera actually sits relative to the site (not just the site's own size), real corners of the geometry end up well past `far` from the camera -- live-confirmed via `window.__NS` on Joe's own running session: at a typical zoomed-out angle, the site's far corner sat 5335 units from the camera, 1335 past the 4000 far plane. Bumping `camera.far` live to 6000 made previously-invisible geometry (a marker, ground-plane detail) reappear instantly, confirming the mechanism before writing the fix.

Fixed by computing the far-plane/max-distance budget per district instead of using a fixed constant: `siteAwareCameraFar`/`siteAwareMaxDistance` default to the `ORIGINAL_*` values (correct for the pre-compute state) and get recomputed in `computeNegativeSpace()` right after the real site bounds are known, from the real 3D diagonal (`Math.sqrt(dx² + dy² + dz²)`, height included) with the same ~500m margin logic `ORIGINAL_MAX_DISTANCE` already established (v3.0.23) -- just against the real number instead of one tuned once against whichever district was tested first. Applied live immediately unless Manhattan context is currently active (which has its own, much larger `BOROUGH_CAMERA_FAR` budget already in effect); `exitBoroughContext()` now restores these recomputed values instead of the fixed `ORIGINAL_CAMERA_FAR`/`ORIGINAL_MAX_DISTANCE` it used before. Verified the new formula against Chelsea's real DOM-synced bounds (4191m diagonal → far=6286.5, comfortably past the 5335-unit clipped corner found above) before shipping.

Syntax-checked clean, 293/293 audit checks passing (2 new). **Not yet confirmed with the actual patched compute pipeline running** -- verification was against the live-running v3.4.94 session's real geometry and the standalone formula, not the shipped code path itself; needs Joe's own reload + a genuine zoom-out to confirm end to end.

v3.4.94 - 2026-09-24 - Joe (with two screenshots): "1. if i select my neighborhood > negative space > select negative space i get cut poché building with shape handles controls. 2. select my neighborhood > negative space > draw a floor plan view > go to 3d view no poche and no shape handles. This an issue because i am getting two different results." Reproduced both paths live in Chelsea to confirm before touching anything: Path 1 (toggle Negative space directly, no box) showed the translucent void shell, a real handle, everything normal. Path 2 (draw a box in Plan, pick a direction to enter a locked elevation -- poché correctly showed there too, box-scoped -- then click Home/Perspective) landed in a genuinely inert state: `capFillToggle` disabled, `activeSectionBox` still truthy, no handles, the whole panel looking washed out.

Root cause, found by reading `resetToDefaultView()` (the real handler behind the Home/⌂ button, via `recenterCamera()`): a comment already sitting above it, dated v3.2.23, describes "(Home's own reset of the cutaway mode -- `zoomToBoroughContext()` deliberately preserves an active box-scoped cutaway)" as if this already happened. It never did -- `activeSectionBox` was cleared by exactly two places in the whole file: `cancelPlanBox()` (an in-progress, not-yet-committed draw) and `clearActiveSectionBox()` (the "Full width" button, only shown while a locked N/S/E/W elevation is active). Neither runs on the Home transition. So drawing a box, picking a direction, then hitting Home left `activeSectionBox` genuinely still set while `sectionModeAxis` went back to `null` -- exactly the combination every poché/handle visibility formula in the file (`buildingCapFillGroups.x/y.visible`'s `xFreePerspective`/`yFreePerspective` terms, `capFillGroups`' own equivalents, `refreshCutawayRowActiveState()`'s `capInert`) treats as "nothing can show," because that guard was written for the box being mid-draw/not-yet-committed, not for a real, permanent dead end with no visible way back out. The comment promising a reset was never followed up with actual code.

Fixed by calling the already-existing `clearActiveSectionBox()` at the start of `resetToDefaultView()`, rather than a bare `activeSectionBox = null` -- it also clears any stale Plan-view box outline overlay (`updatePlanBoxOutline()`), exactly the cleanup this situation needs and already written for the identical "Full width" case. `zoomToBoroughContext()` (Manhattan context toggle) is the one place preserving the box is genuinely wanted per the v3.2.23 comment -- unaffected, since the new call lives in `resetToDefaultView()` itself, not in the shared `resetSharedViewState()` both functions call.

Live-verified the mechanism before shipping (couldn't call the module-scoped fix function directly from outside, since `window.__NS`'s `activeSectionBox` is a read-only getter): reproduced Joe's exact broken state, then manually drove the SAME underlying operation via the existing "Full width" button (which already calls `clearActiveSectionBox()` for the elevation-view case) followed by Home -- confirmed `activeSectionBox` genuinely clears, `capFillToggle` re-enables, and a real handle (green dot + guide line) reappears, visually matching Path 1's baseline exactly. Since the fix calls that same function from the one remaining place it wasn't being called, this confirms the mechanism, not just the code structure. **Not yet confirmed with the actual patched code path executing** (only the equivalent manual route was tested) -- needs Joe's own reload to confirm the real Home-button click now does this itself, per the standing "local live" testing rule.

Syntax-checked clean.

**Prevention, not just the fix**: Joe's follow-up -- "this was the root of a lot of problems how do we prevent this from breaking and happening again?" -- is fair; this exact class of bug (a shared reset that a new caller silently doesn't fully participate in) already caused v3.4.69, 70, 73, and 74, each one "X forgot to reset what Home already resets," found and fixed one at a time as each was separately noticed by Joe. A fifth instance shipping is the actual signal something structural was missing, not just bad luck. Added a self-check rather than trusting memory to catch a sixth: `refreshCutawayRowActiveState()` already computes `capInert` (the one formula that decides whether poché/handles get disabled) -- `capInert` is only EVER legitimate as a brief mid-workflow state, right after drawing a box in Plan before a direction is picked (`isPlanViewActive` true). Landing here in free Perspective/Home (`isPlanViewActive` false) is never legitimate -- it means some reset path left `activeSectionBox` stale, exactly this version's own bug. Now fires a loud `console.error` + toast the instant that combination occurs, in ANY function that reaches this code path -- known today or written next month -- rather than waiting for a user to notice a washed-out panel and report it. This turns the NEXT occurrence of this bug class into an immediate, impossible-to-miss dev-time signal instead of a silent UI degradation someone has to stumble onto.

v3.4.93 - 2026-09-24 - Joe: "we need to fix the building cap / poche issue. this project has ~200 iterations is drawn out" -- after two separate live-diagnosis sessions on buildings' Y-cap poché (v3.4.91's own investigation, then a follow-up this session) turned up a real, 100%-reproducible failure with no confirmed root cause: swept 5 real Y values across Chelsea's full range and every single one showed the stencil pass rendering only 1-2 stray pixels while the real content (confirmed via `AlwaysStencilFunc` bypass) was 110-125+ pixels. Ruled out cross-axis `clearStencil()` interference (the other axis's group is genuinely hidden whenever only one locked elevation shows), scene interference (isolated the exact 2 write meshes + 1 quad actually live), and a plane-sync bug (`xClipPlaneStencil`/`yClipPlaneStencil` update symmetrically). Tried reverting just Y's write pass off `opaqueWritePass` (back to the original v3.0.38 `transparent:true`) as a quick fix -- worked at one test value (Y=-800), then failed at four others tried immediately after (Y=-1743/-300/500/1964), which is arguably worse than a clean failure: an intermittent "fix" that looks confirmed on the first check is exactly how this bug went unnoticed inside v3.4.82-91's own testing for months.

Also caught and corrected a real methodology mistake made earlier in this same session: an automated pixel-color diagnostic (matching `BUILDING_POCHE_COLOR`, a near-black charcoal, against rendered pixels with a ±10 tolerance) had been silently producing false positives -- coincidentally matching shaded/unlit building facades and background pixels that were nowhere near the actual cap, not the cap itself. That test had reported X's cap as "clean across all 5 values tested," which was wrong; redone with an unmistakable full-opacity bright-green swap (the same technique v3.4.84 used for exactly this class of legibility problem), X turned out to show zero real content at several of those same values. The corrected method is what actually found the Y failure was real and the X result was inconclusive, not decorative.

Given two sessions of chasing an intermittent, unexplained GPU stencil-state bug with no reproducible root cause, replaced the technique for buildings' X AND Y caps entirely -- the same move already proven out for Z in v3.4.91, extended to cover the "cross-section width varies with slice depth" case v3.4.91 explicitly left for the stencil technique to handle. It doesn't need to: slicing a building's own real 2D footprint polygon by the line X=value (or Y=value) via the standard even-odd scanline rule (`sliceFootprintAtLine()` -- walk every edge, record line crossings, sort, pair into intervals) gives the exact real interval(s) the cut passes through at that building, cheaply and deterministically. Combined with the building's own `[minH,maxH]` (already captured in `buildingFootprints` for the Z case), `buildBuildingXYCapGeometry()` builds one real flat rectangle per interval per building -- oriented to match negative space's own established xQuad/yQuad rotation convention (`rotateY(PI/2)` for X, `rotateX(-PI/2)` for Y) so nothing about the visible placement changes, only how it's produced. `rebuildBuildingXYCap(axis)` reuses one shared material per axis (`buildingXCapMaterial`/`buildingYCapMaterial`, carrying the same octant-restriction `clippingPlanes` the old quads used) and is called from `setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()` on every threshold change, same pattern `rebuildBuildingZCap()` already established from `setHeightCut()`. `createPlaneStencilGroup()`/`makeCapQuad()` are no longer called anywhere in `buildBuildingCapFillGroups()` -- negative space's own 3 call sites are completely untouched, this only retires the technique for buildings.

Live-verified the actual replacement algorithm against real data before shipping, not just reasoned through: since the module-scoped `buildingFootprints` array isn't reachable from `window.__NS`, reconstructed real per-building footprints directly from the live scene's own mesh geometry (convex hull of each of Chelsea's 5,258 real building base vertices -- an approximation for non-convex/L-shaped footprints, but exact for the simple rectangular majority) and ran the real `sliceFootprintAtLine`/rectangle-construction logic against it, live, in Joe's own browser. Swept the same values that broke the old technique: Y=-300 (completely dead before) now produces 10 real, correctly-positioned rectangles, confirmed visually with the same bright-green technique that caught the earlier false positive. Full sweep across both axes' real ranges (Y: -1743/-800/0/800/1964, X: -973/-500/0/700) came back with sensible nonzero counts everywhere real buildings exist, near-zero only at the genuine district edges -- no repeat of the old technique's unpredictable gaps.

`audit_deploy.js`: 3 stale v3.4.83/84 checks (asserting buildings' X/Y still called `createPlaneStencilGroup()`/`makeCapQuad()`) updated to assert the technique is gone; 1 stale v3.4.82 check (asserting a quad-position sync line that no longer exists) split and corrected; 5 new checks added for the new functions. 288/288 passing. Syntax-checked clean. **NOT yet live-verified with the actual updated file loaded** -- the verification above proved the algorithm and the full render/clipping/visibility pipeline against real data live, but Joe's running session is still on v3.4.92; needs his own reload of this file before the real, shipped code path (not the live-injected proxy) is confirmed end to end.

 -- the version line was position:fixed to the canvas's bottom-right corner, easy to miss. `header.top` and `#viewport` (the 3D canvas) are both `position:fixed` full-bleed elements, the header just layered on top via z-index -- so the simplest, most robust placement is as a genuine third line inside the header's own left-side div, right under the tagline, rather than trying to track the header's dynamic height with separate fixed-position math. Moved the markup there, changed `#footVersion` from a bare bottom-right `<div>` to a `<p>` alongside `<h1>`/`.tagline`, and swapped its CSS from `position:fixed` to a plain in-flow style matching the tagline's own look (small, muted).

This makes the header taller by one line, which mattered: two other elements used hardcoded pixel offsets tied to the OLD (shorter, 66px) header height -- the `#stats` panel (`top:calc(70px + ...)`) and the box-draw banner (`top:70px`) would otherwise now sit partly underneath the taller header. Bumped both to 86px. Checked for every other reference to the old header height or footVersion's old position before finishing -- one comment on `#controls` and one on `#navPanel` both turned out to be purely historical documentation of past fixes (describing pixel math that was true AT THE TIME, not an ongoing dependency), correctly left as-is rather than rewritten.

v3.4.91 - 2026-09-23 - Joe: "still seeing bldg shells and no caps" in Chelsea/Clinton/Hudson Yards specifically, even after the shape+trigger fixes in v3.4.88/89. This was a genuinely new bug, not a repeat of the earlier ones -- isolated it live before touching any code: merged just 50 of Chelsea's buildings (ruling out scale as a factor -- the full district has 5,258), then in a single frame bypassed the stencil test entirely (AlwaysStencilFunc showed the cap correctly, proving the geometry, camera, and quad were all fine) and switched back to the real stencil check (NotEqualStencilFunc showed nothing) -- confirming the stencil technique itself was silently producing zero passing pixels for buildings' merged geometry in this district. Checked every input that could plausibly cause that and found nothing wrong: cap-face winding (15 buildings, all correct), side-wall winding (5 faces, all correctly outward), stencil buffer availability (confirmed 8-bit, allocated), autoClearStencil (true -- fresh buffer every render), interference from the other 6 real stencil-consuming groups in the scene (hid them all, no change), and duplicate/overlapping geometry (checked bounding-box overlaps among the 50 buildings -- normal adjacent-building overlap, nothing resembling a true duplicate). No root cause found despite all of that.

Rather than keep chasing an unexplained silent failure in the stencil approach, replaced it for the Z-axis (Height Cut) case with a technique that doesn't depend on a stencil buffer at all. The insight: a Z cut's cross-section through any building is always exactly that building's own footprint outline, unchanged at every height within its own range -- a horizontal slice through a vertical extrusion never changes shape along the extrusion's own axis. That footprint shape already exists in the app, built once per building by `outerRingShape()` and fed straight into `extrudedMesh()` -- it just wasn't being kept afterward. Added `buildingFootprints`, capturing `{shape, minH, maxH}` per building/part right alongside the existing `extrudedMesh()` calls. `buildBuildingZCapGeometry(height)` filters this list to whichever buildings' own height range actually contains the given height, builds a flat `ShapeGeometry` per qualifying building at that height, and merges them -- no stencil test, no per-pixel logic, just the real buildings that are genuinely present at that height, unioned. `rebuildBuildingZCap()` swaps this geometry in on every Height Cut change (the cap's actual shape now depends on height, not just its position, so `setHeightCut()` calls it directly) and reuses one material across every rebuild rather than recreating it.

Validated the core approach live before finalizing, not just the code: approximated the same technique directly in the browser using each real building mesh's own bottom-cap vertices as a footprint stand-in, filtered by height range, merged, and rendered in bright green -- 2,394 qualifying buildings at Height Cut=18 in Chelsea, appearing exactly where expected. Confirms the underlying technique produces real, correctly-shaped, visible geometry where the stencil approach produced silent nothing.

Scope: this only replaces the Z-axis case. X/Y (locked-elevation and grazing-angle-Perspective) caps keep the stencil technique -- a vertical cut's cross-section width genuinely varies by how deep into a building the slice falls, unlike Z's fixed footprint shape, so a direct-geometry equivalent would need real per-slice computation, not a simple lookup. Negative space's own poché (all 3 axes) is unaffected -- this only touches buildings' Z cap.

`audit_deploy.js`: 4 new checks; 5 pre-existing checks (from v3.4.82/83/84/88/89) updated in place for the new construction and call counts.

v3.4.90 - 2026-09-23 - Joe: "is there a good reason why we have the two Non Manhattan and Manhattan mode? ... One view is not mimicking the other as if its another project." A real, well-evidenced concern, investigated rather than just discussed. Manhattan context turned out to not be a separate system at all -- it's the exact same scene (buildings, negative space, poché/caps, everything) with the camera repositioned further out and an outline/label overlay (`boroughsGroup`) toggled on. The actual problem: `zoomToBoroughContext()` was a hand-written entry point that separately, partially re-implemented what `resetToDefaultView()` (Home) already did -- box-draw cleanup, camera switch, rotate unlock, view-indicator update -- copied by hand instead of shared. The evidence wasn't hypothetical: four past bugs (v3.4.69, 70, 73, 74) were all exactly this pattern, "Manhattan context forgot to reset X that Home already resets," fixed one at a time as each was separately noticed.

Looking for a fifth instance of the same pattern rather than assuming the first four were the only ones -- found one, live: enter a locked N/S/E/W elevation, then toggle Manhattan context on, and the lighting stays stuck on `orthoLight` (built to face that elevation dead-on) instead of switching back to the perspective-appropriate `sun` -- only `resetToDefaultView()` ever reset `sun`/`orthoLight` visibility; `zoomToBoroughContext()` never touched it. Confirmed visually (screenshotted both states): flat, washed-out shading vs. a normal, properly-lit borough view. Live-verified the actual fix's effect too, not just the diagnosis -- manually toggled the lights on the real broken scene and confirmed the shading contrast returned to normal.

The feature itself is legitimate and deliberately designed -- real care went into it (v3.2.23's "preserve an active box-scoped cutaway through Manhattan context" is an intentional, documented decision, not an oversight; v3.4.62's "frame off the site's own size, not the borough's" likewise). Removing it would throw away real design work to fix what's actually a code-organization problem, not a feature problem.

Fix: extracted `resetSharedViewState()` -- box-draw cleanup, camera switch, rotate unlock, light reset, indicator update, the parts both callers genuinely need identically. `resetToDefaultView()` and `zoomToBoroughContext()` now both call it first, then each layers its own specific camera framing on top (site-fit + cutaway-mode reset for Home; wider clip planes + borough-scale framing + cutaway-mode PRESERVATION for Manhattan context -- `applySectionMode(null)` deliberately stays OUT of the shared function, kept only in `resetToDefaultView()`, so v3.2.23's persistence behavior survives unchanged). The next fix to "what a clean reset looks like" now only needs to be written once.

Updated 6 pre-existing audit checks (from v3.4.69/70/73/74's own history) that asserted the old, now-relocated inline code; added 3 new ones for the extracted function itself.

`audit_deploy.js`: 3 new checks, 6 updated in place.

v3.4.89 - 2026-09-23 - Joe: "building cut height poche for non Manhattan and Manhattan is not showing." A real regression from v3.4.88's own fix, found live rather than guessed: with Buildings + Negative space both on and Height Cut set to a real, engaged value (30m), `sectionModeAxis === null`, no box active -- every condition v3.4.88 added should have shown the cap -- the Z-cap stayed hidden (`parent.visible: false`, confirmed directly on both layers' quads). Toggling the Section-fill checkbox off and back on (which calls `refreshCapFillVisibility()` via its own existing wiring) fixed it immediately with nothing else touched -- proving the cap itself was already correctly built and positioned, just never told to re-check whether it should now be visible.

Root cause: v3.4.88 made the Z-cap's visibility depend on `sectionPlane.constant` (only show once Height Cut is genuinely engaged, not sitting at its neutral 0), but `setHeightCut()` -- the function that actually changes `sectionPlane.constant`, called every time the Height Cut slider or number input moves -- already called `syncCapFillPlanes()` (positions the quad) but never called `refreshCapFillVisibility()` (decides whether to show it at all). The two were never wired together, so the cap's visibility only ever caught up lazily, on the next unrelated camera-orbit or checkbox event -- not on the Height Cut change that actually mattered.

Added the missing `refreshCapFillVisibility()` call to the end of `setHeightCut()`, right after the other 3 side-effect calls that already run there on every Height Cut change.

`audit_deploy.js`: 1 new check.

v3.4.88 - 2026-09-21 - Joe: "z cut is still displaying in plan when going to draw plan region - confusing to new user not consistent." His own diagnosis was wrong, in a genuinely useful way -- confirmed with a completely fresh compute (Height Cut definitively untouched, verified at its own neutral value 0) that the same dark rectangular area appeared in the exact same spot. Not a leftover cutaway carrying over at all.

Traced to the actual cause via direct scene raycasting (not guessed): the dark area was `buildingCapFillGroups.z`'s own charcoal poché quad, genuinely rendering there, at 0.85 opacity. Two compounding bugs, found and fixed together:

1. **Wrong shape.** The quad's `restrictPlanes` included `xClipPlaneNeg`/`yClipPlaneNeg` (the other two axes' octant-cutaway planes) alongside `SITE_BOUND_PLANES`, combined under this material's default `clipIntersection` (false = AND/intersection). `buildingMat`/`negMat` themselves use `clipIntersection: true` (OR/union) for their own octant cutaway -- a single per-material flag can't express AND for the site-bound planes and OR for the octant pair at once, and the quad was combining both under the wrong one for that pair. Result: instead of matching buildingMat's own tiny-corner exclusion, the quad showed across an entire quadrant. Dropped the mismatched pair from all 3 places this pattern appeared (negative space's Z quad construction, buildings' Z quad construction, and the dynamic resync that updates negative space's quad on every view switch -- all 3 needed to match, or the resync would have silently reintroduced the bug).

2. **Wrong trigger.** Even with the shape fixed, the cap still showed at Height Cut's own neutral/reset value (0) -- because 0 sits exactly at every building's own ground level, so the stencil test finds "material crosses here" at essentially every building on the site, not a meaningful cut location at all. `buildingMat`/`negMat` never visibly clip anything at Height Cut=0 in the first place (clipIntersection's OR semantics mean the other two axes' planes rescue nearly every fragment from an inactive height plane) -- the poché quad had no equivalent "is this axis actually doing anything" check. Added `sectionPlane.constant > 0` to both layers' Z-cap visibility formulas.

Live-verified the actual fix, not just the diagnosis: patched both changes into the real running scene at the exact dark pixel and confirmed the area returned to normal -- then set a real, engaged Height Cut and confirmed the cap still correctly appears there, so this isn't just suppressing the feature.

Also worth noting for the "why do these buildings look so dark" question along the way: ruled out flipped cap-face normals (checked directly, correctly oriented) and the Plan-view light being off (confirmed active, correctly positioned overhead) as contributing causes -- this was entirely the poché quad's own bug, not a lighting or material issue.

`audit_deploy.js`: 3 new checks.

v3.4.87 - 2026-09-21 - Joe: "right off the bat: not sure why the cut section in dark in the view its should be consistent with the rest - otherwise its confusing." Reproduced directly rather than guessing: drew a box over open ground (a light, subtle pink tint, close to intended) and the same size box over a dense dark-building cluster (near-black) side by side -- same fill, wildly different apparent color depending on what's underneath.

Root cause: buildings' own roof faces already render quite dark from directly above (the same characteristic `BUILDING_POCHE_COLOR` needed a higher opacity to read against, v3.4.84) -- a flat 15%-opacity tint blended on top of an already-dark surface reads as near-black, not red. Confirmed the actual blend math before picking a fix, not just eyeballing it: at the original 0.15 opacity, even the brightest reasonable red barely moves the result over a near-black roof (44,26,28 with the old color, only 55,33,34 with a much brighter one) -- color alone physically cannot make a 15%-opacity layer "stay reddish" over near-black, only a real opacity increase can.

Asked Joe how he wanted it to look (more opaque solid fill / outline-only / brighter color) rather than picking unilaterally; he chose brighter color. Implemented both what he asked for and the opacity increase the math requires to actually deliver it: `planBoxFillMesh` raised from `0xb23a2e`/0.15 to `0xff6b57`/0.35 (the same color the outline ribbon already uses -- dark theme's own accent), which reads as 102,50,47 over that same dark building -- genuinely red now. Matched `#planBoxOverlay`'s CSS background to the same values, so the actively-drawing state and the restored/committed state still look like the same indicator, not two different ones.

`audit_deploy.js`: 1 check updated in place (the pre-existing v3.4.80 fill-color check), 1 new check for the overlay/mesh consistency.

v3.4.86 - 2026-09-20 - Joe: "local host is live buts i cannot do anything." A real regression, not vague -- confirmed via the actual browser console (`read_console_messages`, not guessed): `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'addEventListener')` at the exact line v3.4.85 added, `controls.addEventListener('change', refreshCapFillVisibility);`. It sat at top-level module scope, right after `refreshCapFillVisibility()`'s own definition, much earlier in the file's linear execution than `initScene()` (and the `controls = new OrbitControls(...)` assignment inside it) ever runs -- `controls` was still `undefined` at that point, throwing and halting the rest of the synchronous boot script entirely. That's exactly why nothing worked: empty neighborhood dropdown, dashed-out stats, blank scene -- everything that normally gets wired up after that point in the script never ran.

Root cause was placement, not logic: the two pre-existing listeners this was modeled on (`updateCompass`, `updateStreetLabelScale`) both live INSIDE `initScene()`, right after `controls` is actually assigned -- v3.4.85's version copied their reasoning but not their location. Moved the new listener to sit right alongside them, inside `initScene()`. `refreshCapFillVisibility()` itself is a hoisted function declaration, so it's safely callable from there regardless of where in the file it's defined -- only the `controls` reference needed to wait.

Checked for a second latent issue rather than assuming this was the only one: swept every other `controls.` reference in the file for the same top-level-before-assignment class of bug -- none found, all either comments or properly inside function bodies.

Not yet live-verified with a real reload (can't safely patch a page that already crashed mid-boot) -- confirmed the root cause directly against the real browser console, and the fix mirrors an already-working, established pattern exactly.

`audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks.

v3.4.85 - 2026-09-20 - Joe: "so why give the ability to see/goto a 3d view?" -- a fair challenge to v3.2.40's fix, which hid X/Y cutaway poché in free Perspective entirely to avoid a real rendering artifact (a full-site flat plane, viewed at a grazing/near-edge-on angle in free orbit, visually smears into what looks like a solid wall). That blanket hide threw out every ordinary viewing angle to avoid a problem that only actually happens at the extreme ones.

Replaced the all-or-nothing hide with a live per-angle check. `capQuadFaceOnEnough(planeNormal)` compares the camera's actual view direction to the cut plane's own normal (`|cos(angle)| > GRAZING_THRESHOLD`, 0.25 -- roughly 75° off face-on, a deliberately conservative floor inside the range v3.2.40's own screenshot showed the artifact) and returns false outright for orthographic Plan view (a vertical X/Y plane is always edge-on from directly above, not just sometimes -- no angle math needed there). Hooked to `controls.addEventListener('change', refreshCapFillVisibility)`, the same established pattern `updateCompass()`/`updateStreetLabelScale()` already use, so it re-evaluates on every orbit, not just on discrete view switches -- orbiting toward a grazing angle live-hides that plane's fill before the artifact reappears, orbiting back brings it back.

Applies to both layers identically -- negative space's own X/Y poché gains free-Perspective visibility for the first time here too, not just buildings' (which already had it, per the corrected v3.4.82 doc claim that was actually premature until now). Updated the two doc/tooltip spots that still said negative space's fill was "only visible in a locked N/S/E/W section."

Not yet live-verified -- code-reviewed against the confirmed v3.2.40 root cause.

`audit_deploy.js`: 3 new checks.

v3.4.84 - 2026-09-20 - Joe's screenshot after v3.4.83: "still seeing shell with no cut face cap & poche." Live-verified on his actual session before assuming the stencil fix hadn't taken -- it had. Swapped the real cap's color to bright green (unmistakable, bypasses any legibility question) and it appeared exactly where expected, correctly shaped, only on the truncated rooftops. The real problem: charcoal (`0x2B2E38`) at the original 0.4 opacity is too close in tone to the shaded/unlit side of building facades under this lighting -- it was rendering correctly the whole time, just blending into buildings' own dark faces instead of reading as a distinct cap. A functional success masquerading as a visual failure.

Asked Joe how to fix the contrast (lighter charcoal / higher opacity / different hue) rather than guessing; he chose higher opacity. `makeCapQuad()` gained an optional `opacity` parameter (defaulting to the original 0.4, so negative space's 3 calls -- still passing only their original arguments -- are unaffected); buildings' 3 calls now pass 0.85. Live-verified the actual visual result before finalizing, not just the parameter change: patched the real quad's opacity live and confirmed clearly distinct dark caps across the skyline, matching the original working proof-of-concept from earlier in this feature's development.

`audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks.

v3.4.83 - 2026-09-20 - Joe: "still seeing a shell w/ no charcoal cap showing cut/pouche" -- v3.4.82 shipped but genuinely didn't work, confirmed directly on his live session (checkbox correctly un-dimmed and checked, quad correctly positioned and visible, merged geometry correctly renders as real buildings when drawn plainly -- but the actual stenciled poché never appeared, even zoomed in close).

Root cause, found via a controlled live A/B test (same camera position, same merged geometry, only one material flag changed between runs): `createPlaneStencilGroup()`'s `baseMat.transparent = true` -- the exact v3.0.38 fix that makes negative space's own multi-axis poché work correctly -- reliably produces NO stencil pass at all against buildings' much larger merged geometry (205k+ vertices, vs negative space's far smaller CSG-cleaned solid), even in the single-axis case v3.0.38 was written for. Flipping it to `transparent:false` for buildings only (confirmed in the same controlled test, everything else unchanged) restored the poché immediately.

Added an `opaqueWritePass` parameter to `createPlaneStencilGroup()` rather than touching its default behavior -- negative space's own 3 call sites are unchanged (still 3 arguments, so `opaqueWritePass` is `undefined`, `transparent` still computes to `true`, byte-identical to before); buildings' 3 call sites now pass `true`.

Checked whether this reopens the ordering bug v3.0.38 fixed, rather than assuming it's safe: negative space and buildings are geometrically COMPLEMENTARY (negative space is the mold minus every building, by construction), so a stencil ray at any given (x,y) column only ever crosses one of the two geometries, never both -- buildings' now-opaque write pass (running in three.js's separate, earlier opaque list) can't leave contaminating parity in a pixel negative space's own sequence later reads. And even if it somehow did, real building geometry (normal `depthTest:true`, unlike the stencil-write meshes themselves) already occupies and would depth-occlude that same pixel, so nothing would be visible regardless. Live-verified both layers shown together afterward, not just reasoned through -- negative space's own poché still read correctly with buildings' write pass also active.

Live-verified the fix's actual effect in a controlled A/B test (isolated single test, clean camera/geometry match to the original working case) -- the broader "both layers together" combined-state screenshot later in the same long debugging session was inconclusive due to accumulated test-object debris from extensive prior live patching in that one tab, not a sign the fix itself is wrong. Recommend a fresh-tab verification once this ships before considering it fully closed.

`audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks.

v3.4.82 - 2026-09-20 - Joe: "would it be possible to cap and color w/ a poche when cutting through building in 3d views?" -- his screenshot showed exactly the gap: a Height Cut through Buildings (Manhattan context, free Perspective) leaves each truncated building hollow, no cap at all, since the existing poché/cap-fill system (`capFillGroups`, a real stencil-buffer technique from three.js's own clipping-stencil example) was built specifically against the negative-space solid.

Root cause of the gap, not a missing feature so much as a geometry mismatch: `createPlaneStencilGroup()`'s stencil technique needs exactly ONE continuous `BufferGeometry` to test against -- negative space already is that (a single CSG-computed solid), but `solidGroup` (Buildings) is thousands of separate per-building meshes. Fixed by merging every building mesh's geometry into one (`mergeGeometries`, imported from `three/addons/utils/BufferGeometryUtils.js` -- confirmed live against the actual unpkg-served file before shipping, not assumed: `mergeGeometries` is the real current export name in three@0.185.1, `mergeBufferGeometries` was the old pre-rename name and does NOT exist there), once per compute (`buildBuildingCapFillGroups()`, called alongside the existing `buildCapFillGroups(cleanedGeo)` inside `computeNegativeSpace()`, since both need `siteMinX/Y/capHeight` for quad sizing). `createPlaneStencilGroup()`/`makeCapQuad()` themselves needed zero changes -- same technique, new input.

Color: asked Joe rather than picking unilaterally, since negative space's own poché (`POCHE_COLOR`, the theme accent red) can't just be reused -- Buildings and Negative space can legitimately show together, and using the same color for both would make a building's cap and the void's cap indistinguishable. Landed on a fixed charcoal (`BUILDING_POCHE_COLOR = 0x2B2E38`), same hue family as `buildingMat`'s own base gray (`0x8a94a3`) just much darker/denser -- reads as "the same material, this is where it was cut," not an unrelated color, and stays fixed rather than theme-swapped (matching how buildingMat's own color is fixed too) -- `makeCapQuad()` grew an optional `color` param for this, opting out of `updatePocheTheme()`'s theme-sync for any non-default color passed in.

Deliberately no sky-discard on buildings' X/Y quads (negative space's own X/Y quads need it -- the mold extends to the site's max height everywhere, so a naive cap would reach that ceiling even above a short building; buildings have no such artificial ceiling, their own geometry already ends at each real roofline, so the stencil test alone produces the correct shape).

Found and fixed a real pre-existing bug while wiring visibility, not introduced by this change but newly blocking: `refreshCutawayRowActiveState()`'s Section-fill-checkbox dimming logic (`capInert = axis === null`, v3.4.39) disabled the checkbox in Plan/free perspective on the premise that poché "only ever affects a locked N/S/E/W section" -- already stale by v3.4.44 (negative space's own Z-cap has shown correctly in Plan/perspective since then) and now directly blocking THE scenario buildings' poché is for. Narrowed to the one combination where nothing could actually show: `axis === null && !!activeSectionBox`.

Also updated: the Section-fill help-modal entry, its tooltip, and EXTENDED.md's own description -- all three previously said poché was negative-space-only and locked-section-only.

Not yet live-verified beyond the import itself (confirmed the real `mergeGeometries` export directly against unpkg) -- the full visual result (merge + stencil + cap, live, on Joe's session) is still code-reviewed only.

`audit_deploy.js`: 6 new checks.

v3.4.81 - 2026-09-20 - Joe's screenshot, a full-width elevation with no box/labels involved: "streets are very thick needs to be reduced." This is the real geometry issue the v3.4.79 note flagged as worth a fresh look once labels were fixed -- and it was real, not a label artifact. Root cause: `STREET_HEIGHT` (4m, v3.0.28, deliberately non-literal against real ~15cm curbs so the ribbon has a visible vertical face at any camera angle) is applied uniformly to every street regardless of its own real-world direction -- in a side elevation, EVERY street's ribbon shows this same Z-extrusion as its vertical thickness, so once every real named street renders (v3.4.75, not just the old streetwidth>=60 set), the whole ground level reads as one continuous, uniformly-thick band, confirmed exactly matching Joe's screenshot (the band's thickness is uniform across the entire image width, which only happens when the shared constant -- not each street's own varying real pavement width -- is what's actually visible).

Cut `STREET_HEIGHT` from 4m to 1m -- a 4x reduction, still a genuine, unmistakably visible face from any angle (the original functional requirement), far less dominant in a full elevation. Label sprites (positioned at `STREET_HEIGHT + 2`) shift down proportionally, still just above the now-thinner ribbon.

Not yet live-verified -- code-reviewed against the confirmed root cause and Joe's own screenshot.

`audit_deploy.js`: 1 new check.

v3.4.80 - 2026-09-20 - Joe's second screenshot from the same round: the persistent Plan-view box outline (shown when returning to Plan with an existing box) read as barely visible next to how bold the box looked while actually being drawn. Root cause: `#planBoxOverlay` (the DOM element shown live during an actual drag) is a 2px dashed border PLUS a `rgba(178,58,46,0.15)` tinted fill -- genuinely bold. `planBoxOutlineMesh` was a bare `THREE.LineLoop` -- no fill at all, and WebGL only reliably renders line primitives at ~1px regardless of any `linewidth` set on the material (a real, well-known cross-browser/GPU limitation, not a bug in this code) -- a hairline next to the overlay's 2px+fill treatment.

Rebuilt as a real ribbon (actual triangle geometry via the existing `buildRibbonGeometry()` -- same technique the street ribbons already use, so its on-screen width is real geometry, not an unreliable line-width hint) plus a new `planBoxFillMesh` translucent fill quad echoing the overlay's own tint. A restored box now reads the same as one just drawn, not a fainter afterimage of it. Also hidden alongside the outline in the one other place that already hides the outline when a new box draw starts over an old one (v3.4.54).

`audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks.

v3.4.79 - 2026-09-20 - Joe's screenshot: inside a box-scoped elevation zoomed in close to actually read the box's own labels, giant overlapping text -- the opposite problem from v3.4.77's "labels not showing," and v3.4.77's fix (`max(90, visibleWidth * 0.045)`) didn't cover it: the 90 floor never shrinks below the ORIGINAL size, and 90 itself is far too big once the view is tighter than what it was calibrated against. Confirmed live before fixing, not guessed: reproduced Joe's exact case (drew a narrow box, entered North Elevation, zoomed to a realistic ~258-600m visible width) and it matched his screenshot exactly -- overlapping street names, illegible.

Removed the 90 floor entirely. `visibleWidth * 0.045` alone, tested live at both extremes (v3.4.77's ~7234m default-view case and this ~258-600m tight-zoom case), lands labels around 70-80px on screen either way -- a genuine constant-screen-fraction relationship, not two special cases needing two different constants. Replaced the floor with `Math.max(10, ...)`, a pure degenerate-value guard rather than a "preserve some minimum size" floor. Live-verified the fix's actual effect, not just the diagnosis: applied the corrected formula to the real running scene at the reproduced tight-zoom case and confirmed the overlap was gone.

Also worth noting for the "why are streets so thick" question from the same round: very likely the same root cause, not a separate geometry bug -- the giant overlapping label text (now fixed) was sitting directly on and around the street ribbon band, making the whole area read as a thick, undifferentiated mass. STREET_HEIGHT itself (4m, deliberately non-literal -- real curbs are ~15cm -- chosen at v3.0.28 for visibility at any camera angle) is unchanged; worth Joe's own look once this ships before touching that constant.

`audit_deploy.js`: 1 check updated in place (v3.4.77's own check, re-verified against the corrected formula -- same "superseded in place" pattern used at v3.4.67).

v3.4.78 - 2026-09-20 - Joe's mobile screenshot: the neighborhood dropdown list cuts off mid-option ("Mi..." for Midtown), nowhere near its own `max-height:260px`/`overflow-y:auto` cap (v3.4.5). Root cause: `positionList()` always opened the list UPWARD -- `bottom` anchored just above the button, growing toward the top of the viewport -- with no check for whether there was actually 260px of room above the button to grow into. On a short/mobile viewport with the button positioned high up, the list's own top edge lands above y=0: genuinely off-screen, not just scrolled-out-of-reach, since a `position:fixed` element clipped by the viewport edge has no page scroll to bring it back -- the internal `overflow-y:auto` only governs scrolling WITHIN the box's own drawn bounds, which is exactly the part that was missing.

Confirmed the exact mechanism with real numbers before fixing, not just reasoning about it: simulated the old formula against a cramped case (500px viewport, button around the middle) and got `top: -46` -- genuinely negative, off-screen. Fixed by measuring real space above vs below the button each time the list opens, picking whichever side has more room (the same direction-flip a native `<select>` or any well-behaved dropdown already does), and capping `max-height` to what that side actually has, floored at 120px so even a very cramped viewport still shows a few real options. Verified the new logic against three cases: the original common case (button near the bottom of a tall viewport -- opens above, full 260px, unchanged from today); button near the middle of a short viewport (now correctly opens below instead of clipping); button near the top of a short viewport (opens below with room to spare). Tried to reproduce the exact pixel scenario live via `resize_window`, but it didn't actually change the real viewport in this environment (stayed at 1699x918 regardless of the size requested) -- verified the underlying positioning math directly instead, which doesn't depend on it.

`audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks.

v3.4.77 - 2026-09-20 - Joe clarified his v3.4.76 report: "the labels are not showing" -- not specifically avenues, labels in general. Live-verified precisely rather than guessing further: isolated individual label sprites one at a time (hid everything else in the scene, zoomed the camera right to each), and every one -- avenue or cross street -- rendered perfectly crisp, correctly positioned, correct texture. No rendering bug. Confirmed instead a genuine scale problem: the 90m world-width chosen at v3.1.9 was sized for close-up, already-zoomed-into-a-site viewing, reasonable when Major streets only showed a handful of streetwidth>=60 segments near the site. Since v3.4.75 shows every real named street across the whole district, the DEFAULT Plan/Perspective view (before any box is drawn) has to fit the whole ~1-2km district on screen, where a 90m label is under 2% of visible width -- legible up close (confirmed still fine in a drawn-box elevation, matching Joe's own earlier screenshot showing plenty of readable, if cluttered, text), imperceptible zoomed out.

Added `updateStreetLabelScale()`: computes the current view's real visible width (orthographic: frustum width / zoom; perspective: `2 * distance * tan(fov/2) * aspect`) and sizes every label to `max(90, visibleWidth * 0.045)` -- floors at the original 90m so anything at today's typical scale (a drawn-box elevation) is completely unchanged, grows for wider views so the default whole-district view actually shows readable text. Tuned the 0.045 multiplier empirically, live, not guessed once and shipped: first tried 0.025 (doubles labels to ~180m in Midtown's ~7234m-wide default view) -- still not legible on screen. 0.045 (~325m there) was the value that actually read clearly (Park Ave, Lexington Ave, 5 Ave, Rockefeller Plz, Union Sq all legible in the resulting screenshot) -- confirmed before finalizing the number that shipped. Called from `updateStreetLabelVisibility()` (covers every discrete view-switch call site it already has) and from a new `controls.addEventListener('change', ...)`, same established pattern the nav compass (`updateCompass`) already uses, so manual scroll-wheel zoom/pan within a view stays responsive too, not just switching between N/S/E/W/Plan/Home.

Distinct from and complementary to v3.4.76: that version controls WHICH labels show (box-scoped clutter reduction), this one controls how big the ones that do show actually are (default-view legibility). Both needed -- neither alone would have fixed what Joe was seeing.

Live-verified both the diagnosis AND the fix's actual effect directly on Joe's localhost:8888 (still v3.4.75, pre-fix): confirmed individual labels render correctly when isolated (ruling out a rendering bug), then live-patched the real running scene with the exact sizing formula at increasing multipliers until the default Midtown Plan view was genuinely legible, before finalizing 0.045 as the shipped value.

`audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks.

v3.4.76 - 2026-09-20 - Joe's screenshot: with every real named street now shown (v3.4.75), a box-scoped South Elevation was covered in street-name text from all over the district -- "the view is all text." Root cause: `buildStreetsLayer()` builds one label per unique street name across the WHOLE district's data, once, at load time -- with the old `streetwidth>=60` filter (topped out around 26 names) that was tolerable; at 130-240 names per district it's not.

Added `updateStreetLabelVisibility()`: when a box is actually scoping the current view (same condition `sectionModeNote` already uses for its "Cropped to the box you drew" text -- `sectionModeAxis !== null && activeSectionBox`, extended to also cover a box-scoped Plan view via `isPlanViewActive`), only labels within the box's bounds (+60m padding, so a label whose street continues slightly past the edge doesn't disappear right at the boundary) stay visible; every other label hides. No box scoping the view -> every label shows, same as before. Deliberately labels only -- the street ribbons (ordinary Mesh geometry, not Sprite) are untouched, since Joe's complaint was specifically the text.

Called from the one place that already knows all three relevant pieces of state after every view-mode change (`applySectionMode()`, which nav clicks/`applyPlanBoxDirection()`/`resetToDefaultView()`/`clearActiveSectionBox()` all funnel through), plus once right after labels are first built.

Not yet live-verified -- code-reviewed against the reported symptom.

`audit_deploy.js`: 2 new checks.

v3.4.75 - 2026-09-20 - Joe: "the streets and avenues need some work, they are not complete and there was a decision in the beginning that does not apply anymore." Traced it exactly: the `streetwidth>=60` ("avenue-width and up") filter dates to v3.0.26, when Major streets was a single citywide-then-trimmed embedded payload and a tight width cutoff kept that payload small. Since v3.1.1 moved to small per-district `streets.json` files, that reason stopped applying -- the threshold itself just carried forward unchanged across all 12 districts for 3+ years anyway. It had been flagged once before, at District 6 (v3.2.9-era changelog entry): Fifth/Park/Madison/Lexington Ave -- unquestionably major avenues -- are narrower than 60ft in this exact dataset (54/44/46/50ft respectively) and were silently excluded, noted then as "a real, deliberate design question for later" and never revisited until now.

Asked Joe how the new rule should work (drop the filter / lower the threshold / hand-picked exception list); he chose dropping it entirely -- every real named street.

Re-fetched all 12 districts live (Socrata `inkn-q76z`, `boroughcode='1'`, `full_street_name IS NOT NULL`, no width filter), same real-polygon-clip method as every prior district build (segment midpoint within ~380m of the district's real boundary, established v3.1.7). Named-street counts jumped everywhere -- District 5 (Midtown) alone went from 6 to 174, now correctly including Fifth/Park/Madison/Lexington. Verified zero real streets were lost in the process (checked every old name against the new set across all 12 districts before shipping) -- a batch of apparent "losses" during that check turned out to be the raw dataset's own double-space quirk in cardinal-prefixed numbered-street names ("W  30 ST"), not missing data; normalized to single spaces (767 names across all districts) since the old embedded data never had that artifact and it would have rendered as a visible double space in labels.

Fetched via a direct Claude-in-Chrome connection to Joe's browser (data.cityofnewyork.us isn't reachable from this project's build sandbox) -- built a reusable `__buildDistrictStreetsAll()` covering the real-boundary fetch, the bbox-then-real-polygon clip, and per-district output for all 12 districts in one live session, packaged as a single combined JSON and downloaded once rather than needing Joe to upload 12 separate files. Joe uploaded the resulting `all-districts-streets.json`, split into each `data/district-N/streets.json` here.

**Known tradeoff, flagged not silently fixed**: dropping the width filter also surfaces non-street named features from the same dataset -- park drives, bike paths, bridge/tunnel access ramps (e.g. "W 47 STREET BIKE PATH", "CENTRAL PARK ENTRANCE 8 AVE") -- that the old width cutoff had incidentally screened out too. Left in per Joe's explicit "every named street," not re-filtered without asking.

Updated the code comment above `buildStreetsLayer()`, AUDIT.md's Major streets entry, EXTENDED.md's Major streets feature description and "adding a district" step 7, and the Major streets tooltip/help-modal copy -- all previously said "avenue-width and up, ≥60ft."

v3.4.74 - 2026-09-19 - Joe asked what else looked like a roadblock after v3.4.73's Home fix. Went looking for every other place the same pattern could recur -- any control that switches out of the box-draw workflow without going through `cancelPlanBox()` -- rather than waiting for another report. Found two:

1. **Manhattan context** (`zoomToBoroughContext()`): identical bug to v3.4.73's Home case, different trigger -- switches to the wide-framed perspective camera regardless of what was active before (deliberate since v3.0.42), but never checked `boxDrawMode`. Toggling it on mid-box-draw left the "Box drawn -- view it from:" panel (or the "Drag a box... Cancel" banner, if no box was drawn yet) floating over an unrelated borough-scale view.
2. **Top compass N/S/E/W clicked mid-draw, before a box exists** (`goToDirection()`): the compass stays visible during the "Drag a box... Cancel" banner stage (v3.4.66 only hides it once a box is actually drawn and the picker panel shows) -- clicking N/S/E/W there falls through to `setOrthogonalView(direction)` directly, switching into a locked elevation while leaving `boxDrawMode` stuck true: banner still showing, cursor still crosshair, "Draw section box" button still reading "Drag a box…".

Both fixed the same way as v3.4.73: `if (boxDrawMode) cancelPlanBox();` before the function proceeds. Checked `navPlan` (re-clicking Plan mid-draw) too -- lower priority/likely harmless since you're already staying in Plan, left alone.

Not yet live-verified, same standing caveat as v3.4.69/70/73.

v3.4.73 - 2026-09-19 - Joe's screenshot: draw a box in Plan, press Home (3D view), "completely lost." Root cause: `resetToDefaultView()` (what Home calls) switches to the free-orbit perspective camera and fits to the whole site, but never touched anything box-draw-related -- `planBoxPanel` ("Box drawn -- view it from:") kept floating on screen, the top compass's N/S/E/W stayed hidden (v3.4.66 hides them the moment that panel shows), and the actual 3D view had jumped to a full-site perspective with no relationship to the box just drawn and no visible way to pick a direction.

Fixed with one line: `if (boxDrawMode) cancelPlanBox();` at the top of `resetToDefaultView()` -- `cancelPlanBox()` is the exact existing cleanup for "box-draw workflow ending without a direction picked" (already used by its own Cancel button), safe to call unconditionally since it's a no-op beyond restoring state when `boxDrawMode` is already false. Because `resetToDefaultView()` is shared (v3.4.6: also called when `loadData()` or `computeNegativeSpace()` finish), this also covers switching neighborhoods or recomputing mid-box-draw, not just the Home button specifically.

Not yet live-verified -- code-reviewed against the confirmed cause (same standing caveat as v3.4.69/70; still haven't added `controls` to the `__NS` debug hook for live-patching).

v3.4.70 - 2026-09-19 - Same screenshot as v3.4.69, second half of the same underlying pattern: Joe's screenshot also showed the View compass's W button and the "West Elevation" corner label stuck active after switching into Manhattan context, even though the camera had already switched back to perspective. Root cause: `updateCurrentViewIndicator()` (v3.4.33) is the one function that syncs both -- `resetToDefaultView()` already calls it for the same reason v3.4.69's fix cited ("undo it here same as recenterCamera() always has"), `zoomToBoroughContext()` never did.

v3.4.69 - 2026-09-19 - Joe: "when you are in elevation view and select Manhattan context you cannot do anything but pan around." Root cause found via code reading, not guessed: `setOrthogonalView()` sets `controls.enableRotate = false` (v3.2.34, keeps a true N/S/E/W/Plan elevation locked to its axis). `zoomToBoroughContext()` already switches back to the perspective camera (`activeCamera = camera`, unconditional since v3.0.42) but never touched `enableRotate` -- so a locked-off elevation's rotate lock silently carried straight into Manhattan context, leaving pan (and zoom) the only working interaction. `exitBoroughContext()` never surfaced this, since it routes through `recenterCamera()` -> `resetToDefaultView()`, which already resets `enableRotate` -- the bug only shows up while staying inside context, never on the way out, which is exactly the case Joe hit.

Fixed with one line: `controls.enableRotate = true;` right alongside the existing camera switch, same place `resetToDefaultView()` does it for the same reason. Manhattan context is meant to be a free-orbit view regardless of what was active before it.

Not yet live-verified on Joe's machine -- held per standing rule, code-reviewed root cause only (see AUDIT.md's own note on why that's not sufficient to call resolved on its own).

Already correctly labeled `'Perspective'` internally (`VIEW_LABELS`, v3.4.33) -- just the visible tooltip never matched. Picked "Home (3D view)" over "3D view" (keeps the icon's usual Home meaning) or "Recenter to 3D view" (keeps the fit-to-bounds half too) -- Joe's choice.

v3.4.65 - 2026-09-19 - Joe: "also after a section box is drawn i cannot select the box edges to drag." Reproduced live before touching anything: drew a box, left it at the "Box drawn -- view it from:" stage (no direction picked yet), tried to drag an edge -- it silently started a brand new box draw instead, discarding the one just drawn.

Root cause: v3.4.59's edge-drag only ever looked at `activeSectionBox`, the COMMITTED box a direction has already been picked for. The box you've just drawn but haven't picked a direction for yet is `pendingPlanBox`, a different variable entirely, which `hitTestBoxEdge()` never checked. Made worse by `boxDrawMode` staying true through that whole awaiting-direction stage (it only goes false on Cancel or a direction pick) combined with the pointerdown handler's `if (!boxDrawMode)` wrapper around the edge-check -- so an edge-grab attempt there fell straight through to the new-drag-start branch instead of ever being tested.

`hitTestBoxEdge()` now selects `boxDrawMode ? pendingPlanBox : activeSectionBox` -- one function covering both stages, correctly returning null while boxDrawMode is true but nothing's been drawn yet. The pointerdown handler's `if (!boxDrawMode)` wrapper around the edge-check is gone -- the check always runs now, since hitTestBoxEdge() itself decides which box is relevant. `pendingPlanBox` doesn't have a 3D outline mesh the way `activeSectionBox` does (`planBoxOutlineMesh`) -- its only visual is the screen-space CSS overlay, normally driven by raw pointer coordinates during a fresh drag. Editing its world bounds via an edge-drag needed the reverse: a new `worldToScreenXY()` helper (the deliberate algebraic inverse of the existing `screenToWorldGround()`, same rect and camera) feeding a new `updatePlanBoxOverlayFromWorldBounds()`, so the overlay stays in sync with the world-space bounds being dragged. Hover-cursor feedback (ew-resize/ns-resize) now also fires during the awaiting-direction stage, and preserves the crosshair cursor `setBoxDrawMode()` already sets as its non-edge fallback while boxDrawMode is on, rather than stomping it back to a plain pointer between edges.

v3.4.64 - 2026-09-19 - Joe: "if i zoom into an area and select draw section box the frame zooms out." Traced against the code, not just the report: `setOrthogonalView('plan')` has always framed on the whole site (`currentTargetAndSpan()`'s own deliberate v3.0.42 default), regardless of whatever the perspective camera happened to be zoomed into beforehand -- and `setBoxDrawMode(true)` always calls it with no way to say otherwise. A past live-verify fix (already in the file, its own comment right above this one) had addressed the "no control" half of this same report -- re-enabled pan/zoom after the switch -- but the reset-to-full-site itself was treated as expected/unavoidable at the time. It's reasonable for a true N/S/E/W elevation (that comment's own reasoning: an elevation is normally about seeing the whole thing flat) but wrong specifically for entering Plan to draw a box, where the entire point of having zoomed in was to draw a box around that exact area.

`setOrthogonalView()` now takes an optional second parameter, `overrideTargetSpan`, which replaces `currentTargetAndSpan()`'s always-full-site result when supplied -- every other existing caller passes nothing and is completely unaffected, including the deliberate default this doesn't touch. `setBoxDrawMode(true)` supplies it, but only when actually coming from the perspective camera (`activeCamera === camera` -- if boxDrawMode somehow gets toggled on while already in an orthographic view, there's no perspective FOV/distance to convert from, so it falls through to the original full-site framing unchanged): converts the perspective camera's current distance-to-target and FOV into an equivalent orthographic half-height (`2 * dist * tan(vFOV/2)`, floored at 100m so an extreme close-up can't produce a degenerate frustum), and captures `controls.target` directly as the new center. Not a pixel-for-pixel match between two different projection types -- not really possible, and not the goal -- close enough that "roughly where and how zoomed in I just was" reads correctly, which is what the report was actually about.

v3.4.63 - 2026-09-19 - Follow-up on the v3.4.60 audit's own flagged-but-not-fixed gap: `resetCutaway()` set `xThreshold`/`yThreshold` directly, duplicating everything `setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()`/`setHeightCut()` already do (slider value, `updateValueInput`, `syncCapFillPlanes`/`updateCutLinePlanes`/`syncHandlePositions`) while missing the one thing those three canonical setters have that this hand-rolled version didn't: `refreshActiveSectionProfile()` when the reset axis matches the active section. Two code paths doing the same thing is exactly the shape of bug that caused v3.4.51's original gap in the first place -- routing through the real setters means any future fix to them applies here automatically, no second edit to remember.

Also a real behavior improvement, not just a bypass fix: resetting to a flat 0 made sense in the old octant-cutaway-only world, but means nothing relative to a drawn box -- 0 could easily sit outside the box's own bounds. When a box is active, Reset now re-centers X/Y on that box's own center (the same value `applyPlanBoxDirection()` already sets on a fresh direction pick) instead of an arbitrary site-wide default with no relationship to what's on screen. No box active: still resets to 0, unchanged.

v3.4.62 - 2026-09-19 - Joe: "Ok go ahead fix camera" -- picking the camera-framing fix over dimming the cutaway controls, for the Manhattan-context zoom issue found a couple sessions back (site becomes imperceptible against the full-island view, making working cutaway tools look broken).

`zoomToBoroughContext()`'s `span` (the distance the camera backs off to fit everything in the initial view) was derived from `boroughSize` -- Manhattan's own ~9km extent -- which is exactly what put the camera far enough back that a district-scale site, and any cutaway edit on it, became sub-pixel. `controls.maxDistance` (still `BOROUGH_MAX_DISTANCE`, 18000) is untouched and comfortably larger than any realistic `siteSize*5` framing distance, so a normal scroll-out still reaches the old full-island view for anyone who wants that overview back -- this changes the default framing, not what's reachable.

v3.4.59 - 2026-09-02 - Joe: "should we be able to drag the 4 edges of an existing section box?" -- discussed the tradeoffs, agreed it was worth building, then: "ok build it." Until now the only way to change a box at all was to draw a brand new one from scratch.

Same drag-a-plane technique the X/Y/Height cutaway handles already use (screenToWorldGround() feeding a live bound update), applied to the 4 edges of `activeSectionBox` instead of the single-plane thresholds. Deliberately edits the COMMITTED box -- the one `planBoxOutlineMesh` already draws -- not a box mid-draw; the two states never overlap, since the outline (and so these new edge-grab zones) are hidden for the entire time a new box is being drawn (v3.4.54's own fix). `hitTestBoxEdge()` checks proximity to each of the 4 edges, gated on `activeSectionBox && isPlanViewActive && !boxDrawMode`, with a grab tolerance scaled to the model's own span (1.5% of it, floored at 4m) -- same scaling philosophy `updateHandleScale()` already uses for the cutaway cones, since a fixed meter tolerance that feels right on a small site is unusably tiny on a 4,000m+ district. Dragging an edge updates only that one bound, clamped against a `BOX_EDGE_MIN_SIZE` floor so it can never collapse or invert the box, and calls `updatePlanBoxOutline()` on every move so the outline redraws live rather than only jumping once on release. Hover feedback (ew-resize/ns-resize cursor) is gated the same way, so it costs nothing outside Plan or without a box active.

Resizing doesn't need its own profile/threshold refresh -- `activeSectionBox` is what `getSectionRanges()` already reads live, and v3.4.51's existing out-of-range guard re-centers the X/Y cutaway threshold automatically the next time a direction is picked, if the resize moved it outside the new bounds.

Confirmed exactly what the screenshot showed: `planBoxOutlineMesh` (the persistent world-space outline of the last-committed box, v3.4.37) only ever hides when `!activeSectionBox` -- and starting a new drag never touches `activeSectionBox` at all, since that only updates once `applyPlanBoxDirection()` commits a direction for the NEW box. So the old outline sat there, unchanged, through the entire new draw, stacked on top of the new box's own drag-preview overlay with nothing distinguishing which was which.

v3.4.53 - 2026-09-02 - Joe: "ok lets try option B" -- after a deliberately small, one-block box still showed poché inside the outline on all four elevations at once, ruling out box size as the variable and confirming Option A (a true zero-width single-plane section) essentially never lands exactly on most buildings regardless of how the box is drawn.

Implemented as a genuinely separate mechanism from POCHE_ROOF_DISCARD, not a repurposing of it -- conflating the two was a real risk here given how hardened that constant already is. POCHE_ROOF_DISCARD controls the WASH's own top edge (must stay false -- the flat mass's ceiling never hugs a roofline, that's the settled v3.4.12 decision, untouched). The new POCHE_BUILDING_DISCARD controls something orthogonal: how tall a BUILDING CUTOUT is allowed to be. A new uSkyBuildingDiscardOn uniform, same off-by-default/per-quad-applySkyConfig pattern as uSkyRoofDiscardOn, adds a second, independent GLSL discard test reading the same uSkyHeightTex/localMaxH the outline already surveys -- but in the opposite direction (z < localMaxH, carving a hole below the profile height, vs. roofDiscard's z > localMaxH + margin, capping the wash above it). Wherever the elevation profile says a building exists at a column, that column now gets a hole up to the real surveyed height, regardless of whether the true single-plane cut (xClipPlaneStencil/yClipPlaneStencil, still fully intact) happens to intersect it there. The old stencil-based hole is now a strict subset of the new, wider one -- additive, not replaced, so this can be reverted on its own without touching the single-plane mechanism.

Stated plainly, the trade-off Joe is accepting: the X/Y cutaway slider still drags and the real single plane underneath still moves, but the cutout is no longer sized off that plane -- so moving the slider no longer visibly changes which buildings show as holes. That's the literal cost of "show everything in the box" over "show one true slice," same tradeoff laid out before he chose B.

v3.4.52 - 2026-09-02 - Joe, on the S elevation, after N/S came back correctly for the first time: two small arrows pointing at the same kind of gap as before -- much smaller now, but still there. Same mechanism I'd already found and flagged a few messages back (never implemented, got pulled into the N/S investigation): `buildSectionProfile()` divides the plotted range into a fixed 512 bins regardless of how wide that range actually is. Checked live on his host at the time -- a 1158m-wide box came out to 2.26m per bin. A building edge that doesn't land on a bin boundary rounds to the nearest ~2m, enough to misalign a setback edge by a visible sliver without being a repeat of either the granularity issue (v3.4.49) or the single-plane-vs-elevation issue (v3.4.50).

Bumped the bin count 512 -> 2048 on both `buildSectionProfile()` (the real, slab-based section pass) and `buildAxisHeightProfile()` (the citywide envelope used for the very first render before any section view is entered, so that pass is equally precise too). Straight resolution increase to an existing DataTexture/array pipeline -- no logic change, negligible cost since it's a 1D texture.

Also confirmed live, not just re-asserted: Joe asked "what happened to the streets toggle" a second time. Nothing changed there across any of tonight's versions -- `viewStreets`/"Major streets" is still exactly where it's always been, inside the collapsed "Display" accordion. v3.4.51 - 2026-09-01 - Joe: "this happened again" on N/S, right after F5 had appeared to fix it last time. That was the actual tell -- filed under the old GPU shader-compile timing race originally, but a race wouldn't reliably clear itself on reload and then reliably recur; something deterministic clears on reload and persists otherwise. Dug in live against his actual v3.4.50 host instead of pointing at F5 again.

Traced it to `goToDirection()` -- the persistent compass N/S/E/W buttons, separate from the box-direction picker's own boxViewN/S/E/W buttons. `applyPlanBoxDirection()` (the picker) has always called `setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()` to center the cut threshold on the box before switching views. `goToDirection()`'s direct path, used whenever a box is already active and you just click a compass direction on a DIFFERENT axis than the one currently showing (was on W, click N), never did -- it calls `setOrthogonalView()` straight through. If that axis's threshold had never been touched this session it just sits at its literal init value (`let xThreshold = 0, yThreshold = 0;`), and v3.4.50's narrowed slab (±`SECTION_SLAB_HALF_WIDTH`, down from the box's own full depth) means a threshold sitting anywhere outside the box now finds zero buildings -- a genuinely empty profile, matching Joe's own diagnostic snippet from two versions ago exactly (`profileNonZeroBins: 0`). The old wide box-depth slab used to paper over a stray default threshold almost by accident; narrowing it in v3.4.50 (Option A, at Joe's own direction) is what turned a harmless leftover value into a real empty-view bug. F5 "fixed" it before only because a full reload resets both thresholds to 0 AND clears `activeSectionBox`, forcing a fresh box + a direction pick through the picker -- the one path that was already correct.

Fixed in `setOrthogonalView()`: before the axis actually switches, if the incoming direction's threshold is outside the active box's own bounds, it's re-centered via the same `setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()` the picker already uses. Scoped to out-of-range only, so a manually-adjusted, still-valid threshold from an earlier visit to that axis is left alone -- flipping E/W back and forth keeps whatever position you'd set, this only steps in for the case that was producing a blank view.

v3.4.50 - 2026-09-01 - Joe kept sending screenshots of poché showing inside the red skyline outline and I kept fixing the wrong layer of it -- v3.4.49's per-part granularity fix was real but not the dominant cause. Traced it one level deeper: the poché cutout (`xClipPlaneStencil`/`yClipPlaneStencil`) has always been a true single-plane section tied to the X/Y cutaway slider, going back to before "Draw section box" existed -- `SECTION_SLAB_HALF_WIDTH = 2` (meters). But when the box feature was added (v3.4.0-v3.4.13), it widened the red outline's own slab to the box's FULL DEPTH so the outline could preview the whole block's silhouette -- and nobody carried the cutout mechanism along with it. So the outline quietly became a full-depth elevation projection while the cutout stayed a 2m-wide true section, and the gap between them was just... however tall the nearest building not exactly on the plane happened to be.

Laid out the tradeoff for Joe as two options with a visual comparison (three small elevation panels: current mismatch, Option A, Option B) rather than picking one and hoping: Option A keeps the true single-plane section and trims the outline back down to match it (buildings off the current plane just don't show until you slide to them); Option B keeps the full-depth elevation preview and widens the cutout to match it (every building always shows, but the X/Y slider stops meaning anything physical). Joe: "proceed with option A - True Section."

Fixed in `getSectionRanges()`: both the axis-x and axis-y box-active branches no longer compute `halfSlab` from `activeSectionBox`'s own extent -- they now always use the fixed `SECTION_SLAB_HALF_WIDTH` margin around the current `xThreshold`/`yThreshold`, identical to the no-box (pre-v3.4.0) behavior. `plotLo`/`plotHi` still crop to the drawn box in both branches -- the box's other job (screen-horizontal cropping, saving a footprint, driving the direction picker) is completely untouched. Dragging the X/Y cutaway slider still moves the section live, same as always; the outline just now reports only what's genuinely at that plane instead of surveying the whole box.

v3.4.49 - 2026-09-01 - Joe kept sending sketches and screenshots saying poché was visible INSIDE the red skyline outline, and after several rounds of me measuring the wrong thing (pixel colors, which matched -- the wash itself was never bleeding into anything), he zoomed in and it was obvious: for stepped/setback buildings, the blue cutout stopped well short of the red outline's height, leaving a real gap of poché between them. Different bug from the toggle-carryover fixed in v3.4.48, and also not POCHE_ROOF_DISCARD (confirmed live: that stays off for the poché quads, unchanged, correct).

Traced with a live window.__NS scene inspection first: negativeMesh and solidGroup were both confirmed hidden, so the blue cutout comes entirely from capFillGroups' own stencil geometry -- which clips the real per-PART Manifold solid (see loadData()'s partsByBuilding loop, one ExtrudeGeometry + one CSG solid per building PART, each with its own footprint and height). Then in the data pipeline: buildBuildingBBoxCache(), which feeds buildSectionProfile() (the red skyline line's own data source), only ever looped buildingsGeo.features -- the coarse WHOLE-BUILDING footprint with one single height value. For a tower-on-podium building, that painted the podium's full width at the tower's height in the profile -- exactly the gap in Joe's sketch. The real solid never had that height across the full podium width to begin with; it was always the profile line overshooting, not the poché under-filling.

Fixed at the source: buildBuildingBBoxCache() now joins partsGeo the same way loadData() already does (building_id -> properties.id) and pushes one bbox entry per PART (that part's own footprint, that part's own height) instead of one bbox for the whole building.
