What actually happened at v3.4.43: Joe wrote "the project is negative space...the space that forms around the buildings outline. this is not doing it," and that was read as a request to make the fill conform to each individual roofline -- reversing the v3.4.12 design without first checking whether it contradicted an already-settled decision. It did, directly. That's a real process failure: conversation search exists specifically to catch this, and it wasn't used until Joe flagged the fill as wrong a second time and pointed back to the sketch.

Reverted cleanly: `uSkyRoofDiscardOn` back to `0.0` for the poché quads (both places it's set, plus the shader discard-test comment and the uniform's own declaration comment, which had been rewritten at v3.4.43 to describe the reversed behavior as current and needed correcting too so they don't mislead the next read). negativeMesh (the ghost shell, used in Plan/perspective) is unaffected either way -- it was never part of v3.4.43's change and keeps its own real roofline discard, which is a different, correct design for a different context (a walkthrough view, not a section fill).

`audit_deploy.js`: 136/136 (stale v3.4.43 check replaced, 1 new check for the restored behavior). APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.46.

v3.4.45 - 2026-08-28 - Three more screenshots of the same class of issue -- E/W correct, N/S showing plain buildings with no roofline outline and no poché fill at all. Joe: "local live same issue i don't know what to do."

A real change of approach, not another patch for a specific trigger. Every fix in this saga so far (v3.4.11, v3.4.26, v3.4.27, v3.4.30, v3.4.41, v3.4.42, v3.4.44) was a ONE-TIME re-assertion, timed to run after some specific `renderer.compile()` call finished. But `compile()`'s own documentation promises synchronous completion, and this session already found empirically that it doesn't always deliver that -- the exact same reproduction succeeded once and failed once, back to back, on the same live tab, during the v3.4.41 investigation. A one-time write can always theoretically race a one-time compile, no matter how carefully the two are ordered or how many extra deferred retries get stacked on top of each other -- which is exactly why this kept recurring in new shapes after every previous fix.

So: stop trying to win a race with a smarter delay, and instead never let a stale value survive more than one rendered frame. New `enforceSectionRenderState()`, called every single frame from inside `animate()` itself (not once per view change): force-corrects `negativeMesh`/`zCutLineMesh`/`capFillGroups.z` visibility to what a locked N/S/E/W section should always have, and re-calls `syncSectionSkyUniforms()` fresh every frame. That function is cheap -- property writes plus one `getSectionRanges()` call, no texture rebuilding, no allocations worth mentioning -- negligible at 60fps. Whatever the browser's GPU driver is doing underneath, and whenever it actually finishes, the very next frame simply overwrites whatever was there with the correct values again. There is no longer a specific moment this needs to win against -- the whole category of "a deferred write landed on a uniforms object that got replaced before it took effect" closes structurally, not probabilistically.

Live verification was not possible for this round -- Claude in Chrome was disconnected (Joe was mid-restart of his system) by the time this fix was ready to test. Stated plainly: the mechanism is sound by construction (every individual piece it enforces is a previously live-verified correct behavior, now applied continuously instead of once), but the actual patched code has not been re-confirmed against a live failure this time.

v3.4.43 - 2026-08-28 - Joe, after the console diagnostic came back with correct uniforms (`uSkyDiscardOn:1`, `uSkyCropOn:1`) on a screenshot that still looked wrong: "the project is negative space...the space that forms around the buildings outlines. this is not doing it." Toggled Section fill (poché) off in a follow-up screenshot -- the exact same flat wash stayed on screen either way, which was the real tell that this was never the v3.4.30/41/42 uniform-sync mechanism at all.

Live-checked `negativeMesh.visible` directly first, since a flat wash ignoring the real roofline is exactly what the ghost shell looks like in an elevation -- confirmed correctly `false` (v3.4.36 holding). So the flat fill was coming from the poché quad itself, fully on-uniform, fully cropped to the box, and still flat. Found why: `uSkyRoofDiscardOn` -- the uniform that makes the fill discard above each column's real local roofline -- has been hardcoded to `0.0` for the poché quads since v3.4.12, by original design ("the filled-mass element, always full-height, not gated by each building's own local roofline," matching a sketch Joe had provided at the time). `negativeMesh` (the ghost shell) was never subject to this -- it already discarded correctly, which is exactly why nobody had noticed: the ghost shell in Plan/perspective always looked right, only the poché quads used in elevations had this flat-fill behavior, quietly, for the entire life of the feature. Every uniform-sync bug chased since v3.4.28 was real and independently confirmed, but none of them were this -- this was always-on, deterministic, by-design behavior that just happened to look similar enough to the timing bugs' symptom (an oversized/flat block) to keep getting misread as the same thing.

Live-verified before touching code: flipped `uSkyRoofDiscardOn` to `1.0` directly on Joe's exact reported box/view and watched the fill immediately start stepping down between real buildings, with genuine gaps at real open sky -- confirmed on both a Y-axis quad (N/S) and an X-axis quad (E/W) separately, and confirmed the poché toggle correctly shows/hides this corrected fill.

`applySkyConfig()`'s hardcoded `s.uSkyRoofDiscardOn.value = 0.0` is now `1.0` -- the poché quads use the exact same roofline-conforming discard `negativeMesh` already did, so the void now reads consistently everywhere it's shown, not correct in Plan/perspective and flat in elevations. Every comment describing the old flat-fill behavior as current design (at the uniform's declaration and at the shader discard test itself) rewritten to explain why it changed, not just that it did.

v3.4.42 - 2026-08-28 - Five more screenshots of the same underlying bug from v3.4.41, this time worse: E/W elevations correct, N/S showing nothing but a solid poché block, no buildings at all.

Tried hard to reproduce deterministically before touching anything further: recomputed fresh, drew several different boxes including one matching Joe's own tall/narrow proportions from his screenshot, tried going straight to N from Plan and going E→W→N→S in the same order Joe's screenshots showed. Every single attempt came back correct. That's itself informative, not a dead end -- a bug that won't reproduce under scripted, evenly-timed clicks but does under real manual use points at something timing- or environment-dependent that automated testing doesn't naturally trigger.

One concrete, plausible gap in v3.4.41's own fix: `requestAnimationFrame` callbacks are paused entirely while a browser tab is backgrounded or not visible -- not delayed, paused, until the tab regains focus. If there was any tab-switching (or even the screenshot tool itself briefly stealing focus) between picking a direction and that deferred frame actually running, v3.4.41's re-sync may simply not have fired yet by the time Joe looked at the result. This wouldn't show up in same-tab automated testing, where the tab stays focused throughout.

Added a `setTimeout(..., 250)` alongside the existing `requestAnimationFrame` call, not instead of it -- rAF still fires fastest in the common case (focused tab, next real frame), the timeout is a second, independent safety net that still fires (throttled, not suspended) even if the tab was backgrounded when the rAF would have run.

Stated plainly: this is a reasoned, defensible improvement given everything found so far, not a proven fix -- the underlying bug could not be reproduced live this round to confirm the timeout closes it. If it recurs, the next useful piece of information is whatever was happening around the moment of the screenshot -- another window focused, a tab switch, anything that might explain why a deferred callback wouldn't have run yet.

v3.4.41 - 2026-08-28 - Three screenshots, same box-cropped West elevation at different zoom levels: real buildings correctly outlined on the left, but a huge reddish/maroon rectangle extending far past them to the right with nothing in it.

Diagnosed live rather than guessed. Found the poché quad's actual GEOMETRY is a fixed ~11,000-unit plane regardless of any box (confirmed by reading its world-space bounding box directly) -- it relies ENTIRELY on shader-side discard uniforms to crop down to whatever box is active, not on `clippingPlanes`. Read those uniforms directly and found `uSkyDiscardOn: 0` on the active quad -- the exact master-switch-stuck-off mechanism v3.4.30 already found and fixed once (it gates BOTH the crop discard and the roofline discard; off means neither runs and the quad renders its full raw size, unclipped). Manually setting the correct values on the live, broken scene immediately fixed the render, confirming the mechanism before writing any fix.

Where this diverged from v3.4.30's own story: that was a fixed, deterministic call-ordering bug (`syncSectionSkyUniforms()` running before a later recompile could discard its write), and the ordering it fixed is still correct today, read and re-verified line by line. This time, the EXACT SAME reproduction sequence -- draw a box from Plan, pick a direction -- was tried twice in a row on the same live tab: broken the first time, correct the second. That's not a fixed ordering bug, it's a genuine race between `renderer.compile()` returning and the browser's own GPU/driver actually finishing shader compilation underneath it, which apparently isn't always done by the time `compile()` returns despite being documented as synchronous.

A fixed-ordering fix can't close a race. `applySectionMode()` now re-asserts the sky uniforms one more time via `requestAnimationFrame()` after every call, covering every path into it (nav buttons, `applyPlanBoxDirection()`, `resetToDefaultView()`) from one place rather than patching each call site separately. The next animation frame is a point where any compile work queued this frame is guaranteed to have actually finished -- "immediately after `compile()` returns" apparently isn't always that point.

Stated plainly rather than rounded up: the underlying mechanism and the manual fix are both directly proven live. The defensive `requestAnimationFrame` re-sync is well-reasoned against everything found, but its actual effect against the live code couldn't be fully re-verified this round -- a repeated automated test to confirm it closes the race across many cycles overloaded the tab before finishing. Worth specifically hammering this exact sequence (draw box from Plan, pick a direction, repeat several times) on the next live pass.

v3.4.40 - 2026-08-28 - Two screenshots: neighborhood computed, full panel visible as expected (a returning session -- `ns_exploredOnce` already set, correctly skipping the one-decision stage), then a small box drawn and "East Elevation" active alongside a STILL-OPEN "Box drawn — view it from" panel.

Diagnosed live rather than guessed from the images: reproduced the box-draw flow twice (fresh, and redrawing while already in an elevation) and both worked correctly -- the picker closed properly either time. The actual difference: clicking the always-visible View compass's OWN E button (`navE`) instead of the picker's own `boxViewE` button. Confirmed by reading `activeSectionBox` vs `pendingPlanBox` directly after reproducing it: genuinely different boxes. Worse than a cosmetic stale panel -- `setOrthogonalView(direction)` alone never touches `activeSectionBox` or `pendingPlanBox` at all; only `applyPlanBoxDirection()` does, and only the picker's own buttons ever called it. So the elevation that appeared was silently cropped to whatever box (or none) was already active BEFORE the new one was drawn, not the one just drawn -- a real correctness bug, not just a leftover panel. This ambiguity didn't exist before v3.4.34: the compass used to live in a separate floating panel elsewhere on screen; consolidating it into the same panel as the box-direction picker put two different "click E" buttons right next to each other with no way to tell them apart by looking, and only one of them was ever wired to the real commit logic.

Fix: new `goToDirection(direction)` checks `pendingPlanBox` first and routes through `applyPlanBoxDirection()` -- the exact same commit path the picker's own buttons already use -- falling back to a bare `setOrthogonalView(direction)` only when no box is actually pending. All four compass buttons (`navN`/`navS`/`navE`/`navW`) call this now instead of `setOrthogonalView()` directly. One function, one source of truth for "what does picking a direction actually mean right now" -- not two copies of similar logic that can silently drift apart, which is exactly how this happened in the first place.

Live-verified before shipping: drew a real box, confirmed `activeSectionBox` and `pendingPlanBox` were genuinely different objects on the broken path, then ran the fixed logic directly against that same live state and confirmed `activeSectionBox` now matches exactly what was just drawn, with the picker panel correctly hidden afterward.

Moved out of the Simple tab into the Export accordion, next to the STL button, where its actual purpose has a neighbor that makes sense.

**Tooltip positioning, the actual root cause named plainly**: the `?` badge tooltips only ever checked "does this stay on screen" -- clamped to viewport edges, with zero awareness of what other panel content might be sitting behind them. Invisible while the panel was short; v3.4.34-38 made it much taller, and Joe's screenshot showed the Simple/Advanced tooltip opening directly over Export STL and Model scale, both genuinely still there. Structural fix, not a one-off nudge: tooltips now anchor to the empty viewport space to the right of `#controls` itself, so they can never cover another control regardless of how tall the panel gets in some future round.

v3.4.38 - 2026-08-28 - Joe pushed back hard on the whole direction of the last several rounds: "'no idea what to do next' is the workflow UI/UX issue I am trying to describe... if I pass this off to a friend and he's sitting there scratching his head... that's the big picture item that needs to be reconciled." Correct -- v3.4.35's hint/highlight treatment was decoration on a workflow that was still fundamentally a flat panel of ~15 controls a stranger has to read and sequence on their own. A hint helps someone already looking in the right place; it doesn't build the mental model for someone who isn't.

Two mockups, approved before any real code changed, per the standing process for IA-level work:
1. **Panel redesign** -- exactly one decision visible at a time, everything else genuinely absent (not just hinted-at) until reachable: Neighborhood + Compute alone -> one real choice ("See the void" / "Draw a section") -> full panel, unlocked only then. Joe: "yes lets start here and then we can refine."
2. **View-picker layout** -- Joe: "a little house, P, N, S, E, W makes sense to me but could be designed layed out better." Three side-by-side options; Joe picked "C" -- N/S/E/W in a real compass cross (Home dead-center, matching the old floating nav panel's own arrangement before v3.4.34 flattened it into a row), Plan pulled out as its own full-width button since "look straight down" isn't a compass direction the way N/S/E/W are.

One real bug caught and fixed mid-review, worth naming: the first version of the combined mockup had the compass CSS rules defined but never actually applied the position classes to the buttons -- same mistake in spirit as several real-app bugs this session (code that looks right, isn't verified against what actually renders). Fixed by adding a headless-Chrome screenshot step for every mockup from that point on, not just the real app.

**Implementation**, moved into the real file after both mockups were approved: `#decisionStage` (new) sits between Step 1 (Neighborhood/Compute, now the ONLY thing visible on load) and `#exploreExtras` (the full panel -- Buildings/Negative-space, the view picker, Draw section box, Units, Display, Export all moved inside it now, alongside what was already there). `showPostComputeUI()` decides which of the two to show based on `ns_exploredOnce` (same flag v3.4.35 introduced, now driving a real state swap instead of just a hint's visibility). `dismissExploreHint()` (name kept since several call sites already reference it) now calls `showPostComputeUI()` in addition to its original job. The two choice cards don't reimplement anything -- each just calls `.click()` on the real button its choice corresponds to (`viewNegative` / `drawBoxBtn`), so there's exactly one source of truth for what either action actually does. View-compass buttons are the exact same elements as before (same ids, same listeners), each now carrying its own `compassN`/`compassW`/`compassHome`/`compassE`/`compassS` grid-position class -- verified this time, not just written.

**Verification, deliberately more thorough than earlier rounds given the direct conversation this session about audits and regression coverage**: full duplicate-id sweep, a reverse check that every `getElementById()` call resolves to a real element, a jsdom structural test confirming actual parent/child relationships (Step 1 content outside both new wrappers; everything else correctly inside `#exploreExtras`; each compass button carrying its own position class), `audit_deploy.js` at 124/124 (3 stale checks from superseded v3.4.34/35 behavior replaced with checks proving the replacement, 6 new checks added for this round's own mechanisms), and -- since Claude in Chrome wasn't connected this round -- a static harness built from the REAL extracted `<style>` block and the REAL `#controls` HTML (not the standalone mockup file), screenshotted in all three states via headless Chrome. Confirmed live from that: Step 1 alone, the decision cards with real neighborhood/count text, and the compass cross rendering as a genuine plus-shape. Also caught and resolved one false alarm this way (`firstRunHint` appeared still visible in the forced-state screenshots) by reading `computeNegativeSpace()` directly rather than assuming -- it already hides that hint immediately on Compute, pre-existing behavior, unrelated to this change; the harness just didn't simulate that one JS side-effect.

**What's still NOT verified**, stated plainly rather than rounded up to "done": the real end-to-end JS-driven flow (an actual compute finishing, the real Negative-space/box-draw button clicks firing through `dismissExploreHint()`, real N/S/E/W camera switches) requires the live 3D/WASM engine, which needs network access this sandbox doesn't have and a live browser connection that wasn't available this round. Structurally and visually verified; not yet interaction-verified. Held for Joe's own live test rather than shipped as fully proven.

v3.4.37 - 2026-08-28 - Joe's screenshot showed Plan view rendering almost empty -- a tiny building fragment, scattered thin dashes, a red line, otherwise bare dark canvas -- with the same complaint as before: "as a new user i would be confused not knowing what to do next." Diagnosed live before proposing anything: connected to the browser, reproduced the EXACT sequence (drew a box, viewed it from W, clicked P), and read the live scene state directly rather than guess. The camera frustum was genuinely full-site (±3964/±2041, matching Chelsea's real ~1913x3658m extent) -- not a camera bug at all. The actual cause: v3.4.28/32's own box-scoping (Joe's own earlier explicit request -- "why can't we have it so nothing displays outside of the section box") was still clipping `negativeMesh`/`buildingMat`/`groundMesh`/`streetMaterials` down to just the tiny box, because `activeSectionBox` persists across view switches and `isPlanViewActive` was driving the SAME box-clipping logic for Plan that elevations correctly use. So casually clicking P after browsing an elevation left the whole site invisible except that one box, with nothing explaining why.

Proposed the fix before touching code, given it partially reverses a previous explicit request: Plan should never geometry-clip to the box at all -- always show the full site for orientation, with the box drawn as an outline instead. Joe: "yes."

`applySectionMode()`'s `axis===null` branch (Plan and free perspective/Home, the two cases that share it) now ALWAYS uses the plain classic octant/corner-cutaway fallback for `negativeMesh`, `buildingMat` (via `syncBuildingClipping(axis, false)` -- always `false` now), and the Z cap-fill quad, regardless of `activeSectionBox`. `groundMesh`/`streetMaterials` dropped the `|| isPlanViewActive` from their own clip condition, so they only box-scope for a real locked elevation (`axis !== null`) now, never Plan. Box-clipping is completely untouched for N/S/E/W -- a section still shows exactly what's in the box's depth, which is correct there.

New `updatePlanBoxOutline()` replaces the lost visual connection to the box: a real world-space `THREE.LineLoop` traced around the box's actual `xMin/xMax/yMin/yMax` at a fixed Z just above the ground plane, `depthTest:false` + high `renderOrder` so it always draws on top regardless of what's underneath. Lazily created on first real use, shown only while `isPlanViewActive && activeSectionBox`, called from both `applySectionMode()` (every view change) and `clearActiveSectionBox()` (box cleared while Plan might be open). The "Full width" button, which v3.4.28 had extended to cover Plan too, reverted to elevation-only -- Plan has nothing left for it to widen now that it's never narrowed in the first place.

Live-verified before shipping: applied the exact same logic directly to Joe's own live, already-broken tab (box active, Plan showing the tiny fragment) and confirmed the full site reappeared with a clean outline where the box is.

Answer: `negativeMesh`, the full molded-volume ghost shell, rendered at 0.15 opacity with a roofline-discard shader meant to make it hug each real building's height where one exists. Followed up with two screenshots and no comment, which read as "look at this, something's off" rather than confirmation it was working as intended -- asked directly whether the issue was that it wasn't visibly stepping with the skyline, or whether it shouldn't be there at all. Joe: "it should not be showing when going into any elevation view."

`negativeMesh.visible` now also requires `sectionModeAxis === null` at both of its two assignment sites (the compute-time reset and `refreshViewToggles()`) -- the ghost shell only shows in Plan/perspective now, where it's the one shape actually representing the site's full molded volume. An elevation's real content -- the red skyline outline and the poché fill (`capFillGroups.x`/`y`) -- is untouched; neither depends on `negativeMesh`'s own visibility at all, only on `showNegative` and `sectionModeAxis` in their own right.

v3.4.35 - 2026-08-28 - Joe: "the user experience needs work after computing negative space a user has no idea what to do next? what do you suggest?" Proposed three things, Joe said yes to all three, then added a fourth in the same breath: "units make no sense in the beginning when there are no values to enter."

**The real, concrete gap found before proposing anything**: every compute -- including a RECOMPUTE while already looking at a section -- unconditionally reset `showNegative` to `false`, silently. So the actual point of the tool (the void) could vanish with zero explanation mid-session, and a first-time user landing fresh saw only grey buildings with no strong signal that there was anything else to turn on. Fixed: `showNegative = sectionModeAxis !== null` -- a fresh compute (Plan/perspective, nothing explored yet) still defaults off, preserving the original v3.2.43 reasoning for why an unexplained corner-cutaway shouldn't greet a new user; but a recompute while already inside a locked N/S/E/W section now stays exactly how it was, matching v3.4.6's own "recompute doesn't reset the view" rule and v3.4.31's own "a section view is never shown with Negative space off" rule -- this just closed the one remaining gap between those two already-established behaviors.

**Persistent hint, not just a toast.** A toast already existed at compute time ("turn on Negative space... or Draw section box for a section") but lives ~3 seconds -- easy to miss entirely right after a ~25s compute wait when attention has wandered. New `#exploreHint`, same dismiss-once-and-remember pattern `#firstRunHint` already established (`localStorage`, permanent across sessions, not just this page load) -- gone the first time the user does any of: turns Negative space on, picks a real N/S/E/W view (nav buttons or the box-direction picker, both funnel through `setOrthogonalView()`), or draws a box (which also funnels through the same function on completion).

**Visual weight for the two things that actually matter.** Negative space and Draw section box used to look identical to Units or Metric/Imperial -- flat grey `.secondary`, no hierarchy, nothing said "try this first." New `.suggested` class: an accent OUTLINE, deliberately distinct from both `.secondary` (nothing to see) and `.active` (already on/filled) so it doesn't read as already-enabled. Removed by the same `dismissExploreHint()` call that clears the hint text -- a first-visit nudge, not a permanent decoration.

**Units relocated.** Metric/Imperial only ever affected the Advanced-cutaway slider labels/values (m vs ft) -- already gated behind a compute inside `#sectionRow` -- so showing the toggle before that point offered a choice with nothing yet to apply it to. Moved from its old always-visible spot above Neighborhood into `#exploreExtras`, gated the same as everything else in that wrapper.

Not yet live-verified -- this tab was still on v3.4.34 when the fix landed and code can't be pushed to Joe's server directly; held for his reload, same as v3.4.34's own structural change.

This is the structural move the mockup proposed: view-switching and the box-draw workflow relocate from the separate right-side `#navPanel` (and a THIRD, temporary floating `#planBoxPanel` that only existed mid-workflow) into the same `#controls` panel neighborhood/compute already live in, inside a new `#exploreExtras` wrapper gated at the exact same two points `#sectionRow` already was (hidden until a compute has actually run, cleared on district switch). v3.4.32 - 2026-08-28 - Joe: "why can't we have it so that nothing displays outside of the section box... ground plane, artifacts etc." A real, previously-uncovered gap: the ground plane and the Major-streets ribbons/labels have never had any `clippingPlanes` at all. Every OTHER piece of geometry in a box-scoped view (`negativeMesh`, `buildingMat`, both poché quads) box-scopes correctly -- that's the whole story from v3.4.0 through v3.4.29 -- but nobody had ever extended the same treatment to the CONTEXT geometry sitting underneath all of it. Symptom: a locked N/S/E/W elevation with a small box active still showed the ground (and any street ribbons crossing it) stretching across the entire site width -- exactly the near-empty canvas with a tiny building cluster and a huge bare tan strip in Joe's screenshots.

Fix: `groundMesh` and every material in `streetMaterials` now get the same box-rectangle `clippingPlanes` (reusing `getPlanBoxClipPlanes()`, dropping its Height-Cut plane via `.slice(1)` since the ground/streets are already flat at their own fixed Z and don't need a vertical bound) whenever a box is actually driving the current view -- a locked elevation or box-scoped Plan. Neither material has any `onBeforeCompile` custom uniforms (plain `MeshStandardMaterial`/`MeshBasicMaterial`), so unlike `negMat`/the poché quads there's no uniforms-object-discarded trap here to worry about -- a plane-count change on these two is just an ordinary, harmless recompile.

Live-verified the mechanism on Joe's actual running tab before shipping: applied the same clipping-plane logic directly to the live ground mesh and confirmed it visually bounds to the box instead of spanning the full site.

v3.4.31 - 2026-08-28 - Two things from the same message. First: Joe's screenshots showed the "solid block" bug still happening on v3.4.30, with a specific new detail -- "images is from opening N S first. If I open E W first and then go to N S i get different results." Live-swept this exact comparison on Joe's actual v3.4.30 tab via window.__NS: drew a box, dumped every sky-discard uniform (`uSkyDiscardOn`, `uSkyLo`, `uSkyHi`, `uSkyCropOn`, `uSkyRoofDiscardOn`) for both `negMat` and the active quad going straight to N, then reset and did the same going E first then N, with a second, differently-shaped/positioned box for good measure, and once more drawing the second box directly from within an already-open elevation view rather than from Plan. Every single one of these came back byte-identical and fully correct -- v3.4.30's fix held up under direct comparison every way it could be tried. Best explanation is Joe's screenshots were taken before his reload had fully picked up v3.4.30 (footer version alone doesn't guarantee a hard-cache-free reload) rather than a surviving code bug; noted rather than silently assumed, since it couldn't be reproduced against real live state.

Second, a real and independent ask: "if i select N S E W negative space should be active by default." True throughout this entire debugging session -- every single repro needed a manual extra click on "Negative space" after entering a section view before there was anything to look at, since a fresh compute always leaves it off. `setOrthogonalView(direction)` now flips `showNegative` on the first time direction is 'n'/'s'/'e'/'w' and it wasn't already -- covers both entry points (the persistent nav-compass buttons and the box-direction-picker panel) since both funnel through this one function. `audit_deploy.js`: 102/102 (1 new check for the default-on behavior). APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.31.

v3.4.30 - 2026-08-28 - The real fix for the "solid block, no building outline" bug Joe first reported several versions ago and kept reproducing through v3.4.29, despite two earlier attempts (v3.4.26, v3.4.27) at what turned out to be the same bug CLASS via different triggers. This time diagnosed and verified live on Joe's actual running tab via `window.__NS`, not from screenshots alone.

Root cause: `uSkyDiscardOn` is the master switch in the poché quads' fragment shader -- it gates the ENTIRE discard block, including both the box-crop discard AND the roofline discard nested inside it (`if (uSkyDiscardOn > 0.5) { ... if (uSkyRoofDiscardOn > 0.5) {...} }`). Live inspection found it reading `0` on the active axis's quad in a real broken N view -- meaning neither discard was running at all, so the quad rendered its full geometric extent solid, ignoring both the box crop and every real building's roofline. Manually setting it to `1` on the live, already-broken tab immediately resolved the solid block into a correctly-scoped poché fill, with nothing else changed -- confirming this exact uniform, on this exact material, was the whole remaining gap.

`syncSectionSkyUniforms(axis)` (the function that writes `uSkyDiscardOn`) was being called too early in `applySectionMode()` -- right after the FIRST `renderer.compile()`, but BEFORE the active quad's own `clippingPlanes` got reassigned and BEFORE `refreshViewToggles()` flipped its `.visible` from false to true. The SECOND `renderer.compile()` (v3.4.27's own fix, positioned after both of those) settles the recompile those two changes trigger -- but nothing re-wrote `uSkyDiscardOn` into whatever fresh uniforms object that second compile produced. Same underlying bug class as v3.4.26/27 (deferred recompile discarding a just-written uniform), reached through a third, previously-uncovered trigger.

Moved the call to the very end of `applySectionMode()` -- after every `clippingPlanes`/`needsUpdate` change and after the LAST `renderer.compile()`, so it writes into the uniforms object that's actually still in use once the function returns. Same principle `refreshActiveSectionProfile()` already followed for the height-texture half of this same sync (which is exactly why that half was already working while the discard-toggle half wasn't).

`audit_deploy.js`: 101/101 (1 new check: `syncSectionSkyUniforms(axis)` is called after the second `renderer.compile()`, not the first). APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.30.

v3.4.29 - 2026-08-28 - Joe: "z plane artifacts" -- a fresh regression, introduced by v3.4.28 itself, caught within the same testing session.

Live-swept the E-then-N repro Joe described in detail first (draw box, view from E/W, switch to N/S via the persistent nav compass) and couldn't reproduce his original "solid block" symptom on v3.4.28 despite matching his sequence exactly, multiple times, both slow and rapid-fire -- every attempt rendered correctly. Along the way, live inspection via window.__NS did turn up one confirmed (if likely harmless for an orthographic camera) issue: `controls.maxDistance` (3500, a cap really meant for the free-orbit perspective camera, shared via the same OrbitControls instance) silently clamps the ortho camera's actual distance whenever a box's span exceeds ~300m -- `orthoLight` uses the correct, unclamped `camDist` directly and isn't affected, and an orthographic camera's apparent framing is governed entirely by its frustum (derived from `span`, untouched by the clamp) rather than its distance, so this doesn't appear to change what's rendered -- flagged for future cleanup, not fixed this round.

Then Joe reported new artifacts specifically on the Z (Height Cut) plane, and this one traced directly back to v3.4.28's own change: the Z cap quad's clippingPlanes rebuild (added in v3.4.28 to box-scope the Plan poché) lived in the UNCONDITIONAL `if (capFillGroups){...}` block, which runs on every single `applySectionMode()` call -- including every N/S/E/W switch, not just Plan. The box-scoped branch is 4 real planes + `SITE_BOUND_PLANES` (10 total); the fallback is 2 + `SITE_BOUND_PLANES` (8 total) -- so leaving Plan for a locked elevation (isPlanViewActive flips false, box unchanged) silently changed this quad's clippingPlanes COUNT on every transition. That's the identical deferred-recompile-discards-uniforms trigger v3.4.26/27 already fixed twice for other materials (negMat, the x/y quads) -- introduced fresh for a third material by v3.4.28, in the same session it shipped.

The Z quad has no relationship to N/S/E/W at all -- Height Cut is Plan/perspective territory, exactly like the negMat/buildingMat box-scoping already living in the `axis===null` branch. Moved the Z quad's rebuild there too, so it's genuinely untouched (byte-for-byte its pre-v3.4.28 clippingPlanes) for the entire duration of any locked section view, same as it always was before v3.4.28 existed.

`audit_deploy.js`: 100/100 (1 check updated to match the new location, not a net-new addition). APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.29.

v3.4.28 - 2026-08-28 - Joe: "why is the plan poche not showing in the area i selected... if i go back to plan now it shows another area." Connected live via Claude in Chrome this time instead of guessing from screenshots (window.__NS gives direct read access to the running scene) and root-caused it for real before touching code.

Confirmed live: `activeSectionBox` held Joe's real drawn box the entire time -- but Plan and the free perspective/Home view both pass `axis=null` to `applySectionMode()`, and that function has ALWAYS meant "restore the plain octant (dollhouse-corner) cutaway" for `axis===null`, unconditionally, with no `activeSectionBox` check at all. So going back to Plan after drawing a box and picking N/S/E/W didn't show the box, or nothing -- it showed a COMPLETELY DIFFERENT kind of cut: the classic corner, driven by raw X/Y Cutaway threshold values with single-sided/AND-of-three-half-spaces semantics. That corner happens to reuse the same numbers a box recenters onto, which is exactly what made it look like "almost right, but not even close" -- same values, different geometry entirely. Live-measured: `negativeMesh.material.clippingPlanes` were the 3-plane corner regardless of the real box sitting untouched in `activeSectionBox`.

A second, independent piece of the same gap: the Height-cut cap-fill quad (`capFillGroups.z`, the actual visible poché fill in Plan) had its `clippingPlanes` set ONCE at construction in `buildCapFillGroups()` and never rebuilt anywhere afterward -- confirmed via `grep`, no code path touches it again. So even fixing the corner-vs-box issue for `negativeMesh`/`buildingMat` alone wouldn't have moved the actual fill Joe was looking at.

Fixed all three together. New `getPlanBoxClipPlanes()` returns a real two-sided rectangle (the box's own `xMin`/`xMax`/`yMin`/`yMax`, AND-ed) plus the existing Height Cut plane. `applySectionMode()` gained an `isPlanView` param (new module flag `isPlanViewActive`, since `axis===null` alone can't tell Plan and perspective/Home apart) -- `setOrthogonalView()` now passes `direction === 'plan'` through. When `isPlanViewActive && activeSectionBox`, `negativeMesh`, `buildingMat` (via `syncBuildingClipping()`, also given the same param), and the Z cap quad all switch to the box rectangle; otherwise every one of them is byte-for-byte the same classic corner as before -- free perspective/Home is completely untouched, still looks exactly as it always has.

Also gave Joe the "option to extend" he asked for: the existing "Full width" button (previously only ever shown/wired for a locked N/S/E/W view) now also appears in box-scoped Plan, and `clearActiveSectionBox()` no longer silently no-ops there (its old guard only re-applied if `sectionModeAxis` was truthy, which is never true in Plan).

Live-verified the core mechanism directly on Joe's running localhost:8888 before shipping: reproduced the exact bug (drew a box over real buildings, picked N, went back to Plan, saw the same disconnected corner Joe screenshotted), then applied the new clipping-plane logic to the live scene through `window.__NS` and confirmed the poché collapses to a clean rectangle over the actual drawn area instead. The shipped file carries the real, permanent version of that same logic.

v3.4.27 - 2026-08-28 - Joe's screenshots (v3.4.26 confirmed in the footer) showed the exact bug v3.4.26 was supposed to fix, unchanged: N/S views straight out of a box-draw still showed a solid red block with no building outline. His plan-view screenshot ("cut plane is way off from the boundary drawn") turned out to be a real clue pointing at a DIFFERENT, real bug in my own previous fix, not a second independent one.

Root cause of the miss: v3.4.26's `renderer.compile(scene, activeCamera)` call sat ABOVE `refreshViewToggles()` in `applySectionMode()` -- but `refreshViewToggles()` (via `refreshCapFillVisibility()`) is what actually sets the active quad's `.visible = true` for the first time on a given axis. `renderer.compile()` skips invisible objects during its own scene traversal, identical to a real `render()` pass -- so calling it before visibility flips true compiled nothing for that material. The REAL first compile still happened later, on whatever frame rendered next, which is exactly the deferred-recompile-discards-uniforms failure v3.4.26 documented and was supposed to prevent. The fix was correct in mechanism, wrong in placement.

Moved the call to run immediately after `refreshViewToggles()` instead -- visibility is genuinely true by then, and it's still before `refreshActiveSectionProfile()` (a few lines later) writes the real height texture into the quad's uniforms.

Separately: the vertical red band in Joe's plan-view screenshot, visually disconnected from the box he'd just drawn, is very likely NOT an independent bug -- `applyPlanBoxDirection()` only re-centers `xThreshold`/`yThreshold` onto the new box's own midpoint once a direction (N/S/E/W) is actually clicked; before that, the box is just a pending selection and the side panel's X/Y Cutaway values are simply whatever was left over from before this box was drawn. Nothing in the code path claims otherwise. Flagged as a likely non-issue rather than silently ignored -- worth Joe's eyes once this real fix is in, since it may just resolve itself.

Not yet live-verified -- diagnosed and corrected from Joe's screenshots plus a direct trace of `setOrthogonalView()` -> `applySectionMode()`'s actual call order (confirmed `activeCamera` isn't even reassigned to `orthoCamera` until AFTER `applySectionMode()` returns, same pre-existing ordering `negMat`'s v3.4.11 fix already lives with -- not touched here, noted for any future work in this area).

v3.4.26 - 2026-08-28 - Joe: N/S views showed a solid red block covering the whole cropped area, and only started showing the real per-position profile cuts after dragging the Y Cutaway slider -- "maybe related to section box." It wasn't specific to the box workflow, just easiest to spot there (a box makes the wrong full-block fill visually obvious against a wide crop).

Same failure mode as v3.4.11's `negMat` fix, just never extended to the poché quads that hit the identical trigger. Whichever quad becomes the active axis switches its `clippingPlanes` array from 3 planes to 2 (or back) right when entering/leaving a section view -- a plane-COUNT change, which forces a fresh `onBeforeCompile` on whatever frame renders next, building a brand-new uniforms object and repointing `mat.userData.skyUniforms` at it. `refreshActiveSectionProfile()` (called moments later, same function) writes the real per-column height texture into the CURRENT uniforms object -- but the deferred recompile (asynchronous relative to this function) fires first, discarding that write before anything paints. Symptom: the quad renders with `uSkyDiscardOn` stuck at its recompile default (0 = no discard = fills solid, full local-ceiling height, the real roofline profile ignored entirely) on first entry into ANY section view. Any later threshold change re-calls `refreshActiveSectionProfile()` -- by then the recompile has already happened, so that write lands on the real object and sticks, which is exactly why dragging the slider "fixed" it.

Fix: the same `renderer.compile(scene, activeCamera)` technique v3.4.11 already used for `negMat`, called a second time -- now also right after the quads' `clippingPlanes` reassignment, forcing their recompile synchronously before `refreshActiveSectionProfile()` writes into them, instead of leaving it to whatever frame happens to render next.

Not yet live-verified on Joe's machine -- diagnosed from his description of the repro (solid block on entry, fixed by any slider drag) plus a direct code read matching it to an already-documented bug class in this file; held for his reload per usual.

v3.4.25 - 2026-08-28 - Joe's follow-up screenshot, zoomed out with street labels on: a dense, chaotic band of red noise running the full width of the elevation at ground level, captioned "this is all z axis plan geometry rep." Confirmed exactly right.

That band is `zCutLineMesh` -- the TRUE per-building Z cross-section outline, i.e. every single building in the district sliced at Height Cut (0m here) and drawn as its own jagged line. Legitimate in Plan/perspective view, where it's the whole point; meaningless in a locked N/S/E/W elevation, where Height is inert ("full by design" per the Advanced tab's own note). `xCutLineMesh`/`yCutLineMesh` already got this exact suppression in v3.4.11/14 (superseded by the skyline profile line, always off now) -- `zCutLineMesh` was simply never covered by that same cleanup, and kept its original pre-section-view rule: visible whenever Negative space is on, full stop, no `sectionModeAxis` check at all.

Fixed the one line: `zCutLineMesh.visible = showNegative` -> `showNegative && sectionModeAxis === null`. `refreshViewToggles()` (which owns this line) already reruns on every N/S/E/W/Plan/perspective switch via `applySectionMode()`, so this takes effect on every existing view-change path with no new wiring. Plan and perspective both pass `sectionModeAxis === null`, so the Z cross-section stays exactly as visible as before there, where Height actually means something.

v3.4.24 - 2026-08-28 - Continuing the open "north elevation renders solid black" item from v3.4.23. Confirmed the hypothesis: the scene's one `sun` (`DirectionalLight` fixed at world position `(400,300,700)`) lights north- and east-facing walls (positive dot product with the light vector) but leaves south- and west-facing walls at ambient-only (negative dot) -- invisible in the free-orbiting perspective view (you naturally orbit toward the lit side), but every locked N/S/E/W elevation shows exactly ONE fixed facade, so 2 of the 4 were always going to read dramatically darker than the other 2. Verified by hand: L = normalize(400,300,700) = (0.457,0.343,0.820); N view shows the south wall (normal (0,-1,0), N·L=-0.343, dark); E view shows the west wall (normal (-1,0,0), N·L=-0.465, darker still -- would have been reported too, just hadn't been screenshotted yet). S and W read positive and looked fine, which is exactly why only N had come up.

Fix: added a second light, `orthoLight`, repositioned in a new `updateOrthoLight(direction, target, camDist)` to always sit on the same side as whichever elevation camera is active -- a headlight, so the visible facade is front-lit the same way in every one of N/S/E/W/Plan. `setOrthogonalView()` swaps `sun` out for `orthoLight` on entry; `resetToDefaultView()` swaps back on the way to perspective, so the established default-view lighting is completely untouched. Verified the geometry standalone (Node, independent of three.js): old fixed-sun dot products were N -0.349 / S +0.349 / E -0.465 / W +0.465; new `orthoLight` dot products are N/S/E/W all exactly 0.944 -- genuinely identical front-lighting on whichever wall is visible, not just a less-bad fixed direction.

Not yet live-verified on Joe's machine -- staged alongside v3.4.25 below, held per Joe's standing rule until he reloads.

`audit_deploy.js`: 96/96 (final, after 3.4.25's checks landed too). APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.24 (superseded by 3.4.25 above before shipping -- both fixes went out together).

v3.4.23 - 2026-08-25 - Joe caught two real bugs in the v3.4.22 tooltips within minutes of using them: "tool tips cannot be hidden," plus a screenshot showing the tooltip box crushed into 3 wrapped lines instead of readable text. Both traced to confirmed causes, not guessed.

**Crushed/wrapped rendering**: `#controls` only ever set `overflow-y:auto` -- per the CSS Overflow spec, a non-`visible` value on one axis forces the OTHER axis to compute as non-visible too, so `overflow-x` was silently `auto` this whole time. Confirmed with real numbers: the tooltip's actual 200px box extended to screen x=428, but `#controls`'s own box ends at x=256 -- everything past that was clipped, crushing the tooltip into whatever narrow sliver fit inside the panel.

**Can't be hidden**: the tooltip's visibility relied on `:hover` OR `:focus`. Clicking the badge (a real `tabindex="0"` element) focuses it, and focus alone satisfies that OR condition independent of the mouse -- so moving the mouse away never closed it, and there was no obvious way to "unfocus" a `<span>`.

Replaced the whole mechanism rather than patching either symptom individually: JS-managed, `position:fixed` tooltips (escapes ANY ancestor's overflow clipping entirely -- the exact technique already proven for the neighborhood dropdown, v3.4.5), positioned from the badge's own `getBoundingClientRect()` and clamped to the viewport on both axes (plus a check for whether there's room above the badge, falling back to below when there isn't), toggled by click with a click-away-to-close listener -- same convention as that same dropdown, not native focus/hover semantics.

One bug caught in my own new code before shipping: the position function reads `tip.offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` to clamp against the viewport, but those read `0` while the tip is still `display:none` -- had to toggle the `open` class BEFORE calling the position function, not after.

Live-verified fully, not just reasoned: since the badge/tip elements already exist in the currently-loaded v3.4.22 page, injected the corrected CSS and JS directly and reproduced the exact real-mouse-click sequence that broke it -- confirmed the tooltip now renders as one full, readable box (not wrapped), and confirmed clicking elsewhere on the page actually closes it.

v3.4.22 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: "I think there needs to be some tool tips guiding the user what to do. It's not very intuitive." Added a reusable `.info-badge` component -- a small "?" badge, CSS-only hover/focus tooltip (no JS needed, keyboard-accessible via `tabindex` + `:focus`), matching the panel's existing card/border/shadow styling. Placed one next to the Buildings/Negative space row, one next to Section fill (as a sibling of its `<label>`, not a child -- inside the label, clicking the badge would have also toggled the checkbox, since labels toggle their associated input on any click within them), and one next to the Simple/Advanced tabs.

Also added `#firstRunHint`, a small dashed-border hint ("Pick a neighborhood below, then Compute negative space to get started") shown above the Buildings/Negative space row until Compute has been clicked once -- then hidden permanently via `localStorage` (`ns_computedOnce`), not just for the current session, using the exact same persistence pattern already established for theme/uiMode elsewhere in this file.

Live-verified the tooltip mechanism directly (a real badge injected into Joe's running session, confirmed the tooltip renders correctly on `:focus`) -- full HTML/CSS structural verification (the actual new markup in place) needs a real reload, since testing new DOM elements that don't exist yet in the currently-loaded page isn't something a live JS patch alone can fully cover the way a pure logic/CSS-property change can.

Bumped left padding to 16px specifically (matching the panel's own 16px offset from the browser window's edge, for a consistent margin rhythm), leaving top/right/bottom untouched -- the right side in particular was just carefully tuned against the scrollbar gutter in v3.4.20 and didn't need touching. Live-verified: patched the CSS into the running session and confirmed the visible left margin.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.21.

v3.4.20 - 2026-08-25 - Joe's follow-up screenshot: two arrows pointing at the X Cutaway slider and Reset cutaway button, right at the panel's own right "edge" -- "look at the arrows and the edges." Asked what they meant rather than guess a third time on the same complaint, then measured precisely once the answer pointed at the edges specifically.

Confirmed directly: on Windows Chrome (non-overlay scrollbars, unlike macOS's overlay-by-default), `#controls`'s scrollbar physically occupies part of its own width -- `offsetWidth` reads 240px but `clientWidth` only 229px whenever scrolling is actually active. This isn't a fixed, one-time layout -- content re-lays-out fresh, so the moment the panel's content grows past `max-height` (switching from Simple to Advanced, or anything else that adds height), the content area suddenly narrows by 11px as the scrollbar appears, and everything near the right edge visibly crowds inward right at that moment. Exactly what the arrows were pointing at.

Fixed with `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on `#controls` -- reserves the scrollbar's own width permanently, whether or not scrolling is currently needed, so the content area's available width never changes based on scroll state. No shift, no crowding, regardless of which tab or view state the panel is in.

Live-verified precisely: patched the property into the running session and measured `clientWidth` in both Simple (not overflowing) and Advanced (overflowing) mode -- 229px in both, zero difference, confirming the width is now genuinely stable across the exact transition that used to shift it.

v3.4.19 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: "the left side bar is restricted - things are getting cut off." Checked whether the panel is ACTUALLY clipped (the v3.4.4/8 bug class) before assuming it had regressed: measured directly, `#controls`'s content needs 945px but only has 818px available in Advanced mode -- so it genuinely does overflow, but the underlying scroll mechanism (max-height + overflow-y:auto, working correctly since v3.4.4/8) means that content isn't lost, just scrollable. The real problem: nothing here ever styled the scrollbar itself, so it fell back to Chrome's default thin overlay scrollbar, which is nearly invisible against this panel's dark navy background. With zero visible affordance telling you there's more below, a panel that scrolls correctly and a panel that's actually cut off look identical from the outside -- worth fixing even though the underlying mechanism was never broken this time.

v3.4.18 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: "when you draw a section box you can zoom in and out the rectangle floats around." Confirmed directly in the code, not just the screenshot: `#planBoxOverlay`'s position and size were set ONLY during the original pointerdown/pointermove drag, in raw screen pixels -- nothing ever re-synced it afterward. Once the drag ends, `pendingPlanBox` (the box's real world coordinates) gets stored and the direction-picker panel appears, but the visible OUTLINE just stays at whatever screen pixels it was left at from the drag itself -- zooming or panning moves the camera (and the real buildings under it) while the outline stays put, drifting apart from what it's supposed to be marking.

Fixed by re-projecting the outline from its real world coordinates every frame, for as long as a box is pending -- `updatePendingPlanBoxOverlay()`, called from the render loop (`animate()`), reuses the same `project()`-based world-to-screen technique already used elsewhere in this file for screen-space overlays. Keeps the outline correctly anchored regardless of zoom or pan.

Live-verified precisely: drew a box, confirmed the direction-picker panel came up, then forced the orthographic camera's zoom directly (2x, bypassing an unrelated scroll-wheel-doesn't-register-on-this-tool issue) and confirmed the outline stayed exactly aligned with the same real buildings after the zoom -- no drift.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.18.

v3.4.17 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: comparing three towers between the 3D view and the elevation section, "the red outline does not match... could this be causing the visual discrepancy." Investigating this led directly to the real, final root cause of the whole "big rectangle" saga across v3.4.15/16.

Tested a different X value (878m, three different towers, real local max height 45.3m) on the actual v3.4.16 build. The oversized rectangle was back. Bisected mesh-by-mesh again (same technique as v3.4.16) and this time isolated it precisely to the poché quad itself -- confirmed by hiding every other mesh (`negativeMesh`, `solidGroup`, the other quads) one at a time and finding the rectangle persisted with ONLY the quad visible. Queried its `clippingPlanes` array directly: 7 planes, no trace of `localCeilingPlane` at all -- confirmed on a genuinely fresh, unpatched reload + compute, not contamination from earlier testing in the same session.

**Real root cause**: a THIRD piece of code, `refreshCapFillVisibility()` (pre-existing since v3.2.30-32, unrelated to anything built this week), rebuilds the quad's entire `clippingPlanes` array from scratch on every axis refresh -- `xQuadMat.clippingPlanes = axis === 'x' ? [...] : [...]` -- a full wholesale reassignment, not a merge. It runs as part of `applySectionMode()`, right when entering a section view, and had no knowledge that `localCeilingPlane` (v3.4.15) was ever added to the array at construction. So `localCeilingPlane` survived exactly until the FIRST time this function ran -- typically the moment you enter N/S/E/W -- at which point it was silently discarded, before any threshold value was even set. This is why v3.4.16 looked completely fixed testing X=939 (that test happened to check the plane data before this rebuild fired) and why the very next X value (878, or anything after entering the view fresh) always came back broken -- not a flaky bug, a deterministic one-time silent drop that any subsequent verification would have caught if the ORDER of testing had been different.

Fixed by adding `localCeilingPlane` to both branches of both rebuild lines (the x-quad and y-quad versions). No other function does a wholesale `clippingPlanes` reassignment for these quads (checked every call site in the file) -- `syncCapFillPlanes()` and `updateCutLinePlanes()`, the other two functions touching related state on every threshold drag, only mutate existing plane `.constant`/`.normal` values in place and don't touch the array itself, so they're not a similar risk.

Live-verified precisely: reproduced Joe's exact broken state (X=878m, real local max 45.3m, rectangle still showing full district-wide height), computed the correct local ceiling from real geometry, and rebuilt the quad's clipping-planes array exactly as the real fix now does -- rectangle resolved to match the actual buildings.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.17. This closes the loop on the local-ceiling saga (v3.4.15 → v3.4.16 → v3.4.17) -- three real, distinct bugs, each one masking whether the previous fix had actually worked.

v3.4.16 - 2026-08-25 - Joe reloaded v3.4.15 and tested the exact same scenario (Chelsea, X=939m, W view) -- the huge rectangle was still there. Investigated live rather than assume the v3.4.15 fix was simply wrong, and found something bigger.

**Wrong mesh entirely, the whole time.** Hid every poché quad (x, y, AND z) completely -- the rectangle didn't move, proving it was never the poché fill at all. It was `negativeMesh`'s own ghost shell, rendering its full raw, unclipped geometry because its discard shader was silently switched off (`uSkyDiscardOn` read back `0` despite being in a true section view). This is the exact same "shader recompile resets custom uniforms" bug class v3.4.11 already found and fixed once -- but reachable from a SECOND path that fix never covered: dragging or typing a new cutaway value (`setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()` -> `refreshActiveSectionProfile()`) never re-synced the uniforms at all, only entering a section view fresh did. Verified precisely: forced `uSkyDiscardOn` back to `1` manually, the huge rectangle vanished immediately -- confirmed this was the dominant, masking bug the whole time, with the v3.4.15 local-ceiling fix underneath it working correctly but invisible.

**A second, related bug found while fixing this**: there were two separate, conflicting implementations of the local-ceiling fix already in the file -- the intended one (a persistent `localCeilingPlane` object, added once at quad construction, its `.constant` kept current in `refreshActiveSectionProfile()`), and a leftover second one (`applyLocalPocheCeiling()`) that appended a BRAND NEW plane to the quad's clipping-planes array on every single axis change -- growing it without bound -- and was never even called from the threshold-drag path that actually needed the fix. Removed the duplicate entirely; consolidated into the one correct implementation.

Fixed the root uniform-reset bug by adding the same `renderer.compile()` + `syncSectionSkyUniforms()` pairing v3.4.11 already established, but inside `refreshActiveSectionProfile()` itself rather than only at its callers -- this function is the one thing both the axis-change path (`applySectionMode()`) and the threshold-drag path (`setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()`) already call, so fixing it once here covers both call sites instead of needing the fix duplicated at each one.

**Live-verified the fix specifically, not just the symptom**: reproduced the exact broken state (forced `uSkyDiscardOn` to `0`), applied the same compile-then-resync the real fix does, and confirmed the value held after a delay -- the actual test that matters, since the bug was always a LATER silent reset, not an initial failure. Then restored the full normal state (Buildings + Negative space, all three quads, X=939) and zoomed into the result: a real per-building fill that hugs the actual geometry, no flat rectangle anywhere.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.16.

v3.4.15 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: "the building elevation is correct but the red cut line is not. is this cutting further in?? this is the problem." Live-verified with real numbers before answering: computed the buildings actually crossing Joe's exact reported cut (Chelsea, X=843m, W view) directly against their real geometry -- they topped out at 61.6m. The poché fill, however, went all the way to 395m -- Chelsea's tallest building anywhere in the district, nowhere near this cut. Not a bug in the sense of wrong math -- v3.4.12 deliberately made the fill's ceiling the whole site's cap height -- but exactly the design tradeoff flagged (and left unanswered) back in v3.4.12/13's own writeup. Confirmed the actual choice with Joe before building: the ceiling should reflect the buildings actually near the cut, not the whole district.

Added `localCeilingPlane`, a new persistent Z-clip plane shared by both poché quads (only one is ever visible at a time). Its `.constant` is kept in sync with the real local section profile's own maximum height inside `refreshActiveSectionProfile()` -- the exact same per-slab array (`buildSectionProfile()`) that already drives the skyline line and the roofline-discard texture, so "local" here means precisely what it already means everywhere else in this pipeline: buildings within the current slab, recomputed live every time the cut position changes (dragging a slider, not just entering the view). Falls back to `siteCapHeight` if a slab happens to have zero buildings crossing it, so an empty cut doesn't collapse the fill to nothing. `SITE_BOUND_PLANES`' own Z bound (the hard district-wide outer limit) is unchanged -- `localCeilingPlane` is an ADDITIONAL, tighter bound layered on top, added to both quads' `restrictPlanes` at construction alongside it.

Live-verified directly: computed the real local ceiling (65.6m, buildings + margin) from Joe's exact scenario and patched the equivalent plane into his running v3.4.14 session -- the fill now caps at a height that actually matches the visible buildings instead of towering over them. This particular fix needed a NEW clip plane added to the quads' construction-time array, which (unlike a plain `.constant` mutation) only takes effect on a fresh Compute -- confirmed the corrected geometry/behavior via the live patch, but the exact construction-time wiring itself will get its first true test on Joe's own reload + Compute.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.15.

v3.4.14 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: "what is the red line cutting? I should just see the elevation of the building" (Buildings-only mode), then "selecting negative space button shows a whole new group of building forms being cut. So what are the two different cut lines? This needs to be figured out." Exactly right that there were two -- traced precisely via `window.__NS` rather than guessing: compared the set of visible red `LineSegments` in the scene before and after clicking Negative space, and found the count went from one to three the moment it was toggled on.

**Root cause, fully confirmed**: two separate, uncoordinated formulas were both controlling `xCutLineMesh`/`yCutLineMesh` (the true per-building CSG cross-section outline) at once. `updateSkylineProfileLine()` (runs on every axis change) already tries to suppress this mesh in a true section view -- its own comment says so explicitly: "per-building cut-line loops: only meaningful/legible in octant/perspective mode now that section mode has its own clean silhouette instead." But that suppression is only a conditional AND against whatever `.visible` already was, not an authoritative set. `refreshViewToggles()` (runs any time the Negative space button is clicked) was independently RE-DERIVING `.visible` from `showNegative && sectionModeAxis === 'x'/'y'` -- true in exactly the case the other function was trying to suppress -- and runs LATER, so it silently overwrote the suppression every time Negative space was toggled on inside a section view. v3.4.11 had removed this same line's OTHER branch (`sectionModeAxis === null`, decluttering the plain default view) without also removing this second, conflicting one -- leaving the one remaining case be the exact one that was never supposed to fire, which is why it went from "occasionally shows in two places" to "always double-outlines" the moment Negative space is on in any section view.

**Also confirmed**: the skyline profile silhouette (v3.2.39, the newer, intended clean section-cut indicator) was being added to the scene by `updateSkylineProfileLine()` with NO `showNegative` check at all -- so it showed even in plain Buildings-only mode, which is exactly the red line Joe was asking about in his first screenshot ("I should just see the elevation").

Fixed both: `xCutLineMesh`/`yCutLineMesh` are now unconditionally `false` in `refreshViewToggles()` -- fully superseded (v3.4.11 already hid it from the default view, `updateSkylineProfileLine()` already intends to hide it from section views, so it has no remaining state where it's the designated thing to show). The skyline profile line's own visibility is now explicitly synced to `showNegative`, both at creation (`updateSkylineProfileLine()`) and on every toggle (`refreshViewToggles()`) -- the ONE intended red indicator, on only when Negative space is on and a section axis is active.

**Live-verified both halves directly**, not just reasoned: patched `xCutLineMesh.visible = false` into the running session on Joe's exact reported scenario (Chelsea, X cutaway 834m, W view) -- confirmed only the single clean skyline line remained, no double outline. Then toggled Negative space off and confirmed the skyline line itself also disappeared -- Buildings-only mode now shows zero red lines, plain elevation only, exactly matching Joe's stated expectation.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.14. Both changes are plain JS visibility logic (not a shader change), fully live-patchable and live-verified this round -- unlike v3.4.13's depth-slab fix, no data-only/pixel-uncertain caveat needed here.

v3.4.13 - 2026-08-25 - Two things Joe found after loading v3.4.12, both confirmed live and traced to real, related causes.

**"After creating the section box I am still seeing all these graphics to the right when zoomed extents."** Reproduced live: a stray brown handle cone and a green wedge (the X/Y/Height cutaway 3D drag handles) rendering well outside a drawn box's own cropped view, disconnected from anything, still positioned at their OLD threshold-based location -- box-draw is a self-contained interaction (drag a rectangle, pick a direction) with no use for the drag-the-3D-handle workflow it's meant to supersede in this case. v3.4.11 already hid these for the plain default (`sectionModeAxis === null`) view; this extends the same fix to the "a box is active" case within an actual section view (`!activeSectionBox` added to `handlesOn`'s condition). Verified live: hid the same cone/wedge meshes directly in Joe's running session, screenshot confirmed clean before writing the real fix.

**"There are two different building outlines that I am looking [at]. The area that I picked does not match this elevation."** Zoomed into a real section and found the answer directly: the red skyline/poché outline surveys every building across the drawn box's FULL DEPTH on the cut axis (deliberate, v3.3.0's slab) -- but the actual solid geometry (both the ghost and Buildings) was still bounded by only a single, ONE-SIDED clip plane at the box's own center. An ordinary orthographic view then projects every building on the kept side, at ANY depth, onto the same 2D image -- so the outline (wide survey) and the solid mass (thin slice, still theoretically showing the whole rest of the site behind it) were built from two genuinely different, uncoordinated subsets of the data. Confirmed with Joe this was the actual problem, not something else, before touching code: "solid buildings/negative-space fill [should] represent everything across the box's full depth (matching the outline), not just a thin slice at the center." Yes.

Fixed with `getCutAxisClipPlanes(axis)`: a single one-sided plane when no box is active (unchanged -- a genuine full elevation on that axis remains correct for the plain slider-driven case, by design), or a real TWO-sided depth slab bounded to the box's own extent on that axis when one is. Applied to both `negMat` (the ghost) and `buildingMat` (Buildings), combined with the existing plotted-axis width-crop planes (v3.4.10) rather than replacing them. `xCutLineMesh`/`yCutLineMesh` (the true CSG cross-section outline, a fundamentally different single-plane-by-design element) deliberately left untouched -- not the source of this particular mismatch.

Verification note, in the interest of not overclaiming: confirmed the clip-plane DATA is exactly correct (right values, right count, right combination with the width planes) directly in Joe's running session. Could NOT get a fully trustworthy screenshot-level visual confirmation this round -- hit the same "backgrounded tab can serve a stale compositor frame to the screenshot tool" issue this project has run into before (v3.2.37), and a second browser tab stopped responding entirely partway through this investigation (unrelated to the app itself -- a fresh tab worked immediately). The fix is reasoned-correct and data-verified, not pixel-verified, this time.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.13. Handle-hiding fix IS visually live-confirmed; depth-slab fix is data-verified but needs Joe's own reload to see it actually rendered for the first time.

v3.4.12 - 2026-08-25 - Joe drew directly over a screenshot: a solid red mass filling the whole frame, labeled "negative space which is the inverse of what we see in elevation section" -- "this was the whole point of the project." The actual founding concept, stated as clearly as it's ever been stated.

Root cause of the gap: v3.2.35 deliberately made the poché fill stop at each building's own LOCAL roofline, specifically so the void wouldn't read as "a filled slab" -- solving a real problem, but a DIFFERENT one than what Joe is asking for here. That fix was about the ghost SHELL reading wrong in ordinary walkthrough-style views; Joe's sketch is specifically about the POCHÉ FILL in a section view, and there it's the filled-slab look that's actually correct -- a true figure-ground inversion, buildings as cutouts, everything else uniformly filled up to one consistent ceiling, not a jagged band tracing every rooftop.

Fixed by splitting the roofline discard out into its own uniform (`uSkyRoofDiscardOn`) rather than reverting v3.2.35 outright, which would have broken what it fixed. Defaults to `1.0` (unchanged) for negMat's own ghost shell -- still reads as open sky in perspective/octant views, exactly as v3.2.35 intended. Set to `0.0` specifically for the poché quads (`makeCapQuad()`'s `applySkyConfig()`), so their fill extends to the quad's own real top edge instead of stopping at each local roofline -- confirmed that edge is already exactly the site's flat cap height, not infinite sky, via `SITE_BOUND_PLANES`' existing Z bound (`siteBoundMaxZ.constant = siteCapHeight`, v3.2.38) -- precisely the clean flat ceiling in Joe's sketch, not something new to build. The width crop (`uSkyCropOn`, v3.4.0) is unaffected either way, still independent.

Likely also explains the "Section fill... doesn't do much" finding from the sweep a turn earlier: a fill that only ever hugged each rooftop was subtle enough to be nearly invisible next to the skyline outline; a fill that spans the full ceiling height should be dramatic and obvious to toggle.

Honest limitation on verification: this is a shader change (`onBeforeCompile` only runs once, at a material's first real compile) -- unlike this session's other fixes, it genuinely can't be live-patched into an already-running page the way a plain JS/CSS change can. What WAS confirmed live: the quad's own Z-clip bound really does cap at `siteCapHeight` (not infinite sky), which is the geometric half of the claim. The shader logic itself is a small, narrowly-scoped change (one new uniform gating one existing line, default unchanged everywhere except the two poché quads) -- reasoned correct, syntax-checked, 91/91 on `audit_deploy.js`, but genuinely needs Joe's own reload to see rendered for the first time, not just reasoned about.

APP_VERSION bumped to 3.4.12. Held per Joe's standing rule pending his decision on whether to test this now or continue the sweep's still-open Buildings-crop thread (v3.4.11) first.

v3.4.11 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: "a lot of graphic artifacts, I don't think the tools are working as good as they use to... too many issues to pick apart, what do we do?" -- correct call. Agreed to a real regression sweep instead of another reactive one-off patch: methodically exercised the core workflows live in Joe's actual browser (default view, section-with-a-box, Buildings vs Negative space) rather than waiting for the next screenshot.

**Found and fixed with high confidence**: switching axis (default octant <-> an N/S/E/W section) changes negMat's clipping-plane COUNT (3 planes down to 1) -- a different plane count can require the shader to recompile, and per the exact mechanism v3.4.1 already documented, any recompile builds brand-new sky/crop uniforms at their 0/off defaults and repoints `mat.userData.skyUniforms` at them. v3.4.1's fix only ever covered the VISIBILITY-toggle trigger for a late compile; this is the SAME failure mode from a second, different trigger (a clipping-plane-count change on axis switch) that nothing had covered. Confirmed precisely live: after drawing a box and picking N, `sectionModeAxis` correctly read `'y'` and negMat's clipping planes were correct, but `uSkyDiscardOn`/`uSkyCropOn` still read `0` -- as if the uniform sync had silently never run, even though it's called unconditionally right after the clip-plane assignment. Fixed by forcing the pending recompile to finish synchronously (`renderer.compile(scene, activeCamera)`) BEFORE the uniform sync runs, inside `applySectionMode()` itself -- reusing the exact technique v3.2.35 already established for this exact class of bug (that call, in `computeNegativeSpace()`, covers a fresh compute; this covers every subsequent axis switch too, which nothing did before). Live-verified: forced the same compile-then-sync order directly in Joe's running session and confirmed the crop values held stable afterward (checked again after a delay, still correct) -- something that reliably failed before the fix.

**Also fixed**: the cutaway handles (3D drag cones) and cut-line outlines showing in the plain default (perspective/octant) view read as a serious rendering bug after the north-facing camera change (v3.4.6/7) put them front-and-center in frame instead of mostly out of it -- not actually broken geometry (a cone viewed near edge-on legitimately silhouettes as a flat wedge), but a real visual regression from the camera-angle change. These are precision-cutaway tooling, more relevant once actually inside a section view; `xCutLineMesh`/`yCutLineMesh` and the handle/guide-line visibility no longer show in the default octant/perspective/Plan view (`sectionModeAxis === null`) -- section-view visibility is unchanged. This one's logic is sound (same `sectionModeAxis` pattern proven correct elsewhere) but could NOT be re-verified visually live in this session -- the OLD, still-running code kept re-asserting its own visibility computation on every refresh, overriding any one-off patch attempt; needs Joe's reload to see it actually take effect.

v3.4.10 - 2026-08-25 - Joe's annotated diagram: "Draw a Section Box should only display what's within the bounding box... what's happening here is just zooming into the area." Confirmed against the screenshots: with "Buildings" active (not Negative space), drawing a box and picking N still rendered the full-width skyline of solid buildings, even though the red section-cut silhouette correctly narrowed to the box's width -- exactly matching his diagram's "clip/remove" annotations on either side of "this is the boundary."

Real gap, not a small bug: the box-crop (and the plain slider-driven cutaway before it) was only ever wired into `negMat`/the poché quads/cut-lines -- the section-mode pipeline never touched `buildingMat` (Buildings' own material) at all, so that view has never respected ANY cutaway, box-drawn or slider, since the feature's origin. Asked before building (a real scope question, not guessable safely): confirmed Joe wants (1) Buildings to crop to a drawn box the same way Negative space does, and (2) Buildings to ALSO crop with the plain slider-driven cutaway even with no box drawn, for full consistency between the two view modes.

Added `syncBuildingClipping(axis)`, called from `applySectionMode()` right alongside where `negMat`'s own clipping gets set. `buildingMat` itself is `const`-scoped inside `loadData()` (same shape as the `negMat` scoping gotcha from v3.2.31) -- but every building/part mesh shares that exact material object by reference (`extrudedMesh()` never clones it), so grabbing it off any one mesh already in `solidGroup` gives the correct shared instance without promoting anything to module scope. Default octant state (`axis === null`) mirrors `negMat`'s own `[sectionPlane, xClipPlane, yClipPlane]` / `clipIntersection: true`. Single-axis section state adds the box's plotted-axis width bounds as two ordinary `THREE.Plane` clipping planes when a box is active -- deliberately NOT reusing the sky-discard shader's `uSkyCropOn` mechanism (that shader also does per-building roofline sky-discard, which genuinely can't be a flat plane; a box's width crop is a plain half-space cut, and ordinary clipping planes already do that with zero new shader machinery, shared with `xClipPlane`/`yClipPlane`'s existing mechanism).

**Live-verified the primary, explicitly-reported case** by reproducing Joe's exact steps (TriBeCa, box drawn over a cluster, N view) and patching the equivalent logic directly into his running v3.4.9 session before writing anything to the file: Buildings now stops precisely at the drawn box's edges, matching the red section skyline exactly -- screenshotted before and after. The secondary case (plain octant-default cutaway, no box) uses the identical clipping mechanism and the same constants already proven correct for Negative space, but wasn't independently visually obvious in this session's specific test district (the affected corner happened to have few buildings there) -- reasoned-correct via shared code path, not separately spot-checked this round.
