v3.4.9 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: "when in a neighborhood and select plan its not zoomed to extents the view is zoomed in for Manhattan ON/OFF the neighborhoods most fit the screen." Reproduced live on his own running session (Chelsea/Clinton/Hudson Yards, not yet computed): clicking Plan showed only a small fragment of the district. Confirmed the real site's bounding box directly (`solidGroup`, ~1913x3658 units) against the orthographic camera's actual frustum span at that moment (~550-1017 units) -- roughly 4-7x too tight, not proportional to the real site at all.

Root cause: `currentTargetAndSpan()` (shared by every N/S/E/W/Plan view) has three branches -- Manhattan-context-on (spans the whole borough, by design, see v3.2.24), post-compute (`siteCapHeight` defined, uses the real precise site bounds), and a THIRD fallback for when NEITHER of those apply. That fallback was `{ target: controls.target.clone(), span: 500 }` -- a hardcoded generic span plus whatever `controls.target` happened to already be, a leftover from wherever the camera was last centered (often a PREVIOUS district entirely). This fallback made sense before `resetToDefaultView()` (v3.4.6) existed, when there was no other real geometry to fall back to before a Compute -- but `loadData()` has always built the real buildings (`solidGroup`) well before Compute ever runs, so there was real geometry available to fit to the whole time; nothing was ever wired up to use it for the orthographic views specifically, even after v3.4.6 fixed this exact class of bug for the perspective default view.

Fixed by giving this fallback the same treatment v3.4.6 gave `resetToDefaultView()`: when `solidGroup` exists (i.e., a district has loaded, whether or not it's been computed yet), fit to its real live bounding box instead of the hardcoded span/stale target. Verified live by computing the real corrected span/target directly from Chelsea's actual `solidGroup` box and re-running the orthographic camera's own framing math with it, right in Joe's running session (no file reload needed to test the math) -- confirmed the corrected view shows the full district edge-to-edge, matching the real geometry.

Manhattan-context-on framing (spans the whole borough while targeting the site's own center) is unchanged -- that's a deliberate, previously-explained design choice (v3.2.24: seeing the district IN CONTEXT of the wider island), not the bug being reported here. Flagging this distinction explicitly in case the ask also covers wanting the district itself sized larger/more prominent even with Manhattan context on -- that would be a different, deliberate change to make, not a bug fix, and hasn't been made here.

v3.4.8 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: "i have brought this same side panel issue up several times and its still not fixed for advanced mode." Screenshotted the panel's top row (Buildings/Negative space) hidden, only a sliver of "space" visible. This looked like the same symptom v3.4.4/3.4.5 already addressed, but it wasn't the same bug -- confirmed live rather than guessing again, since this exact complaint had already gone through two rounds of fixes that didn't stick.

Live-inspected `#controls`'s actual computed styles and bounding rect on Joe's real running session (not a static screenshot read): `max-height` WAS being applied correctly (v3.4.4's fix works), `scrollTop` was 0 (v3.4.5's reset works) -- but the panel's clamped top edge landed at `y=16`, and `header.top` (the "Negative Space" title bar) is ALSO `position:fixed`, 66px tall, with a HIGHER z-index (20) than the panel (15). The panel's top-margin math had only ever reserved a flat 16px for "space above the panel" -- it never accounted for the header's own 66px footprint. So the panel's now-correctly-clamped top edge was landing 50px INSIDE the header's own band, and the header's opaque background painted over that slice of the panel -- not pushed off the top of the browser window (v3.4.4's bug), not scrolled to the wrong position (v3.4.5's bug), but hidden BEHIND another fixed element entirely. Explains why it specifically recurred in Advanced mode: that's the one state tall enough to actually reach this ceiling.

Fixed by reserving the header's real height plus a clear gap in the max-height calc (102px total: ~70px header + 16px gap + 16px bottom margin, still folding in `env(safe-area-inset-top/bottom)` for notched devices) instead of the old flat 32px. Verified live by patching this exact CSS rule directly into Joe's running v3.4.7 session (a `<style>` override, no file reload needed to test a pure CSS change) and re-measuring: panel top now lands at y=86, header ends at y=66 -- a clean 20px gap, confirmed via both `getBoundingClientRect()` and a screenshot showing the full Buildings/Negative space row visible below the header.

v3.4.7 - 2026-08-25 - Joe screenshotted the compass needle right after loading v3.4.6: it did NOT point up. "This is not positioned in the direction of NORTH when opening the project — was this not clear? ... this goes for zoom extents [and orientation] with Manhattan context [too], and not [only without it]."

v3.4.6 fixed WHETHER the camera re-fit to the district's real extents on load/switch/compute, but deliberately left `DEFAULT_CAM_POS`'s actual azimuth untouched, flagging the orientation itself as a possible separate follow-up rather than guessing. This is that follow-up, now confirmed necessary: `DEFAULT_CAM_POS = (600, 600, 500)` positioned the camera NORTH-EAST of the target looking SOUTH-WEST -- true north was behind-and-to-the-side of the camera's own facing direction, not "ahead, into the screen," so `updateCompass()`'s needle (which points toward true north relative to where the camera is CURRENTLY LOOKING, not where it's positioned FROM) read off to the side instead of up. Verified the exact math before shipping, not just reasoning about it: computed `updateCompass()`'s real azimuth formula by hand for both the old and new position -- old gives -135° (needle rotates 135° off vertical, matching the screenshot exactly), new gives 0° (needle points straight up).

Since `BOROUGH_VIEW_DIR` (Manhattan context's own camera direction, v3.0.21) derives its horizontal azimuth directly from `DEFAULT_CAM_POS`/`DEFAULT_CAM_TARGET` at runtime (deliberately, so the two views don't spin the compass when toggling between them), correcting this ONE constant fixes both the plain default view AND Manhattan context together -- directly answering the "does this apply with Manhattan context too" half of the ask without needing a second, separate fix.

New `DEFAULT_CAM_POS = (0, -848.53, 500)` -- due south of the target, looking due north, same horizontal distance and elevation as the old value (just rotated in azimuth, not moved closer/farther or raised/lowered). This is genuinely due north, not a mixed direction -- it trades away the small diagonal/corner-view character the old NE-facing default had (previously you saw two building faces at a corner; now you see the site's south face closer to head-on). Flagging that trade explicitly rather than silently making it: if the corner-view look is preferred over an exact compass reading, a modest east or west skew (keeping the needle close to, but not exactly, vertical) is a one-line follow-up -- but exact alignment was the explicit, repeated ask here, so this ships pure due-north first.

v3.4.6 - 2026-08-25 - Joe: "when a view opens it should always be zoomed to extents and positioned North direction." Confirmed live on his own session before touching code: right after loading TriBeCa (a real, fully-built district), `camera.position` read back as the generic hardcoded `(600, 600, 500)` -- completely unrelated to TriBeCa's actual location or size. Traced it: `loadData()` (runs on first page load AND every neighborhood switch) never repositioned the camera at all -- its own comment even said so ("the camera position itself is left as-is, not re-framed"). `recenterCamera()` (the Home button) already had the correct fit-to-extents math, but it only ever ran when Joe clicked Home, and only used the precise post-compute site bounds (`siteMinX` etc, undefined until a Compute finishes) -- so a freshly loaded but not-yet-computed district had no real fit available even if something HAD called it.

Extracted `recenterCamera()`'s math into `fitCameraToBox(box)`, and wrapped the "reset to the app's one canonical view" behavior (perspective camera, free rotate, exit section mode, fit to whatever real geometry currently exists) into `resetToDefaultView()`. `fitCameraToBox()` takes an explicit `THREE.Box3` rather than reading `siteMinX`/etc directly, so `resetToDefaultView()` can fit `solidGroup`'s own live bounding box (available immediately after `loadData()`, no compute required) when `siteCapHeight` isn't defined yet, and the more precise post-compute bounds once it is. Wired in at both real "a view opens" moments: the end of `loadData()` (covers first load and every neighborhood switch), and the end of `computeNegativeSpace()` -- guarded on `sectionModeAxis === null` there specifically, since a recompute while ALREADY inside an N/S/E/W section view is deliberately supposed to stay in that section (existing behavior, `applySectionMode(sectionModeAxis)` a few lines above it) -- an unconditional reset would have silently kicked Joe out of whatever section he was mid-recompute in. `recenterCamera()` (Home button) now just calls the shared `resetToDefaultView()`, same behavior as before, no change from Joe's perspective there.

The "positioned North direction" half: the perspective default's own direction (`DEFAULT_CAM_POS` relative to `DEFAULT_CAM_TARGET`) is unchanged -- this fix is about ALWAYS returning to that one consistent, already-north-referenced default whenever a new view opens, rather than leaving the camera at whatever arbitrary angle/zoom (or even N/S/E/W/Plan/section mode) a previous district or session left it at. If the exact angle itself should be adjusted to a stricter due-north bias (it currently leans north-EAST, per the file's own existing convention comment above `DEFAULT_CAM_POS`), that's a one-line follow-up, not a structural change -- flagging it rather than guessing which Joe actually wants.

v3.4.5 - 2026-08-25 - Joe screenshotted the panel again right after loading v3.4.4: (1) opening the Neighborhood dropdown showed it cut off top and bottom, and (2) the panel opened with its own top row (Buildings/Negative space) already scrolled just out of view.

**Both traced back to v3.4.4's own fix.** (1) `#neighborhoodList` has always been `position:absolute; bottom:calc(100% + 6px)`, popping up above the button -- correct as long as its containing panel had no clipping of its own. v3.4.4 added `overflow-y:auto` to `#controls` specifically so the Advanced tab's extra height wouldn't push content off the top of the screen -- but `overflow` on an ancestor clips ANY descendant's painted content regardless of position scheme (absolute OR fixed), so the dropdown, meant to float freely above everything, got clipped to whatever sliver of `#controls`'s own scrollport happened to be visible instead. Fixed by reparenting `#neighborhoodList` to `document.body` in `initNeighborhoodPicker()` and computing its position directly from the button's `getBoundingClientRect()` each time it opens (plus on window resize while open) -- the same escape-the-clipping-container technique `positionPlanBoxPanel()` already uses for the box-draw direction picker. Verified against the actual extracted `initNeighborhoodPicker()` code in a jsdom harness (9 checks: reparenting, options still populate, open/close still work, outside-click-to-close still works after the move) rather than just reasoning about it, since DOM-reparenting-and-still-functioning is exactly the kind of thing that's easy to get subtly wrong.

(2) The scroll-to-top-of-panel issue is reasoned, not confirmed via live inspection (no way to attach a debugger to Joe's exact scroll state) -- most likely cause is browser scroll-anchoring shifting `#controls`'s own `scrollTop` when its content height changes underneath it (switching to Advanced, or Compute revealing the section panel, both add a large chunk of height). Added `overflow-anchor:none` to `#controls` to stop that heuristic from applying here at all, plus explicit `document.getElementById('controls').scrollTop = 0` right after both of those exact height-changing moments (`setUiMode()`, `computeNegativeSpace()`'s section-panel reveal) as a direct belt-and-suspenders fix regardless of the precise mechanism.

(1) "Compute negative space should not display the poché boundary — very confusing": `computeNegativeSpace()` has auto-enabled Negative space right after every compute since v3.0 ("since that's what you just asked for"), immediately showing the octant-cutaway ghost + red poché with zero explanation — v3.2.43 already tried softening this once with a caption, and it evidently still isn't read as ready-to-use. Changed the post-compute default to Negative space OFF, Buildings only — the toggle is still one click away, and Draw section box works exactly the same either way (it only needs a compute to have happened, not Negative space to already be on — see v3.4.2's `siteCapHeight` guard). Toast text updated to match ("turn on Negative space to see it, or Draw section box for a section").

(2) "Dialogue gets cut off": real bug, not a rendering glitch — `#controls` (the main left panel) is `position:fixed` and anchored by `bottom`, so it grows UPWARD as its own content gets taller, and had NO height limit at all. Once the Simple/Advanced tabs (v3.4.3) added the Advanced tab's real height on top of everything already in the panel, the panel's own TOP (Buildings/Negative space toggle, Neighborhood picker) pushed up past the top of the browser window entirely — not scrolled-away, literally clipped off-screen with no way to reach it, since `body` is `overflow:hidden` and the panel itself had no scroll of its own either. Joe's screenshot shows this exactly: mid-layout-break, header area disrupted, "Compute negative space" barely back in frame. Fixed with `max-height:calc(100vh - 32px - env(safe-area-inset-top) - env(safe-area-inset-bottom))` plus `overflow-y:auto` on `#controls` itself — the panel now scrolls internally once its content exceeds the viewport, keeping every control reachable regardless of window size or which tab is open, instead of silently losing controls off the top edge.

v3.4.3 - 2026-08-25 - Joe's ask: the old slider-driven cutaway controls should be split into Simple/Advanced tabs, "similar to the other projects" -- matching the underline-tab convention already established in Sound/Relief and Modulor Massing (Simple hides precision/tooling controls while keeping their current values live under the hood; Advanced reveals everything), rather than removing anything. Checked first whether the sliders were actually safe to delete outright, per Joe's literal ask to remove "whatever doesn't impact the new tools" -- they're not: the box-draw workflow (v3.4.0) sets X/Y Cutaway to the drawn box's center and then relies on those same sliders for fine-tuning afterward (dragging X/Y after drawing a box translates the slab center while keeping the drawn width, per `getSectionRanges()`), so deleting them would have broken the very feature meant to replace them. Tabs, not deletion, was the right shape.

Added `#uiModeTabs` (Simple / Advanced, underline-indicator style copied from the CSS pattern in Sound/Relief and Modulor Massing, adapted to this project's navy/brick-red palette) at the top of the section panel. Wrapped Height/X/Y Cutaway, Flip X/Y, the cutaway key-hint, "Show cutaway handles", and "Reset cutaway" in a new `#advancedCutawayControls` container, hidden by default (Simple). Left "Full width", "Section fill (poché)", and "High detail (slower)" outside that wrapper and visible in both tabs -- they're box-workflow feedback and rendering-quality toggles, not precision-dialing controls, so they don't belong in the same bucket as the sliders. Mode persists to `localStorage` (`ns_uiMode`), matching the same pattern as the existing theme toggle right above it in the file.

Switching tabs changes nothing about actual state -- `xThreshold`/`yThreshold`/`sectionModeAxis`/`activeSectionBox` all keep whatever value they're at whether or not their row is visible, since `setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()` never required their DOM row to be visible, only present. This is the same "hides controls, keeps sensible defaults" contract Sound/Relief's own v1.3 changelog entry describes for its own Simple/Advanced tabs.

One real bug caught while restructuring, not by live-testing but by re-reading the code after moving elements: `refreshCutawayRowActiveState()`'s "Cropped to the box..." status note was inserted via `sectionRow.insertBefore(note, sectionCutRow)` -- valid only when `sectionCutRow` is a DIRECT child of `sectionRow`. Nesting it one level deeper inside the new `#advancedCutawayControls` wrapper would have made that call throw (`insertBefore`'s reference node must be a direct child of the node it's called on) the very first time a section view was entered, silently breaking the whole cutaway panel setup that runs afterward in the same function. Re-anchored the insert on `#advancedCutawayControls` itself (still a direct child of `#sectionRow`) instead -- which also fixes a second, related issue: that note is core box-workflow feedback ("cropped to the box you drew..."), not a precision-slider detail, so it needs to stay visible in Simple mode too, not get hidden along with the rest of the Advanced-only block.

v3.4.2 - 2026-08-25 - Joe reported two things live, directly on his own session: (1) "selecting 'draw a section box' zooms all the way out and there is no control in the views framework", and (2) "why is there an existing red section box active?" Traced both by reproducing his exact click via `document.getElementById('drawBoxBtn').click()` and reading `window.__NS` state before/after, rather than assuming the earlier live-verify pass (which happened to always test from Plan view already) covered every entry path.

**Issue 1, real: `setBoxDrawMode(true)` disabled `controls.enabled` outright -- the entire OrbitControls object, not just the piece that could conflict.** Two things compound into "zoomed all the way out with no control": (a) auto-switching to Plan re-fits the camera to the WHOLE site -- confirmed via `orthoCamera.left/right/top/bottom`, this is byte-for-byte identical to what the plain "P" nav button has always done, not a new bug, but it's jarring if you'd zoomed into something specific first, since nothing preserves that zoom; (b) `controls.enabled = false` then made it impossible to zoom back in or pan at all while drawing. But disabling the WHOLE controls object was never actually necessary: every N/S/E/W/Plan view already sets `controls.enableRotate = false` (v3.2.34) specifically so a stray drag can't tilt a true elevation off-axis -- and with rotate off, a LEFT-button drag already does nothing in OrbitControls (rotate is what LEFT does by default), so it was never going to fight this feature's own pointerdown/move/up listeners. Pan (RIGHT-drag) and zoom (scroll) use different inputs and never conflicted with box-drawing at all. Fixed by removing the `controls.enabled` toggle entirely -- pan and zoom now work exactly as they do in every other orthographic view, including mid-draw, so the "zoomed all the way out" moment is immediately recoverable by scrolling back in. Also added a persistent status banner (with its own Cancel button) while drawing, replacing the old 3.2-second fading toast, which wasn't enough signal that anything was still interactive or even still in progress.

**Issue 2: not a bug.** The red-outlined "corner removed" view Joe saw is the default octant cutaway (Height=0 / X=0 / Y=0 planes, `clipIntersection: true`) that computeNegativeSpace() has shown immediately after every compute since v3.2.x -- entirely pre-existing, unrelated to the box-draw feature, and not new in v3.4.x. No code change; noted here so it's on the record that this was asked and answered, not silently ignored.

v3.4.1 - 2026-08-25 - Two real bugs caught live-verifying v3.4.0 against Joe's actual running session, both found by exercising the box-draw workflow in orders it hadn't been tested in yet.

**Bug 1: NaN camera crash if a box is drawn and a direction picked before ever running Compute.** `setOrthogonalView()`'s new box-framing override (v3.4.0) divided by `siteCapHeight` unconditionally to compute the camera target's Z -- but "Draw section box" was always clickable regardless of compute state (matching the existing Plan button's own pre-compute-safe behavior), and drawing one before a first Compute leaves `siteCapHeight` genuinely `undefined`. `undefined / 2` is `NaN`, which silently propagates through `target`/`span`/`camDist` into `orthoCamera.position` -- Three.js just renders nothing for a NaN camera position, no console error at all, so the whole viewport went blank with zero diagnostic signal. Confirmed live via `window.__NS`: `orthoCamera.position` read back as `{x:null,y:null,z:null}` (JSON.stringify's rendering of NaN). Fixed two ways: the override now also checks `siteCapHeight !== undefined` before engaging (falls back to `currentTargetAndSpan()`'s own existing pre-compute-safe branch otherwise, same as plain N/S/E/W always has), AND `setBoxDrawMode()` now stops the workflow at its actual entry point with a clear toast ("Compute negative space first, then draw a section box") rather than letting Joe draw a box that can't produce anything real either way -- `buildSectionProfile()` needs `buildBuildingBBoxCache()`, which only exists post-compute.

**Bug 2, more subtle: the crop silently doesn't render if you enter a section view before ever toggling "Negative space" on.** A `ShaderMaterial`'s `onBeforeCompile` only runs the FIRST time that material is actually rendered -- materials aren't compiled while every object using them is invisible -- and each time it does fire it builds a brand-new `shader.uniforms` object at hardcoded defaults (`uSkyDiscardOn`/`uSkyCropOn` both `0.0`), which `addSkyDiscard()` then points `mat.userData.skyUniforms` at. Reproduced live: entering an N view while "Negative space" was still OFF meant `negativeMesh` had never rendered, so `applySectionMode()`'s uniform-setting block silently no-op'd on its own `if (negMat.userData.skyUniforms)` guard (nothing existed yet to write into). Toggling "Negative space" ON afterward triggered the shader's first real compile -- fresh uniforms at their off/0 defaults -- and nothing had ever pushed the CURRENT section+crop state into them. Every visible symptom of a working section (side panel, caption, grayed rows, slider values) was completely correct; only the actual ghost shell/poché fill/crop silently never engaged. Confirmed via `window.__NS`: `sectionModeAxis` correctly read `'y'`, but `negativeMesh.material.userData.skyUniforms.uSkyDiscardOn.value` read `0`. Fixed by extracting the uniform-pushing part of `applySectionMode()` into its own `syncSectionSkyUniforms(axis)`, called both at the original axis-change site and from `refreshViewToggles()` right after `negativeMesh.visible` flips true while already in a section view -- so a late first compile gets the current state immediately, not just at the next axis change.

Both fixes are logic-level and don't touch `getSectionRanges()`/`buildSectionProfile()`/the shader crop-discard condition from v3.4.0 -- those were confirmed working correctly in the one order that WAS tested live (Compute → Negative space ON → draw box → pick direction), where the crop visibly narrowed the skyline silhouette to the drawn box's width.

v3.4.0 - 2026-08-25 - Joe's next ask, after v3.3.0's real position-dependent sections shipped: "would it be easier to show a neighborhood in plan and then draw a bounding box around the area I'd like to see in 2D section/elevation?" — a genuinely better way to set the same inputs the sliders already drive (which slab, how wide a crop), by drawing directly on the model instead of dialing meter values and hoping you landed on the right block.

Added a "Draw section box" workflow: click it (auto-switches to Plan view if you're not already there), drag a rectangle directly over the model, then pick N/S/E/W from a small popup. Screen-to-world unprojection reuses the same raycaster pattern the cutaway-handle drag already uses, intersecting against a z=0 ground plane — valid regardless of camera angle since Plan is a true top-down orthographic view (v3.1.2/v3.0.42). No new camera math needed.

The box feeds the *existing* v3.3.0 pipeline rather than replacing it: `getSectionRanges()` is now the single place that decides what slab (cut-axis depth) and crop (plotted-axis width) `buildSectionProfile()`, the sky-discard shader, and the skyline silhouette all use — it returns the box's own bounds when one's active, or the original fixed-±2m-slab/full-site-width defaults otherwise. `buildSectionProfile()`'s signature changed from a single `cutThreshold` to explicit `slabLo`/`slabHi` so it can express an arbitrary-width slab, not just a fixed one centered on a point.

Genuinely cropping the visible width (not just the slab depth) needed one real shader change: the sky-discard fragment shader previously *clamped* world position to the texture's UV range, which means anything past the profile's own domain kept sampling the nearest edge column forever — a poché/skyline that silently ignored any crop. Added a `uSkyCropOn` uniform that hard-discards outside `[uSkyLo, uSkyHi]` instead, on only when a box is active (off in the plain v3.3.0 case, where clamping to the site edge is exactly the desired behavior).

Camera framing (`setOrthogonalView()`) targets and spans the box itself when one's active, rather than the whole site — the point of drawing a box is to see just that block, not a full-width elevation with it buried in the middle. Sliders stay fully live afterward, per Joe's own framing ("box sets them, rather than replacing them outright"): dragging X/Y after drawing a box translates the slab's center while keeping its drawn width, since `getSectionRanges()` reads the *live* threshold for the slab center and only the box's own extent for its width. A "Full width" button (shown only when a box is actually active) clears it and reverts to the full-site default. `activeSectionBox` is reset on every neighborhood switch, same as every other site-bound variable there — stale box coordinates from a previous district's geometry would silently misbehave otherwise.

v3.3.0 - 2026-08-25 - Joe's real ask, after living with the section view for a session: "get real 2D views that are cutting the building forms" -- perspective shouldn't even be part of the conversation, N/S/E/W should be TRUE cuts through the building forms, not a fake. Confirmed the camera itself was already correct (true orthographic, `activeCamera === orthoCamera`, not perspective) -- the actual gap was upstream of the camera entirely.

Root cause, found by connecting to Joe's live session and moving Y from -0.25 to -800 in an N view (TriBeCa, well within the real data's range): the rendered skyline was pixel-identical before and after, even with `negativeMesh` hidden outright. The section view's skyline + poché fill (v3.2.35/39) were both built from `buildAxisHeightProfile()` -- a CITYWIDE envelope (tallest building anywhere along the FULL site depth, at each column), computed once at Compute time and never touched again. Correct for the original sky-discard purpose (the ghost's open-sky ceiling genuinely shouldn't move with a slider), wrong for the visible skyline/poché, which need to show what's actually AT the current cut depth -- an elevation silhouette, not a real section.

Added `buildSectionProfile()`: only buildings whose footprint bbox actually crosses a thin (±2m) slab around the CURRENT cut threshold contribute to the profile -- a genuine position-dependent architectural section, at the same bbox precision every other profile in this file already works at. Wired into `refreshActiveSectionProfile()`, called from `setXCutaway()`/`setYCutaway()` whenever the ACTIVE axis's threshold changes while in section mode (a no-op in perspective/octant/the perpendicular axis), and from `applySectionMode()` on entering a section view, so it shows correctly the instant you switch, not just after the first drag.

Perf: re-flattening/reprojecting raw GeoJSON rings on every drag tick (the original per-compute cost) would have lagged visibly during a live drag. Added `buildBuildingBBoxCache()` -- a flat `{xMin,xMax,yMin,yMax,h}` array built once per compute -- so `buildSectionProfile()` is a cheap numeric filter+bin pass instead. Verified in a Node harness against real data before shipping (couldn't live-test this one directly: Joe's browser can't reach this sandbox's own test server, a different constraint than the CSG work's original "verify in a Node harness" precedent, same practice): TriBeCa, moving the Y cut from one site edge to the other changes 226 of 512 profile bins (genuine depth-dependence, confirmed against real building bboxes) -- and on the largest district (UES, 5,893 buildings), `buildSectionProfile()` runs in 0.043ms/call, no visible perf risk even at every 'input' tick of a drag.

Also addressed the corollary: Height Cut and the PERPENDICULAR cutaway axis have always been architecturally inert in a locked section view by design (v3.2.30-33's whole point was a true elevation showing the FULL building width and FULL building height, not a corner cutaway) -- but they stayed fully live-looking, which is exactly what read as "none of the tools work." Added `refreshCutawayRowActiveState()`: grays out the two inert rows and adds a one-line note ("Full elevation shown — only X/Y cutaway moves the cut here") whenever a section view is active; the genuinely-active row (now real, per the fix above) stays fully interactive. All three rows return to normal the moment you're back in perspective/octant/Plan.

v3.2.42 - 2026-08-25 - Live-verified v3.2.35/39/40/41 against Joe's actual running localhost session (previously all shipped but unconfirmed) -- and found a genuine gap in v3.2.41's own fix while doing it. Octant-view poché suppression (v3.2.40) reproduced exactly on Joe's reported Chelsea/X-103/Y-1743 settings: `capFillGroups.x/y.visible` both `false`, no band. N/S section skyline + open-sky-above-roofline (v3.2.35/39) confirmed clean on a live compute. Root cause: `loadData()` has always force-reset `computeBtn.disabled = false` as its very first UI-touching line, well before its own `await fetch(...)` -- predating v3.2.41 entirely. `switchNeighborhood()` disables `computeBtn` (among others) immediately before `await loadData(id)`, but that line inside `loadData()` runs synchronously on entry and silently undid it, reopening the exact race v3.2.41 was meant to close, scoped to just this one button -- worse than the original symptom, since a Compute click landing in that window runs against the PREVIOUS district's still-live `buildingsGeo`/`partsGeo` with no error, no stale-label tell, just a silently wrong result for the district shown.

Confirmed the line was genuinely redundant before removing it: `computeNegativeSpace()` already owns `computeBtn.disabled` fully itself, on both its error-return path and its normal-completion path, and `switchNeighborhood()`'s own `finally` re-enables every guarded control (including `computeBtn`) once `loadData()` truly finishes. Removed the stray reset in `loadData()`; nothing else relied on it, including the initial `boot()` call (the button already starts enabled in markup).

v3.2.41 - 2026-08-24 - Fixed the real root cause behind two things Joe kept intermittently seeing: the "white cap" sky-discard slab reappearing (v3.2.35/39's fix silently not engaging) and a neighborhood dropdown label stuck on the previous district's name. I'd previously logged the dropdown issue as an open item, then wrongly retracted it as my own testing mistake after a retest happened not to hit the race window -- both were the same real bug, caught for certain this time by reproducing it live on a genuinely fresh tab: switch districts, then immediately fire view/compute clicks without waiting for the district switch's `await loadData(id)` to actually finish. `currentNeighborhoodId` (and this button's own label) hadn't updated yet when the second click's logic ran, and `applySectionMode()`'s trailing sky-discard setup could end up running against a `negativeMesh` from an in-between state, leaving `uSkyDiscardOn` stuck at 0 despite `sectionModeAxis` being correctly 'x'/'y'. Confirmed directly: waiting for `switchNeighborhood()` to actually finish before doing anything else, both symptoms disappeared completely, every time; racing it, both reappeared together, every time.

`switchNeighborhood()` already disabled `neighborhoodBtn` itself for the duration of the switch -- but nothing else. Fixed by disabling every control that can trigger a compute or a view/camera change for that same window: `computeBtn`, `viewSolid`, `viewNegative`, and all `.navBtn` (N/S/W/E/Home) elements, re-enabled in the same `finally` block that already restores `neighborhoodBtn`. A disabled `<button>` doesn't fire click events at all (standard DOM behavior, verified live rather than assumed), so this closes the whole race rather than patching either individual symptom.

v3.2.40 - 2026-08-24 - Fixed the octant/perspective cutaway view reading as "confusing beyond belief" (Joe's exact words, with a screenshot annotated "cut / poche??!!??"). My first guess (drop the translucent ghost shell in that view) was wrong -- caught before shipping by actually isolating each mesh live rather than continuing to guess: hiding `negativeMesh` made zero visible difference to the dominant diagonal maroon band in the screenshot; hiding BOTH poché quads together made it disappear completely, leaving a genuinely clean view.

Real cause: a poché quad's stencil test correctly restricts color to only where real solid crosses the plane, but the quad's own geometry still spans the full site extent. Seen from a sufficiently oblique/grazing angle -- exactly what free-orbit in octant mode allows, and what the axis-locked N/S/E/W section views never allow (camera can't orbit away from facing the plane head-on, v3.2.34) -- even a correctly-masked flat plane visually reads as one continuous wall, because scattered stencil-passing pixels project onto adjacent screen pixels from that angle. Compounded by octant mode already showing BOTH the X and Y quads simultaneously. Not a bug in the stencil itself (why v3.2.13/v3.2.25/v3.2.38's fixes never caught it) -- specifically a flat-plane-at-grazing-angle problem, only possible where the camera can freely orbit.

Fixed by not showing poché fill in octant/perspective mode at all (`refreshCapFillVisibility()`: `capFillGroups.x/y.visible` now requires `sectionModeAxis === 'x'`/`'y'` exactly, dropping the `|| sectionModeAxis === null` that showed both quads there). The existing cut-line outlines (1D lines, not 2D planes, so unaffected by the grazing-angle problem) remain as the "here's the cut" indicator in octant mode on their own. Poché fill in the true N/S/E/W section views is completely unchanged -- single plane, camera always faces it head-on, already confirmed clean earlier this session.

Live-verified directly against Joe's exact reported settings (Chelsea, X cutaway -103m, Y cutaway -1743m) before writing the fix to source: reproduced the dominant diagonal band exactly, isolated it to the poché quads via visibility toggling, confirmed the new logic produces the same clean result, and confirmed N view's poché fill is untouched.

v3.2.39 - 2026-08-24 - Replaced the busy per-building cut-line outlines with a single clean stepped skyline silhouette in section views. Joe drew a mockup of what he wanted: one continuous profile line, not a separate closed-loop box around every building. What xCutLineMesh/yCutLineMesh (v2.5.0) draw is the TRUE CSG cross-section trace of negativeMesh at the cut plane -- accurate, but negativeMesh has one separate cavity per building gap, so the trace is genuinely many small closed loops, not one line. Not fixable by tweaking the trace itself -- a real architectural section silhouette is a simpler, different thing: just the tallest LOCAL roofline as a function of position, which is exactly the same per-axis height profile already built for the sky-discard fix (v3.2.35). No new geometry computation needed, just a new way to draw the same data as a stepped line (`buildSkylineProfileLine()`) instead of sampling it in a shader.

`buildAxisHeightProfile()` now returns both the GPU texture (still used for sky-discard) and the raw Float32Array (`negHeightProfileX`/`negHeightProfileY`), so the same per-compute data drives both features. `updateSkylineProfileLine()`, called from `applySectionMode()`, builds a `THREE.LineSegments` from the active axis's profile array -- one horizontal segment per bin plus a vertical riser wherever the height changes between adjacent bins -- positioned at the cut plane using `xThreshold`/`yThreshold` (the flip-independent source of truth for "where the cut sits", not `xClipPlane.constant`/`yClipPlane.constant`, which flip sign depending on flip state and would have needed re-deriving the visible half-space direction, a mistake already made and caught once this session on `SITE_BOUND_PLANES`). The old per-building loops (xCutLineMesh/yCutLineMesh) now only show in octant/perspective mode, where they still make sense; section mode uses the new silhouette instead.

Live-prototyped directly against Joe's running session before writing any source: built the profile array from `solidGroup`'s live bounding boxes (same technique as the original sky-discard prototype), rendered it as a `LineSegments`, confirmed visually it produces exactly the clean stepped-profile look from Joe's sketch, tightly tracking the real rooftops -- caught and fixed one own mistake mid-prototype (first attempt used a stale profile built before a district switch had actually completed, producing a visibly misaligned line against the wrong district's buildings; rebuilding from the current live `solidGroup` fixed it, and this is the same "verify the actual current state, don't assume a click completed" lesson from the neighborhood-dropdown mixup found later this session).

## Open items found this session, not yet fixed
- **Neighborhood dropdown button label can go stale.** Found live: selecting a district from the list correctly updates the actual computed data (confirmed via building count and the header subtitle, which both update correctly) and correctly sets the `active` class on the right `.opt` element, but `#neighborhoodBtn`'s own displayed text can lag behind and keep showing the PREVIOUS district's name. Cosmetic only -- the underlying selection and compute are correct -- but worth fixing so the label doesn't visibly disagree with what's actually loaded. Not yet investigated further (where the button's textContent is supposed to sync from the active `.opt`'s click handler).
- **`uSkyDiscardOn` (the v3.2.35 sky-discard toggle) can read 0 while `sectionModeAxis` is correctly 'x'/'y'.** Caught live once this session: a section view showed the old full-depth ghosted slab despite being genuinely in section mode; direct inspection showed the shader uniform itself was 0 (off). Clicking the same N/S/E/W button again reliably fixed it (re-running `applySectionMode()` sets it back to 1). Tested changing Height Cut, X/Y cutaway, and the poché toggle individually afterward as possible triggers -- none of them reproduce the drop to 0 in isolation, so the actual trigger is still unknown. Workaround: re-click the section view button if the ghosted slab reappears.

v3.2.38 - 2026-08-24 - Fixed two large triangular red-tinted wedges floating in the sky, converging to a point, that Joe screenshotted in a low oblique octant-cutaway view (not a section view -- `sectionModeAxis` was null). Live-diagnosed step by step directly against Joe's running session: first hypothesized `SITE_BOUND_PLANES` was stale from a prior district, but comparing its 4 planes to `negativeMesh`'s own live bounding box showed they matched the CURRENT district exactly -- a real finding, but a dead end (caught a sign-convention error in my own reasoning about which side of a clipping plane counts as "visible" before concluding this wrongly).

Real cause, confirmed by reading the y-cutaway poché quad's live world bounding box directly: z ranged from -3586 to +4003, against the real model's actual 0-417 range. `SITE_BOUND_PLANES` (v3.2.25) only ever had X/Y-normal planes -- there was never a Z bound. A cap quad's own `restrictPlanes` only adds `sectionPlaneNeg`, which bounds Height Cut from ONE side only (keeps z >= Height Cut, by design -- that's what makes the cutaway carve correctly). Nothing ever bounded the top, so the quad's full 3x-oversized (v3.2.25) raw extent rendered completely unclipped above the building height -- invisible from every previously-tested angle, foreshortened into a converging wedge from this screenshot's low viewing angle. Same root pattern as v3.2.25's original "red box artifact," just on the one axis `SITE_BOUND_PLANES` never covered.

Fixed by adding `siteBoundMinZ`/`siteBoundMaxZ` to the same shared `SITE_BOUND_PLANES` array (now 6 planes: X min/max, Y min/max, Z min/max) -- every quad already spreads `...SITE_BOUND_PLANES`, so no other call site needed touching. `.constant` set alongside the existing X/Y ones in `computeNegativeSpace()`, using `siteCapHeight` (Z min is always exactly 0, the mold's own base, so it's hardcoded rather than tracked as a separate variable).

Verified precisely with a geometric clip test rather than pixel-guessing on-screen (an earlier live attempt at pixel-sampling missed the wedge's actual screen location and gave a false negative): tested a world point at z=2000 (inside the old unbounded wedge zone) against the quad's real `clippingPlanes` array before and after the fix using each plane's own `distanceToPoint()` -- visible before (true), correctly hidden after (false) -- while a legitimate point within the real model's actual height stayed visible in both cases, confirming the fix removes exactly the wedge and nothing else.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.2.38.

v3.2.37 - 2026-08-20 - Fixed the oversized "MANHATTAN" text label from Joe's screenshot. Live-diagnosed by first ruling out that it was any kind of DOM/HTML element or browser overlay -- searched the whole page for any oversized text element (nothing found, largest font-size in the DOM was ordinary UI text) -- then searched the THREE.js scene graph directly and found a `Sprite` with `scale: {x: 2600, y: 650}`, ~29x larger than every street-name label sprite (`{x: 90, y: 22.5}`). Traced to `makeBoroughLabel()`: `worldWidth` (2600 for "MANHATTAN", 2000 for other boroughs) is a fixed absolute world-space size, tuned to look right specifically at `zoomToBoroughContext()`'s intended viewing distance (`BOROUGH_MAX_DISTANCE`, 18000 units) -- correct, ordinary perspective behavior for a genuinely ~2.6km-wide object at that distance, but Manhattan context can stay toggled on while the user zooms in far closer for normal district-level work, and nothing capped the label's apparent size as that happens.

Reproduced directly: positioned the camera 1500 units from the label sprite (matching Joe's screenshot) -- exact same oversized "MANHATTAN" filling most of the frame. Fixed by tracking distance-to-camera each frame (`updateBoroughLabelScales()`, called from `animate()`) and capping the label's effective size at whatever it would be at `BOROUGH_MAX_DISTANCE` -- unchanged real perspective scaling at/beyond that distance, but shrinking further (never growing past its designed size) as the camera gets closer. Only applies to the perspective camera -- orthographic N/S/E/W/Plan views weren't reported as having this problem and are left untouched.

One real verification wrinkle worth recording: the automated browser tab for this session wasn't focused/visible, and its own render loop was throttled (same issue as v3.2.36) -- but this time it went further: even a manually-forced `renderer.render()` call didn't change what subsequent screenshots showed, because the SCREENSHOT TOOL was serving a stale/cached compositor frame for the backgrounded tab, not the actual current canvas content. Caught this by reading pixels directly via `gl.readPixels()` at the text's screen position instead of trusting a screenshot -- confirmed the pixel changed from white (text) to the dark background color after the fix, proving the fix works even though the screenshot tool showed no visible change. Don't trust an unchanged screenshot alone as proof a live JS patch had no effect in a backgrounded automated tab -- verify via direct canvas/pixel readback when a screenshot seems suspiciously identical to a prior one.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.2.37.

v3.2.36 - 2026-08-20 - Fixed flickering Joe reported right after v3.2.35 shipped. Couldn't directly capture the flicker itself this round -- the automated browser tab wasn't focused/visible, so its own render loop was throttled (a requestAnimationFrame-based multi-frame pixel sample came back with zero frames captured after several seconds; a follow-up readPixels attempt came back all zeros too, likely because the renderer isn't configured with `preserveDrawingBuffer` so the drawing buffer's contents aren't guaranteed to survive past the same task). Static screenshots and zoomed crops looked clean, consistent with this being a genuinely temporal/motion artifact rather than something visible in any single frame.

Reasoned the fix from the code instead, with real precedent to back it: `negMat` is `THREE.DoubleSide`, drawing both front and back faces of the same geometry every frame, with NO `polygonOffset` set at all -- exactly the class of problem v3.2.13 already had to fix for the poché quad (coincident/near-coincident surfaces fighting for the same depth-buffer pixel). v3.2.35's sky-discard removed the flat cap that used to occlude most of the interior cavity geometry -- a lot more of that thin/near-coincident interior structure (adjacent buildings' party walls, cavity walls close together) is now actually being rasterized where it wasn't before, so this exact z-fighting class is far more likely to be visible now than when v3.2.13 first fixed it for the quad alone. Applied the same fix (`polygonOffset: true, polygonOffsetFactor: 1, polygonOffsetUnits: 1`) to `negMat` too. Live-tested the change for visual regressions only (static screenshot before/after looked identical, no new artifacts) -- the flicker fix itself is NOT yet live-confirmed and needs Joe's reload to verify.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.2.36.

v3.2.35 - 2026-08-20 - Fixed the "not a true orthogonal view" complaint for real this time -- Joe correctly rejected v3.2.34's diagnosis-in-progress and pushed for a fix rather than more back-and-forth. Root cause, confirmed step by step directly against his running session: the gray/red slab filling the sky above the skyline in N/S/E/W section views was NOT a camera or projection bug (the ortho camera genuinely is axis-locked as of v3.2.34) -- it's real geometry. The void directly above every SHORTER building, all the way up to the district's tallest building (the mold's cap height), was rendering as a solid, uniform fill across its whole depth. Accurate to the model, but not what a real architectural section shows, where anything above a building's own roofline just reads as open sky.

Live-diagnosed by elimination before writing any fix: hid `negativeMesh` alone -- band only lightened slightly (ghost + poché were both contributing, not one or the other). Tested a `depthWrite:true` hypothesis (transparent-material depth stacking making multiple translucent layers along one view ray look artificially more opaque) -- zero visible change, ruling that out. Hid both `negativeMesh` and the active axis's poché quad together -- band vanished completely, confirming it really is the full accumulated void volume, not a rendering/blending artifact.

Fix: a per-axis 1D "local roofline height" profile (`buildAxisHeightProfile()`), built once per compute directly from the real `buildingsGeo` features (reusing the same `flattenRings`/`project()` calls already used for the site bbox) into a small `DataTexture`. `addSkyDiscard()` injects a shared onBeforeCompile (world-position varying + a texture-sampled discard test) into `negMat` and both x/y poché quad materials. In section mode only, any fragment more than `NEG_SECTION_SKY_MARGIN` (4m) above the LOCAL tallest real building at that screen position gets discarded -- a shorter building's own airspace still poché's correctly right up to ITS roof, but the district-wide void above OTHER, taller buildings elsewhere no longer paints a flat ceiling across the whole frame. Perspective, octant cutaway, Plan, and STL export are completely untouched (the discard only activates when `sectionModeAxis` is 'x' or 'y', toggled live in `applySectionMode()` -- axis switches mutate shader uniform VALUES, no material swap needed).

One real timing bug caught before shipping, not after: `onBeforeCompile` only fires on a material's first actual render, so `applySectionMode()`'s trailing call at the end of `computeNegativeSpace()` (re-applying section mode after a recompute that happened while a section view was already active) would have run against not-yet-compiled materials, silently no-op'd the sky-discard toggle, and only picked it up on the next user interaction. Fixed by calling `renderer.compile(scene, activeCamera)` immediately before that trailing `applySectionMode()` call, forcing the compile (and `userData.skyUniforms` population) synchronously.

Live-verified directly in Joe's session before shipping: N view -- zoomed screenshot showed a clean skyline against real open sky, no filled band, just a subtle ~4m dark fringe directly above each roofline. E view spot-checked too, same result. Prototyped entirely via live material swaps in the browser console first (three times -- a naive `negMat`-only patch, then discovering the poché quad was the dominant layer, then the full height-profile version) before writing any of it into `index.html`, per this project's standing rule of live-confirming the concept before shipping.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. APP_VERSION bumped to 3.2.35.

v3.2.34 - 2026-08-20 - Fixed N/S/E/W/Plan not staying true orthographic elevations. Joe reported a section view that wasn't axis-aligned; his own screenshot's camera rotation (checked via the debug hook) confirmed it -- forward vector had a real, non-trivial Y-component in what should've been a pure-X E/W view. Root cause: `setOrthogonalView()` computes and sets a genuinely axis-aligned camera position/orientation each time (confirmed correct at the moment of the click), but never touches `controls.enableRotate`, which defaults to (and stayed) `true` in every ortho view -- OrbitControls remained fully free-rotate even in N/S/E/W/Plan. Live-reproduced directly: entered E view (forward `(0.9998, 0, -0.02)`, correctly axis-aligned), performed one ordinary drag on the viewport, and the camera silently tilted to `(0.9724, -0.2323, -0.02)` -- ~13 degrees off-axis -- with nothing in the UI indicating the view was no longer a true elevation. That's the earlier "gray band above the skyline" investigation's real root cause, not a rendering bug in the gray band itself (that part -- the void volume between shorter roofs and the site cap height -- is real, correctly-computed ghosted-context geometry, still an open item on whether to depth-limit it, but was a red herring for this specific report).

Fixed by setting `controls.enableRotate = false` at the end of `setOrthogonalView()` (covers N/S/E/W/Plan alike) and restoring `controls.enableRotate = true` in `recenterCamera()` (Home). Pan and zoom remain fully enabled in ortho views -- only rotation is locked, so N/S/E/W/Plan now stay genuinely fixed to their axis for the whole time you're in that view, not just at the instant you click the button.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. Not yet live-reconfirmed against the patched file (Joe's local dev server serves from his own directory, not this container -- needs his reload to verify the lock holds under a real drag).

v3.2.33 - 2026-08-19 - Fixed another real section-mode artifact from Joe's follow-up screenshots: two disconnected red rectangles with a visible gap between them in an N/S/E/W section view, instead of one continuous section. Confirmed live before touching code: `sectionModeAxis: "y"` (N view) but `capFillGroups.x.visible: true` -- the INACTIVE X quad was still fully visible, still rendering its own genuine poché wherever real solid crossed ITS OWN threshold (which happened to sit at an extreme site-edge value, `-1127`, catching a real chunk of buildings near that edge). v3.2.30/32 correctly re-bounded the ACTIVE axis's quad into a true full section, but never addressed the INACTIVE axis's quad at all -- it kept its old octant `restrictPlanes` and, critically, was never told to stop being visible. The X/Y quads were never wrong here; simply not hiding the irrelevant one was the bug.

Fixed in `refreshCapFillVisibility()`/`refreshViewToggles()` (not a new code path -- these already own all cap-fill and cut-line visibility): both now check `sectionModeAxis`, showing only the axis matching the current N/S/E/W direction while a section view is active (Plan and perspective, `sectionModeAxis === null`, keep both as before). `applySectionMode()` now calls `refreshViewToggles()` itself at the end, so the visibility change actually takes effect on every transition into or out of section mode.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. Not yet live-reconfirmed (needs Joe's reload) -- the diagnosis (mismatched quad visibility vs. sectionModeAxis) was confirmed directly via the debug hook before writing the fix, same standard as always.

v3.2.32 - 2026-08-19 - Fixed real graphical artifacts in v3.2.30's section mode that Joe screenshotted and marked up: a stray gray band floating above the skyline ("what is this gray bar?"), and a red poché band along the ground that didn't move at all as Height Cut changed ("this base should move as the cut line move -- we should see nothing below the cut line"). Live-confirmed section mode itself was working first (E view, negMat.clippingPlanes correctly down to 1 plane, visible poché boundary with ghosted context on one side) before Joe's screenshot caught these two follow-on artifacts.

Root cause: v3.2.30 dropped `sectionPlaneNeg` from the active axis's poché quad AND cut-line mesh `restrictPlanes`, on the mistaken belief it carried the same "Height Cut defaults to 0" destructive-default risk that `negMat`'s shell clip genuinely has. It doesn't -- that risk is specific to `negMat`'s `clipIntersection:false` UNION semantics (a fragment is removed if it fails EITHER plane), where unioning Height in at its Z=0 default would clip the whole building away. A quad's `restrictPlanes` has no such union interaction; `sectionPlaneNeg` there is an ordinary AND-ed bound on the quad's own geometry, identical to how every other axis's poché quad has always used it. Without it, the active quad's full 3x-oversized raw height (v3.2.25) rendered completely unbounded -- both above the real roofline (the gray band) and below ground (the ever-present red band, ignoring Height Cut entirely).

Fixed by restoring `sectionPlaneNeg` to the active axis's quad and cut-line `restrictPlanes` (still dropping only the PERPENDICULAR axis's threshold plane, which remains the correct, intentional part of the section-mode relaxation). Poché height now tracks Height Cut again, matching every other axis and matching what Joe expected. Also added `sectionPlaneNeg`/`xClipPlaneNeg`/`yClipPlaneNeg`/`SITE_BOUND_PLANES`/`sectionModeAxis` to the `window.__NS` debug hook -- these weren't exposed before, which is part of why this specific class of bug wasn't caught by live-checking before the first ship; the shell-level clipping (`negMat.clippingPlanes.length`) was checkable, but the quad/cut-line-level bug wasn't visible through the hook at all.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. Not yet live-reconfirmed (needs Joe's reload).

v3.2.31 - 2026-08-19 - Fixed a real regression from v3.2.30, my own bug: Joe reported "control handles are missing" -- the actual failure was much bigger than the handles specifically, it was the entire cutaway panel (`#sectionRow`) never appearing after a compute. Found via `read_console_messages` (not guesswork): `applySectionMode()` referenced `negMat` directly, but `negMat` is a `const` declared LOCAL to `computeNegativeSpace()` -- a separate top-level function calling it threw `ReferenceError: negMat is not defined` on every single call, and since `applySectionMode(sectionModeAxis)` runs near the END of `computeNegativeSpace()` (added there in v3.2.30 specifically to re-apply section mode after a recompute), that error aborted the function mid-execution, silently skipping everything after it -- including the line that sets `#sectionRow`'s display to visible. This is the exact "uncaught error silently aborts compute(), whatever ran first still shows, everything after the throw never runs" failure mode this project has hit before (v3.0.37's near-identical case, documented in AUDIT.md) -- worth remembering as a recurring risk whenever adding a new call inside `computeNegativeSpace()`, not just this one instance.

Fixed by using `negativeMesh.material` instead of `negMat` inside `applySectionMode()` -- the exact same object (`negativeMesh = new THREE.Mesh(cleanedGeo, negMat)`), but `negativeMesh` is module-level and genuinely reachable from outside `computeNegativeSpace()`, unlike `negMat` itself. Audited every other variable `applySectionMode()` references (`capFillGroups`, `xCutLineMesh`/`yCutLineMesh`, `sectionPlaneNeg`/`xClipPlaneNeg`/`yClipPlaneNeg`, `SITE_BOUND_PLANES`) and confirmed each is genuinely top-level, not a repeat of the same mistake elsewhere in the same function.

`audit_deploy.js`: 91/91. Not yet live-reconfirmed (needs Joe's reload) -- the root cause and fix are unambiguous (a real thrown ReferenceError caught directly in the console, not inferred), but per this project's own standing rule, nothing ships as "confirmed" without actually seeing it work in Joe's real session.

v3.2.30 - 2026-08-19 - "Real, live section" for the N/S/E/W elevation views, per Joe's ask ("I need the elevation to look like an actual section cut, currently graphically it's a bit messy"). Clarified scope with him first rather than guessing: ghosted context behind the cut (not hidden entirely), reusing the existing Height/X/Y cutaway sliders rather than adding a 4th dedicated control. Previously N/S/E/W were true flat orthographic ELEVATIONS -- architecturally a different drawing type from a SECTION, which cuts a slice and removes everything nearer than the cut plane. The octant cutaway system's existing stencil-based poché (`createPlaneStencilGroup`) already tests each axis's cut against the true, unclipped solid geometry independent of the other two axes -- the octant "only shows in one corner" look comes entirely from `restrictPlanes`, a purely cosmetic mask on each quad's material, not from the stencil test itself. So a true full-width section needed no new geometry or stencil pass, only the active axis's clipping loosened: `negMat` (the translucent negative-space shell, whose existing 0.15 opacity already reads as "ghost") gets just the ONE plane matching the current N/S/E/W direction instead of the normal 3-plane octant intersection, and that axis's poché quad AND cut-line mesh both drop their octant-corner restriction, bounded only by the real site footprint (v3.2.25's `SITE_BOUND_PLANES`) -- both were already computed against the full real geometry, exactly the same fix shape applied twice.

Caught a real bug before shipping, not after: an earlier version of this unioned Height Cut into the section-mode clip too (reasoning: "reuse existing sliders" could mean all three staying active together) -- but Height Cut's default/reset value is 0, and `sectionPlane` only keeps z<=constant, so that would have clipped away the entire building above ground on every fresh section view, defeating the feature by default. Height Cut and the perpendicular (non-view-direction) axis are deliberately excluded from the section-mode clip -- the section is defined by exactly the one axis matching the current elevation, nothing else, so it always starts from a clean full section rather than an accidentally-empty one.

`sectionModeAxis` ('x' | 'y' | null) tracks which axis is active, set in `setOrthogonalView()` (y for N/S, x for E/W, null for Plan) and cleared in `recenterCamera()` (Home always exits section mode back to normal octant behavior). Re-applied after every recompute (`buildCapFillGroups()`/`buildCutLineMeshes()` rebuild fresh quads/cut-lines with default restrictPlanes each time, which would silently drop section mode on a recompute otherwise).
